## 0. Conclusion (first)

> ★**Superseded (2026-09-26 simplification · user decision → ADR-0235) — the two rules below have been displaced. The body is left as the edition 9 record and is not corrected.**★ ① The ✕ rule (this section ③ 「✕ 를 누르면 … 곧바로 빠진다 · 어느 창도 취소 대기를 그리지 않는다」 · §5-2 · §5-6) → ADR-0235 decision 7: a sent row has no ✕, a row whose ✕ was pressed leaves only when the backend answers that it removed it, and a row for which the answer came back that it could not be removed is drawn like a sent row (both backends). ② The rule by which the codex halt after error is released 「사용자의 다음 턴이 성공해야」 (this section ⑦ · §5-5 · §5-6 `stopped_after_error`) → ADR-0235 decision 4: a failed end sets the halt and it is released the moment the next user message is taken in. The rest of the codex hand-over design is written in the head marker of §5-5.

★**Edition 9 — the implementation has landed (P0–P8 · P8 = `37ecc5e`).** The conclusion below stands as is, but three changes are large: ① the session id of codex JSON is persisted when the peer first echoes a user message back (user decision — §5-8 · §10-1 24) ② the codex follow-up turn debt stands only for messages carried by `turn/steer` (§5-5) ③ M15 · M16 green → the operating default hand-over policy = `AtEarliestBoundary` (it holds the tool · answer segments until the boundary — §9). The remaining deviations = §10-6★

1. **Storage is done by the daemon's agent session layer (the agent crate)** — not the WS·net layer, not the frontend·shell (§4-1 · PRD §3-3). The state of the waiting list flows as **one stream of core ring events**, and the daemon registry · the frontend accumulator · the LLM commands converge on **the same reduction rule** (§5-2·§5-3).
2. **Delivery = 「에이전트가 받을 수 있는 가장 이른 때」** (user principle — PRD §3-1). The window in which the boundary can be hit differs by vendor (M7), so the answer splits (§4-2).
   - **claude = stdin as soon as received** (as today). If we held a message and handed it over after seeing the tool complete, we would miss that boundary (M7 0/10) — it has to be written right away so that the vendor folds it in at **the end of the tool that is running now** (§5-4).
   - **codex = holds until the earliest boundary.** 「지금 넘기나 · 쥐나」 is answered per segment by the swappable hand-over policy `HandOverPolicy` inside the codex backend (§5-5 segment table): in the tool segment it holds and hands over at once on the signal that all running tools have ended (M7 10/10 — window 6–64 ms) · in the segment where the answer is written without tools, the same if M16 is green, and at once if red · in segments with no ground to hold — right after a boundary, or while an item not confirmed as a tool is running — it hands over at once (when ambiguous, at once: handing over early is harmless) · and it holds in states where handing over is impossible (connecting · waiting for the turn id · end-of-turn settlement · halt after error · a turn where steer was rejected) (both policies). If a turn is in progress, `turn/steer`; if not, `turn/start`. The session · manager does not know the policy — it sees only `TransportOwned` (ADR-0004).
   - **The codex landing is two steps** (§9): `Immediate` (hand over at once if it can be handed over — the skeleton · ✕ and `withdraw` stand here · it does not look worse than today) → measurement (M15 our daemon's reaction delay · M16 the window at the end of the answer — §3-3) → `AtEarliestBoundary` (holding in the tool segment — and in the answer segment if M16 is green). ★If M15 is red, stop before designing the holding policy and ask the user again (PRD R8)★ ★Edition 9 — M15 green → P8 landed · this stop condition did not arise (§9 P7)★ — what drops out then is only the holding of the tool·answer segments (a message typed in those segments is handed over at once and ✕ removes it from the list only — §9). The codex recommendation of edition 1 (hand over at the **start** of the tool · S5) is rejected (§4-2).
3. **✕ is always on every item of the list — both backends · in any segment** (user decision 2026-09-25 — PRD §3-6). ✕ is 「목록에서 뺀다」, not 「에이전트에게서 거둬들인다」 — user: 「x를 건드릴 이유가 있어? 그냥 x누르면 목록에서 기계적으로 빼는거잖아. 구지 비활성 필요 있냐고」. It comes in as one registered command (and the LLM too, for any item of the list — §5-6), and only the inside differs per backend: claude = a list item is always before receipt, so `control_request` `cancel_async_message` removes it from the vendor queue (§5-4) · codex = path verb `withdraw` — an item that is held is erased (no vendor call), and an item already handed over is removed from the list only (there is no API to remove the one `turn/steer` that was handed over — §3-2). If that message is received late, it stands as a bubble at the end of the conversation (outcome (b) — user: 「상관없어」 · §5-5).
   - ★**When ✕ is pressed, that item is removed from every window at once**(user decision — PRD §3-6)★ — it is not a hiding in the window that pressed it but a request that changes the state of the daemon registry, and no window draws a cancel-pending item. Only the LLM list sees 「취소 중」 through to the outcome (§5-1 · §5-2 · §5-6).
   - ★The outcome is decided by the vendor lifecycle — the one exception is the cancel response `cancelled:true`★ — the `cancelled:false` of the claude cancel response is not 「전달됨」 (§3-1). There are exactly three outcomes: cancelled · end-of-conversation bubble · discarded. ★`false`·an error response is not an outcome and is used **only for attributing the cause of `cancelled`**★ — if a failed turn closes the waiting items with `cancelled`, that is not a user cancel but a discard (§5-2). `true` is the vendor's answer that it removed that message from its own queue, so it is cancelled at once without waiting for the lifecycle line (§5-2). A ✕ pressed on a handed-over codex item is decided by the same rule (§5-5).
4. **A withdrawn message or one that could not be sent is discarded — it does not return to the input box, and there is no notification** (user confirmation, night of 2026-09-25 · PRD §3-7). If a message sent while idle is drawn as a bubble and then rejected by the backend, the bubble is erased without a notification (PRD §3-5 — §5-2 `Dropped{Rejected}`) — the same when it is rejected after passing through the list and becoming a bubble (§5-2 「받음 뒤 거절」). There is also no header line above the list — only the gray items are drawn (PRD §3-2 · §5-7).
5. **The Phase 0 measurements ([report](../../research/mid-turn-phase0-measurements-2026-09-25.md) — claude 2.1.280 · codex 0.156.1) established the premises of both backends** — the claude lifecycle · cancel run in our `-p` stream-json mode (M1·M3) · codex steer · `clientId` echo-back · −32600 hold (M6). Three statements of edition 1 were corrected: a reservation cancel does not take effect mid-turn (§3-1 ③) · there is an asynchronous wait inside the claude fold (§3-1 row 1) · in codex there is a window between tool completion and the check of the waiting message (§3-2).
   - ★**One measurement overturned the ground of one user decision, and the user set a new principle**★ — in codex, 「도구가 끝난 걸 보고 넘기기」 (S1) **entered the same boundary 10/10** (window 6–64 ms). The ground on which the user rejected S1 (「한 걸음 늦다」) held only for claude (0/10). The user set 「받을 수 있는 가장 이른 때 전달」 as the principle and decided to hold codex until the earliest boundary (2026-09-25 — 2 above · §4-2). What remains as a premise is measurement — our daemon's reaction delay (M15) and the window at the end of an answer without tools (M16).
6. **The remaining vendor risk = codex's 「들어갔는데 답을 못 받은 글」** (PRD §3-1 · AC6 · R4) — ★Phase 0b closed it as green ([report](../../research/mid-turn-phase0b-measurements-2026-09-26.md) — M9 · M10)★. The echo of an accepted steer comes before `turn/completed` (M10 · vendor source), so the end-of-turn settlement **collects only responses** — the end-of-turn probe was withdrawn (§5-5 settlement 2). If the echo came but that turn gave no answer, then only for the `completed` turn **one** follow-up turn debt is set per turn (★edition 9 — only for messages carried by `turn/steer`★), and it is released the moment the Idle whose settlement is closed **issues** a user turn or an empty input `turn/start` (M9 — the model answers that message) — before mail, and a mail turn does not release the debt (§5-5 「답 없는 항목」). ★The settlement closes within one deadline measured from the end of the turn, and in a closed Idle there is no item waiting for a response★. ★**An item whose acceptance became unknown (`turn/start` response deadline · decode failure · long turn id · settlement deadline · a success response with no echo) stays in the list**(user decision N10)★ — it is not sent again, and there is no event and no debt. There are three ways out: a late receipt (a bubble — it does not open a turn to answer: the next user turn sees it in the history) · ✕ · agent end. Meanwhile user messages typed after it are handed over in order as usual (user decision N13 (b) — if it is received late, only that bubble may stand after the later messages) and only mail waits (N8 — it is visible in the list). A ✕ pressed on an unknown item (or an item that became unknown after ✕) closes at once as discarded and does not pass through 「취소 중」 — if it is received late, it comes back to life as a bubble (user decision N12 (a) · §5-5 「✕ 한 항목의 수락 모름」).
7. **User input comes before mail · after an oddly ended turn we halt.**
   - **Mail's idle = the same as user input — no turn is running and the user list is empty** (PRD §3-9 · user decision N6). The messaging kernel's busy decision (it adds 「목록이 비지 않았다」 · 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 to the turn-observation fact — both backends) and the codex path's Idle order (user item → follow-up turn for a user message that got no answer → mail) use the same definition (§5-0 · §5-5). Mail may be delayed behind user input — the user accepted that. Mail in terminal (PTY) mode is as today.
   - **Halt after error** (PRD §3-9 · §3-10 · user decision Q8 · N11 (b) widening): if a turn ends oddly (codex = `turn/completed(failed)` · the five exits of `turn/start` — regardless of turn kind · claude = error `result`), **nothing is put in on its own** until the user's next turn ends in success — neither an empty turn nor mail (both backends), nor the codex items still held (from the skeleton · together with the first user message that arrives after that, **in one turn, the remaining ones first** — AC24). An interrupt neither sets nor releases it (both backends — the only thing that releases it is a clean successful end) · it is not released when the list empties through ✕ · nor as time passes · no upper bound (that mail waits for no reason is an accepted cost — a mail notification is backlog). Where it lives: mail = the kernel's busy decision reads `last_end_failed` in the turn observation table (both backends · all versions — each turn classifier sets it at the end of that turn, §5-0) · turns codex opens by itself (empty turn · remaining items) = the path field `halted` (§5-5) · claude list items translate the vendor lifecycle as it is (§5-4). Codex below the floor has no `halted` (the path runs today's behavior together with this whole feature — N1 (a)) — mail halts there too. A message without an answer that has already become a bubble in that turn is left as it is (both backends — no debt is set).
8. **Pulling in by hook (S4) is dropped** (user rejection — 「훅마다 매번 뭔가 실행하면 안 된다」). **Scope = structured chat mode only** — terminal (PTY) slots do not change by a single byte (§6). The turn-interrupt operation is the very next feature after this one (PRD §2 — §1-2).
9. **The shape of the input path (§5-0)** — the write API takes the source (human input / mail) and mail does not go into the list on either backend · the backend reports its mid-turn input policy and whether receipt is possible, so the session does not branch on the backend name · claude's input and cancel are lined up by one session input lock so that **cancel cannot overtake a message**, and for codex the order of hand-over and ✕ is fixed under one path state lock (§5-5). The codex end-of-turn disposition waits for the steer response and tells by generation so that **hand-over does not go out twice** (§5-5). Because of the new WS command, `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is raised to 6 (user approval N4 — §8). The cannot-receive decision (`AckUnavailable`) and the `Queued` that stood on the ring later than it are matched **by the core inside the same lock** (the same shape as the seal — §5-3) · claude `Delivered` (vendor `started`) is written in the turn table as in progress (it closes the gap of the drain turn — §5-4). Because the threads that emit list events also exist outside the pump (session input · codex announce), a live subscriber may receive them in reversed seq order, and today's client **drops** a frame that arrives late — that drop would make the list permanently wrong. **The client keeps seq continuity — both in the live phase and at the buffer→live boundary** (§5-7 — the core lock discipline is unchanged). The daemon sink and the shell relay do not skip seq (an event the sink could not carry = placeholder · a frame with a tag the shell does not know = the same placeholder · a short frame whose seq the shell cannot read = cut the connection → replay everything), so there is no deadline for waiting on a hole. The core invariant is not changed (user confirmation N5).
10. **Open user questions (§10-4) — none.** N11 · N12 · N13 and the ✕ tooltip that edition 7 raised received answers on 2026-09-26 and the body stands on those answers — N11 (b) the halt after error widened into a principle (7 above) · N12 (a) ✕ on an unknown item = close at once as discarded (6 above) · N13 (b) messages after an unknown item are handed over in order as usual (6 above) · tooltip 「목록에서 빼기」 (§5-7). User direction 「복잡한것보다 약간 양보하면서 단순한 방향으로 하되 나중에 문제되면 기능 추가하면 되지」 — the end-of-turn probe · the debt of a late echo · the hidden record after ✕ were passed on as candidates to add later (§10-5). PRD §9's Q1–Q9 and N1·N2·N4–N10 that this document had raised received answers (2026-09-25) and the body stands on those answers (collected in §10-1). N7 · N9 are answers that leave today's behavior as it is — N7 (a) a message sent while claude is loading a resumed conversation becomes a bubble at once (there is no loading signal · PRD §3-11 · the claude part of AC28 is an exception — §5-4) · N9 a bubble drawn while idle that meets an interrupt or agent end before being processed is left (§5-2). N8 is the answer that even if a user item with no receipt coming blocks mail, no upper bound is set (§5-0). N10 is the answer that an item handed over in codex whose acceptance became unknown is kept in the list until a reason (receipt · ✕ · agent end) comes (§5-5 「수락 모름」). Measurements — M9 · M10 · M13 green (Phase 0b — §3-3) · M15 · M16 green (→ P8 landed — §3-3 · §9 P7). What remains is M11 · M12 · M14 · M17 (vendor behavior checks — they do not block · if the result changes the user-visible feel, it is raised to the user again).

---

## 1. Problem and scope

### 1-1. Today's behavior

- **claude (JSON)** — input goes to stdin at once (`session.rs:230`), and **right after** sending, a synthetic echo in the shape of a regular user bubble is put on the ring (`session.rs:241` → `backend/claude/mod.rs:442`·`:620`). Since the CLI puts mid-turn input into its own command queue and folds it in at the next tool boundary, **the bubble is fixed at the place where it was sent, and the rest of the earlier answer continues below it** (the accumulator's 「uuid 첫 항목이 이긴다」 — `structuredAccumulator.ts:56`). The progress the CLI reports (`command_lifecycle`) is a type the decoder does not know, so it is dropped (`backend/claude/mod.rs` `consume_line` end `_ => {}`). No cancel means.
- **codex (JSON)** — input during a turn is held in the path queue (`backend/codex/transport.rs:368` `State.input`) and goes out as a new `turn/start` when the turn ends (`take_turn_locked` `:1158` — only in Idle). It does not carry `clientUserMessageId` (`protocol.rs:537`), and there is no synthetic echo (ADR-0198 decided · not implemented). So mid-turn input does not reach the model **until the turn ends** and does not show on screen either. No cancel means.

### 1-2. Scope (user decision — PRD §2)

- **In:** the **user input** of the structured chat slot (`RichSlot`) (WS `WriteStdin`) — claude stream-json · codex app-server. Both backends include ✕ on a list item (it removes it from the list — and withdraws the message too if before receipt: claude always · codex while we are holding it, §5-5).
- **Old versions with no vendor premise are not given separate support** (PRD §2 · R6 · user decision N1 (a) 「알아서 업데이트」) — it is exactly today's behavior. How to tell them apart = §4-3.
- ★**Terminal (PTY) mode is out**★ — there the agent's own TUI lines up mid-turn input. `transport/pty.rs` · `transport/stdio.rs` · the terminal encoder (`InputEncoder::Raw`) · `TerminalSlot` are **not touched**. The proof is §6.
- **Mail (messages between agents) does not go into the list** (PRD §2). Mail passes two gates: ① the **messaging kernel's busy decision** parks the letter if the receiver is busy (turn observation = ADR-0127 · 30-minute fail-open — messaging kernel ADR-0110) ② after reaching the path it rides the **json input queue** (ADR-0190 — 「보낼 수 있을 때 흘린다」) and its decision rule (ADR-0193 — codex opens a turn only in Idle). This feature changes two things.
  - **User input narrows the 「보낼 수 있을 때까지 붙든다」 of ② in codex** — it holds only until **the earliest boundary**, not until the end of the turn (hand-over policy — §5-5 · ADR-0190 amendment stamp, §8). claude already writes at once today, so its delivery timing is unchanged.
  - **Mail stands behind user input** (PRD §3-9 · user decision N6) — mail's idle = no turn is running and **the user list is empty**. The busy decision of ① reads that fact in addition, and the codex path opens a user turn first at Idle if there is a user item (§5-0 「우편의 한가」 · §5-5 「우편」). The rest is today's path as it is — mail does not steer (codex = `turn/start` at Idle after the end of the turn), and claude writes at once the moment the kernel lets it go. Codex mail also carries ADR-0198's `clientUserMessageId` (the bubble shows at the time of consumption — it does not go into the list). Mail in terminal (PTY) mode is the same as today (the list is always empty).
  - The implementation splits by the source of the session write API (`InputOrigin::{User, Mail}`) — today mail and human input join into the same session verb and there is no source (§5-0).
- **The turn-interrupt operation is out — it is the very next feature after this one** (PRD §2 · §7 · user decision Q5). Today's chat view has no turn-interrupt operation (there is a connection-level wire `Interrupt` but no screen or command calls it · the claude JSON path does not support interrupt at all — `transport/stdio.rs:371`). So this document's 「끊기」 disposition reaches only **over the wire** today (tests and measurements are also done over the wire — §7-2). Whether to discard or send the waiting items as the next turn when interrupting is decided by that feature (only the premise is written in §5-4 「끊기」).

---

## 2. User decisions — the source of truth is PRD §3

The source of truth for 「무엇」 is [PRD](./prd-mid-turn-input-queue.md) §3 (edition 6 — edition 5 (the user Q&A answers after `/review prd` three times) plus the four answers of §10-4 in edition 7 of this document · edition 6 is before review), and this document does not restate it. Below is only the correspondence **rule → implementation spot**. The acceptance criteria (PRD §5) → test correspondence is §7-3.

| PRD §3 | Rule (summary) | Implementation spot |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Hand over at the earliest moment it can be received — claude as soon as received, to stdin · codex holds while holding is not too late and hands over at once at the boundary (in a segment where holding makes it late, at once) · items that could not get into the running turn go into the next turn in the order typed — if several, together in one next turn, each separately · unless discarded, all of them get into a turn that answers · an item that got in but received no answer (codex) also gets an answer — the same message is neither handed over nor drawn twice · exception = a turn that ended oddly (§3-10 — including the follow-up turn) · a codex item received late after the end of a turn (concession — no turn to answer is opened on its own) | §4-2 · §4-3 · §5-4 (claude — draining at the end of a turn is the vendor's · M11) · §5-5 (codex — `HandOverPolicy` segment table · end-of-turn settlement · items with no answer · several left = head `turn/start` + the rest by steer) · measurements = M15 · M16 (§3-3) · late receipt after the end of a turn = no debt (§5-5 「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」) · if the turn that used the debt ends oddly = halt after error (§5-5 — user decision N11 (b)) |
| 2 | Until received, a list above the input box — one gray line · if it overflows, 「…」 + the full text in a tooltip · older ones on top · over 3, 「외 N개」 (press to expand · while collapsed the three oldest) · **no header line** · nothing is drawn in the conversation | §5-7 (`QueuedInputList` · accumulator waiting-uuid suppression) |
| 3 | There is one list — the same in every window · the LLM · and across an app restart · an item that was ✕-ed leaves every window at once | §4-1 · §5-3 (registry) · §5-6 (commands) · §5-7 (re-attach reconciliation · cancel-pending not drawn) |
| 4 | An item leaves one by one only when 「받음」 (the vendor's per-item notification) arrives · writing it in · a receipt acknowledgement is not a receipt · if it never arrives, it is discarded when the agent ends | §5-2 (`Delivered` = claude `started` · codex `clientId` echo) · §5-3 (`finish` synthesis) · ★a codex item that was handed over but whose acceptance became unknown = it stays in the list until one of late receipt · ✕ · agent end comes (user decision N10 — §5-5 「수락 모름」)★ |
| 5 | Idle = no turn running **+ list empty** (not in the middle of resuming) — if the list remains, a new message goes to the end of the list · if a bubble drawn while idle is rejected, it is erased without a notification | §5-1 (classification) · §5-2 (`Dropped{Rejected}`) · §5-4 · §5-5 (FIFO — the no-overtaking ban is among `Held` · a message after an acceptance-unknown item is handed over in order as usual: user decision N13 (b) · Direct synthetic echo) · §5-7 (erasing the bubble) |
| 6 | ✕ is always on every item of the list — pressing it removes it from the list (before receipt the message is withdrawn too: claude always · codex while we hold it · an already handed-over codex item becomes a bubble if received late) · a registered command (the LLM is the same) · Tab + tooltip 「목록에서 빼기」 (all items · same wording on both backends) · no Esc · pressing it removes it from every window at once · the outcome is one of three · 「취소 중」 only in the LLM list (an acceptance-unknown codex item does not go through it) | §5-0 (input lock — cancel after the message · codex `withdraw`) · §5-1 · §5-2 (`CancelRequested` · reduction · `cancelled` cause attribution · cancel-pending not drawn) · §5-4 (claude cancel) · §5-5 (codex ✕ = `withdraw` three results) · §5-6 · §5-7 |
| 7 | A withdrawn message or one that could not be sent (interrupt · agent end · rejection) is discarded · **no notification** · a window's connection loss is not an interrupt · no automatic resend | §5-2 (terminal `Discarded`) · §5-5 (interrupt disposition) · §5-7 (re-attach) |
| 8 | Messages remain separate | §5-4 (a uuid per line) · §5-5 (one steer at a time — several are not loaded into one `turn/start`) |
| 9 | User input comes before mail — mail's idle = no turn running ∧ user list empty · mail may be delayed · after an oddly ended turn, mail is not put in on its own (both backends · even if the list is empty) | §5-0 (「우편의 한가」 — kernel busy decision · doorbell · mail's halt = `last_end_failed` — both backends) · §5-5 (「우편」 · Idle order — the follow-up turn for a user message that got no answer also before mail · halt after error) |
| 10 | After an oddly ended turn (error · request-stage failure), nothing is put in on its own until the user's next turn ends in success — empty turn · mail (both backends) · codex's remaining items (in one turn with the next user message, the remaining ones first) · claude list items as the vendor does · a message with no answer that already became a bubble is left as it is · for old codex the list and automatic turns are today's behavior (mail halts) | §5-0 (「우편의 한가」 — `last_end_failed` (two classifiers) · kernel `BusyPolicy`) · §5-4 (claude — translation as it is · M12) · §5-5 (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 `halted` — trigger = `failed` · five exits · release = `completed` · a `failed` turn has no debt) |
| 11 | A message sent while resuming stands in the list — codex holds it and hands it over when ready · if only the history restore fails, it hands over as it is · claude keeps it in the list and hands it over at once · if the resume itself fails, it is discarded | §5-5 (connecting · `Connecting` gate) · §5-3 (`finish` synthesis) · §5-4 (claude — ★there is no loading signal, so today's behavior — an exception, user decision N7 (a)★) |
| Scope | JSON mode only · mail is outside the list · the turn-interrupt operation is the next feature | §1-2 · §5-0 (source `User`/`Mail` · policy `None`) · §6 |

- **If you meet edition 1's D numbers** (documents from before PRD edition 3 cite them): D1 → PRD §3-1 · D2 → §3-2 · D3 → §3-4 · D4 → §3-5 · D5 → §3-1 「턴 끝 경계」 · ★D6 → §3-7 — the meaning was reversed (return to the input box → discard)★ · D7 → §3-6 · D8 → §3-8.

## 3. Facts — what the two backends give

The evidence has two layers — **source reading** (claude 2.1.280 binary · codex 0.154.0 source) and **measurement** (Phase 0 — claude 2.1.280 · codex **0.156.1**, §3-3). Rows that stand on measurement are raised to 「확실(M#)」, and places where a measurement overturned the source reading are marked with ★. `Phase 0 §N` = a section of the [measurement report](../../research/mid-turn-phase0-measurements-2026-09-25.md).

### 3-1. claude CLI 2.1.280 (stream-json input)

| Fact | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A `user` line on stdin mid-turn goes into the command queue, and if a turn is in progress it is folded in **after all the tool batch has ended and the `PostToolBatch` hook has run, right before the next model request** (`registerFoldInFlight` → `queued_command` attachment). ★**There is an asynchronous wait inside the fold — the 「I/O 양보 없음(빈 async 함수)」 of edition 1 was wrong**★: it first takes a snapshot of the waiting commands (`getCommandsByMaxPriority`), waits for each command's 「prompt.submit at the fold」 asynchronous processing (`ah` → `Ed`), and then folds in **only what is still in the queue by then** (`Oi` filter = `getCommandQueue()` check). That wait was ≈0.55–0.66 s in measurement — the cause is unconfirmed (presumed to be the installed plugin's `UserPromptSubmit` hook — hook events are not printed in the stream. In an environment with no hook it may be shorter). Two results: ① the snapshot is **before** that wait, so 「도구 완료를 보고 넘기기」 still misses that boundary (S1 claude **0/10** — the CLI read our line only 12–31 ms after the tool result) ② the cancel window before the fold is **wider** than the edition 1 assumption (a cancel sent 540 ms after the tool result also took effect) | Fold spot = likely (source reading) · snapshot→wait→filter order = likely (source reading + M3·M7 fit together) · cause of the wait length = uncertain | binary @209592877 · @209469129 · wait @209442306 · filter @209441195 · Phase 0 §4·§8 |
| Progress comes out as `{"type":"command_lifecycle","command_uuid":<우리 uuid>,"state":…}` **in our `-p` stream-json mode** — `queued` (queue entry) → `started` (drained as a turn) → one terminal (`completed`·`cancelled`·`discarded`·`refused`). The matching key is `command_uuid` (the `uuid` of the line itself is newly minted by the CLI). The `completed` of an input that was folded in is right **before** `result` (3 ms), and the `completed` of the input that opened the turn is right **after**. **It also comes out for input sent while idle** (`queued`→`started` within 1 ms). It does not exist for a command with no uuid. The schema description carries a `@internal` marker — the risk that this is a surface the vendor may change without notice (PRD R1) stays as it was even after measurement | Emission · order = certain (M1 — 32 uuids repeated) · surface grade = likely (description) | @197058310 · deliverer @217451681 · Phase 0 §2 |
| The meaning of `cancelled` is wide — host cancel · `cancel_queued` interrupt · things that had been **folded** into an interrupted turn or a fatally failed turn (「cancelled-over-completed」 — closed with `cancelled` after `started`). The description states 「`cancelled` 에 무턱대고 재전송하지 말고 **자기 취소 응답과 대조**하라」. The `cancelled` after `started` can be read as a signal that the input **went into a failed or interrupted turn** — this feature does not use it: that item has already become a bubble at `started`, and a message with no answer that became a bubble in a turn that ended in error is left as it is (PRD §3-10 · user decision Q8 — §5-4). ★**The `cancelled` of an item for which we sent a cancel (`Cancelling`) also cannot be assumed to be our cancel**★ — if a failed turn closing the waiting items with `cancelled` happens **before** our cancel line is read, our cancel afterwards meets a uuid that is not in the queue and answers `cancelled:false` (reservation). If our cancel removed it, `cancelled:true` comes **in the same ms** **after** the `cancelled` lifecycle (M3 ⓐ). So the response value tells apart the **cause** of that `cancelled` — exactly the check the description requires (§5-2 · whether a failed turn actually closes the waiting items with `cancelled` is M12) | Likely (description + M3 order · failure branch not observed) | @197058310 |
| **`control_request` `{subtype:"cancel_async_message", message_uuid}`** is **accepted in our mode without `initialize`** (「cancel_async_message is not supported in this context」 — the wording of a handler on the remote-control bridge side — never came). Response envelope = `{"type":"control_response","response":{"subtype":"success","request_id":"cancel:<uuid>","response":{"cancelled":true}}}` — ★the value is nested in `response.response.cancelled`★. If the cancel takes effect, the lifecycle `cancelled` comes out **before** the response (same ms), and that message does not reach the model. Original text: 「Drops a pending async user message from the command queue by uuid. No-op if already dequeued for execution.」 **It is on the control protocol side, so it has a different grade from the lifecycle** — it has no `@internal` marker and is also exposed as the SDK method `cancelAsyncMessage` (not confirmed in public documentation) | Certain (M3 ⓐ — six times) | @197145604 · print handler @217554898 · bridge wording @104558554 · Phase 0 §4 |
| That handler's decision: ① if folding is in progress (`isFoldInFlight`), it cannot remove (`cancelled:false`) ② if in the queue, it removes and emits the `cancelled` lifecycle ③ if the uuid has not come in yet, it sets 「취소 예약」 (`markCancelPending`) and answers `cancelled:false`. ★**The reservation of ③ does not take effect in the mid-turn fold — the edition 1 statement (「나중에 들어오는 순간 `cancelled` 로 닫힌다」) was wrong**★: the callers of `consumeCancelPending` that look at the reservation marker are **only the between-turns drainer (right before a new turn starts) and the batch settlement**, and the fold path does not look at the marker. Measurement: with the cancel first and the message written 0.7 s later, after `false` that message was folded and **delivered** (`started`). The 「caught by a pending cancel just before dispatch」 in the description is about draining a new turn. **We block the effect with our own ordering** — the CLI **loads stdin lines synchronously** in order (even if message + cancel are sent in the same write, `queued` → `cancelled`), so as long as the cancel line is written **after** the message line, this branch is not taken. ★What guarantees that order is the session input lock (§5-0)★: because `Queued` is visible **before** the message is written, another window or the LLM can press cancel in between, and without the lock the cancel line could overtake the message line. ★**Even so, `cancelled:false` is not evidence of 「이미 전달됐다」**★ (on the boundary, `started` after `false`) — the outcome is decided by the lifecycle (`started` / `cancelled`), and the one exception is the response `cancelled:true` (the vendor's answer that it removed that message from its own queue, so cancelled at once — §5-2 · §5-4). This rule gave the right answer in every branch of the measurement. Side effect: a late cancel leaves a reservation marker in the CLI — harmless as long as the same uuid is not used again | ①·② = certain (M3) · ③ correction = likely (one observation + source reading) · no natural race = certain (M3 c1) · marker harmless = likely | around function `Cu` @217308972 · drainer @217253965 · @217254615 · Phase 0 §4 |
| Envelope: `{"type":"control_request","request_id":<자유 문자열>,"request":{…}}` → `{"type":"control_response","response":{"subtype":"success"\|"error","request_id":…,…}}` | success side = certain (M3) · error side = likely | @197644050 · @201306322 |
| Interrupt `control_request{subtype:"interrupt", cancel_queued:true}` cancels the waiting items together and carries them in the response `cancelled` list. Without it the waiting items **survive and run into the next turn** (`still_queued`) | Likely | @197103021 · @197108837 |
| `system/init` ★**does not come at startup — it comes once each time a turn starts**★: 0.58–0.86 s after the `started` of the command that opened the turn. It does not come for folded input. `capabilities` = `interrupt_receipt_v1` · `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` · `msg_lifecycle_v1` · `mcp_read_resource_v1` · `mcp_tool_ui_meta_v1`. There is a means of feature detection, but **it does not yet exist early in the first turn of an incarnation** (§5-4 absorbs it) | Certain (M4) | @197001639 · list @209757455 · Phase 0 §5 |
| Our JSON path (`StdioTransport`) does **not support** interrupt (`transport/stdio.rs:371`) — claude JSON has no interrupt at all today | Certain (code) | |
| Our spawn already loads a per-agent settings fragment with `--settings` | Certain (code) | `backend/claude/mod.rs:253` · daemon `control/mod.rs:196-217` |
| The `additionalContext` of hooks `PostToolUse`·`PostToolBatch` goes **into the model as context** — **it is not a new user turn** (documentation: the gist of 「adds context to Claude, not a user message」). Besides `command`, hook forms include **`http`** · **`mcp_tool`**, which can be set **without a process**. `once:true` also exists. `disableAllHooks`·`allowManagedHooksOnly`·`allowedHttpHookUrls` in user·managed settings can turn it off or block it. We **could not find** an in-session dynamic registration means that 「대기분이 있을 때만 훅을 거는」 (uncertain) | Likely (official hooks documentation + schema @195644747·@195645407) | code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks |
| The `--replay-user-messages` echo-back **does not come when written; it comes when consumed** — fold path = **right before** `started` (0.4–1.1 ms) · missed the boundary and drained as a new turn = **1.5–2.1 s after** `started` (`system/init` in between) · cancelled input = **does not come**. The `timestamp` field of the echo-back is **the time it was written** (not the time of consumption). → the accumulator has to accept **both** the order in which the echo-back comes before `Delivered` and the order in which it comes after (§5-7) | Certain (M2 — three paths repeated) | Phase 0 §3 |
| Folded input is left in the transcript (`.jsonl`) not as a `user` line but as **one `{"type":"attachment","attachment":{"type":"queued_command","prompt":[…우리 본문…],"source_uuid":<우리 uuid>,"commandMode":"prompt","timestamp":…}}` line**, followed by `queue-operation {"operation":"remove","reason":"absorbed_mid_turn"}`. Cancelled input leaves no body (only enqueue/dequeue). Input that missed the boundary and became a new turn is a plain `user` line. → since today `consume_line` skips `attachment`, **folded input disappears on resume restore** (PRD R5 — this feature makes that case common). We did not read the effect on restore order | Shape = certain (M5) | `backend/claude/mod.rs` transcript section · Phase 0 §6 |

### 3-2. codex app-server (source reading 0.154.0 · measured 0.156.1)

| Fact | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Waiting input goes into the history all at once **after one sampling request and its tools have all ended, before the next request is built** (the loop head). New input does not preempt a sampling in progress. Measurement: two steers mid-tool → after tool completion · before the next sampling, **in the order typed**, two `userMessage` echoes carrying `clientId` | Certain (M6) · spot = likely (source + vendor test) | `core/src/session/turn.rs:329-350` · `tests/suite/pending_input.rs:1123` · Phase 0 §7 |
| Even if the sampling ends with no tool, if there is waiting input it **continues the same turn** (`needs_follow_up = model_needs_follow_up \|\| has_pending_input`). Measurement: steers sent at two points at the end of sampling (`agentMessage` completion · `thread/tokenUsage/updated`) were both consumed as the **follow-up sampling of the same turn**. → **the end of an answer with no tools is also a boundary** — but the window from the end-of-answer signal to the check of waiting items was **not measured** (T3·T4 only hit once each — M16) | Certain (M6 T3·T4) · end-of-answer window = not measured | `turn.rs:452-475` |
| Waiting input that came in after the last check is **only recorded in the history at turn end** (with no response). Phase 0 (M6) could not hit that branch (the window is even later than `tokenUsage/updated`) and ★Phase 0b (M10) hit it★ — 3 out of 4 times · its echo (`clientId`) comes 4.5–4.8 ms **before** `turn/completed` and carries the id of the ending turn · at that moment it is also loaded into `thread/items/list` (3/3) · there is no sampling after that | Existence of the branch · echo order = certain (measured 3/3 · vendor source 0.156.1 `core/src/tasks/mod.rs:655`·`:673`) · early edge of the window = inference (M10 stopped at 3 hits) | `core/src/tasks/mod.rs:588-645` `on_task_finished` (0.154.0 source reading) · Phase 0 §7 · [Phase 0b report](../../research/mid-turn-phase0b-measurements-2026-09-26.md) §3 |
| `turn/steer{threadId, clientUserMessageId?, input, expectedTurnId(필수)}` → `{turnId}`. **A success response comes within 1–5 ms, far before the echo — acceptance is not delivery.** No active turn → −32600 「no active turn to steer」 (measured — a steer sent with the old turn id in the very handler that received `turn/completed`) · review·compaction turns are rejected · id mismatch is rejected. **The method also exists in 0.135.0, and `clientUserMessageId` is in 0.140.0** (the same as ADR-0198's floor) | Response·−32600 = certain (M6) · the rest = likely (tag check) | `app-server-protocol/src/protocol/v2/turn.rs:277-299` · `turn_processor.rs:1006-1122` |
| Input put in is echoed back **at the moment it is consumed** as a `userMessage` item carrying `clientId`. If `clientUserMessageId` is loaded in `turn/start`, `clientId` is also attached to the echo of that first input (today our code does not load it — `protocol.rs:537`) | Certain (M6) | Phase 0 §7 |
| **There is no API that removes just one waiting input that was handed over** — only interrupt (`turn/interrupt`) discards everything (`clear_pending`). Measurement: on interrupt, a message that was steered has **no echo and is not in `thread/items/list` either** (it is silently discarded). → the codex ✕ withdraws a message only while we hold it, and the ✕ of a handed-over item only removes it from the list (§5-5) | API absence = likely (source — exhaustive check of `InputQueue` public verbs) · interrupt behavior = certain (M6) | `core/src/session/input_queue.rs` · `tasks/mod.rs:533` |
| **`thread/items/list` carries the item that went in by steer and the `clientId` of `turn/start` first input** → 「그 글이 이력에 들었나」 **can be asked** — 「기록만」 items are also carried (M10 — 3/3 · the head of the `limit:40` side). ★This design does not use it★ — the echo of an accepted steer comes before turn end and nothing is left to ask, so the end-of-turn probe was withdrawn (§5-5 settlement 2 — kept as the ground for when it is revived). ★**It is a paged list — one page of 「없음」 is not proof of absence**★: walk it with `limit`·`sortDirection`·`cursor` and if `nextCursor` is absent or `null`, that is the end of that direction (schema + measurement 2026-09-16 — `protocol.rs:485-494`). The schema also has a `turnId` field that narrows to one turn (our code did not copy it — `protocol.rs:467-470`) | Consumed items = certain (M6) · 「기록만」 = certain (M10 · 3/3) · page-end rule = certain (measured) | `protocol.rs:222` · Phase 0 §7 · Phase 0b §3 |
| ★**The tools of an interrupted turn keep running**★ — a shell command ran to the end even after `turn/completed(interrupted)`, and its `item/completed` (**old turn id** · status `completed`) arrived 17 s after the interrupt, **after the next two turns ended** → code that counts tools per turn has to **tell them apart by turn id** (with 「턴 경계에서 0」 alone, a late completion makes a negative number) | Shape = certain (one observation) · generality = likely | Phase 0 §7 |
| ★**After a tool-completion notification there is a window until the check of waiting items — 6–64 ms**★ (the check is 2–9 ms after `thread/tokenUsage/updated`). A steer that came in within it **entered the same boundary (10/10)**. It is the **opposite** of the edition 1 source reading (「도구 완료 알림을 보내는 그 프로세스 안에서 곧바로 확인 — 거의 늘 놓친다」). The harness's reaction was 0.1–0.4 ms, and whether **our daemon's reaction delay** (path reader → writer → steer write) is shorter than the minimum window of 6 ms was **not measured** (M15). We also do not know the shape of the window when tools are parallel or the model is slow. → the ground for the tool segment of the codex holding policy (§5-5) | Harness hit = likely (M7) · applying to the daemon = uncertain (M15) | Phase 0 §8 |
| **`thread/tokenUsage/updated` comes after the sampling and its tools have all ended, right before the check of waiting items** — +6–55 ms after the tool-completion notification, and the check 2–9 ms after that (M7, 10 times). It also comes in a sampling with no tools (M6 T4). So it can be used as the last signal of 「샘플링 끝」, but the window after it is the narrowest, so we do not lean hand-over on it (§5-5 boundary signal ③) | Order = likely (M7 · T4) · window = harness measurement | Phase 0 §7·§8 |
| The experimental API `thread/queue/{add,list,update,delete,reorder,start}` is **「내구성 있는 사용자 메시지 큐 + 한가할 때 배출」** — it starts as a new turn when the thread becomes idle. **It is not tool-boundary delivery** — it does not fit the decision to hand over at the earliest boundary | Likely (source) | `ext/queue/src/lib.rs:1` · `service.rs:367-405` · `common.rs:624-656` |
| A `turn/start` in progress is handled as a steer (same turn id) — from the response alone, whether it is a steer or a new turn is told apart **only by id comparison** | Likely (0.154.0 source) | `turn_processor.rs:636-668` |
| **Version source:** the `thread/start` response `result.thread.cliVersion` (measured `"0.156.1"`) is the cleanest — it is a field our `Thread.cli_version` already reads (`protocol.rs:518`) and the handshake leaves in the log (`transport.rs:886`). `thread/resume` also gives the same `Thread` (schema — we did not measure that response). Fallback = `^[^/]+/(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)` of the `initialize` response `userAgent` — its head is **our `clientInfo.name`** and the closing parenthesis holds **our version (0.1.0)**, so finding it with 「마지막 semver」 reads our version | Certain (M8 — measured string + source) | Phase 0 §9 |
| codex also has a `PostToolUse` hook (`additionalContext`). But hooks require **trust approval** (a hash of the command string — including our exe's absolute path), so **the approval pops up again every release** — for that reason ADR-0216 removed the codex hook machinery altogether | Certain (ADR-0216) · hook fields likely | `hooks/src/events/post_tool_use.rs:40-51` |

Even if they diverge, we do not force a match on our side — if the list stays diverged from the vendor, report |
| **M14** | claude: the vendor's handling of a message sent while loading a resumed conversation (`--resume`) | The premise of PRD §3-11 claude (keep it in the list and hand it over at once — the vendor handles it after loading) · if it diverges, report (PRD R5) |
| **M15** | ★**Green (PASS · 2026-09-26) — tool-end reaction delay sum 2275 µs < 6000 µs** (`max(①) 783 + max(리더 밀림) 1405 + max(②) 87` — the three are maxima of different events, so a conservative sum · the dominant term = one tool end in a turn where three parallel tools were pouring output at once) · **end-of-answer reaction sum 221 µs** (a value to compare against M16's narrowest window 9.4 ms) · release daemon · codex-cli 0.156.1 · model `gpt-6-sol`/`high` (M16 is `gpt-6-luna`/`low`) · all handed-over input was received in the same turn · [report](../../research/mid-turn-m15-measurements-2026-09-26.md) · ★limit: the samples for a steer woken directly by the tool-end signal (`hop=signal` · `signal=tool_end`) are zero — under `Immediate` that path does not run end to end, so the legs were measured separately and added (the design below as it is)★ · ★After P8-fix, a steer released by P8's new wake-up (a held answer segment being released by the start of an item outside it — §5-5 segment table 「그 밖」 row) is marked separately with `hop="zone"` (a new Hold segment or boundary signal erases that mark)★ · ★Measurement definition change (P2g): **reader lag is an upper bound of the busy time, minus the time the reader waited blocked on `read()`** (`DrainMark`) — the first trial measurement (M15 phase A · debug) also counted model thinking · waiting between turns (up to 101 s), so it was not used for the verdict · the verdict is by release (in debug ① comes out large)★ → **P8 landed** (§9)★ Our daemon's reaction delay — from the moment the path reader receives the current turn's tool `item/completed` (the running tool set becoming empty) → to the moment the steer write released by that signal ends. ★**In the codex skeleton (§9 P2 — `Immediate`) that path does not run in one go**★ — there is no held item in the tool segment, so there is no steer for the tool-end signal to release. So we **split it into two legs, measure on the real path route, and add the maxima (decision)**: ① **reader signal → writer wake** — since `Immediate` also tracks segments and wakes the writer on the tool-end signal (§5-5), those two timestamps. N times — ★be sure to include a command that pours output until it ends (a build·test that prints thousands of lines) and parallel tools★ (reader lag below) ② **writer wake → steer write end** — in the hop 「`turn/start` 응답(턴 id) → 쥔 항목 steer」 where the same writer steers a held item (from the second of several left at turn end · a message typed before the turn id). Also record that whole hop (response received → steer write end) for comparison. ★② is also measured under a delta flood★ — that hop is at the turn head, so deltas are not flowing yet, but the woken writer contends for the state lock with the reader that processes lines under that lock (§5-5 segment tracking), so that hop alone makes ② come out small. So we also measure N times in the hop where `Immediate` steers at once — a message typed while a running command pours output (what woke it is not the reader signal but only that message's announce, but from waking to the end of the steer write it is the same span). ② maximum = the maximum of the two hops combined. For parallel tools, also record the window from the last completion to the vendor's check of waiting items (the echo of an item handed over at once mid-tool marks that check). ★The two legs are measured only after the reader receives the completion line — the time that line waited in the pipe before that is also reaction delay★. The tool's `item/completed` also comes right after a long delta flow — commands·file edits·MCP tools keep emitting `item/commandExecution/outputDelta` · `item/fileChange/outputDelta` · `item/mcpToolCall/progress` until they end (`decoder.rs:151`·`:153`·`:155` — we only do not translate them, we do receive them), and the reader splits the envelope for each such line and hands it to the translator (`transport.rs:2225`·`:2233-2234`). So when measuring ①, also record the **reader lag** (the time the reader processed earlier lines without waiting even once on a read until just before processing that completion line — the time that line waited in the pipe cannot be longer than this) (★implementation reflected — the `lag_us` of the first implementation (P2d) measured from the earlier short read and counted even the time it waited blocked on `read()` (it diverged from this definition — found in the first trial measurement) · P2g fixed it to this definition: stamp the time before the read and subtract the blocked span (`transport.rs` `DrainMark` — the larger-side error where the tail of the previous chunk is included in the first line after going idle is written in that doc)★). Verdict (decision) = **tool-end reaction delay = tool-end ① maximum + the reader-lag maximum at that time + ② maximum** is green if smaller than the minimum window (M7 — 6 ms). All three measure the same span as the real path and each maximum is added, so that sum does not come out smaller **within the sample** than the real reaction (the moment the completion line reached the pipe → the end of the steer write) — it cannot cover an output flood longer than the sample, so choose the sample as above. ★**The end of an answer is measured separately**★ — measure ① N times also at the end-of-answer signal (`agentMessage`·`plan` `item/completed` when no tool is running — `Immediate` also wakes the writer there, §5-5) (the lines that flowed before are answer deltas, so the reader lag differs from the tool end — write it down per signal), and compare **end-of-answer reaction delay = end-of-answer ① maximum + the reader-lag maximum at that time + ② maximum** with M16's window (M16 row — it is a value to compare, not a verdict). The route of putting a measurement-only hold switch in the skeleton is §10-5 | The tool-segment premise of the codex holding policy (§5-5) — ★**green → P8 landed** (§9 P7 · operating default = `AtEarliestBoundary`)★. ~~If red, stop before designing the holding policy (§9 P8) and ask the user (holding versus another path — PRD R8) — if we cannot hit the tool segment, holding makes delivery one boundary later.~~ ★Edition 9 — this stop condition did not arise★ |
| **M16** | ★**Green (2026-09-26) — A 14/14 · B 12/12 · C(plan) 14/14, minimum window 9.4 ms · [report](../../research/mid-turn-m16-measurements-2026-09-26.md) — P8 can hold the answer segment too (a value to compare with M15 = 9.4 ms)**★ codex: **the end boundary of an answer with no tools** — is there a window from the end-of-answer signal (`agentMessage` · `plan` `item/completed` when no tool is running) to the vendor's check of waiting items · does a steer handed over at the moment we receive that signal get into the **follow-up sampling of the same turn** (M7 method · N times · number of hits and window length — both for a turn with no tools and for the last answer after tools · also a sampling that ends with a `plan` item — if it reproduces with a turn that makes it emit a plan) · compare the window with M15's **end-of-answer reaction delay** (end-of-answer ① + reader lag + ② — M15 row) (it is not the tool-end reaction delay — the reader lag hangs on the lines that flowed before that signal and is measured per signal). If the `plan` end cannot be reproduced, in the segment decision put `plan` as 「그 밖」 (at once) and report that — we do not hold an item whose end signal is not confirmed | The 「답 구간」 field of the §5-5 segment table. Green = hold the answer segment too · red = hand over the answer segment at once. ★Even if red, we do not stop★ — it is a branch PRD §3-1 already decided as 「붙들면 늦어지는 구간은 곧바로」, so it is not a user question (the ✕ of a message typed in that segment only removes it from the list — §5-5) |
| **M17** | codex: for a steer handed over in a turn that ends with `turn/completed(failed)` — does the vendor record that waiting item (does the echo come before `turn/completed`) or discard it | ★Likely (vendor source) — before measurement★: the `Err` branch of 0.156.1 `core/src/tasks/mod.rs` `on_task_finished` (`:624-637`) also emits `TurnComplete` (`:827`) after passing `take_pending_input_for_turn_state` (`:655`) · `run_hooks_and_record_inputs` (`:673`) — it is recorded and the echo comes before `turn/completed(failed)`. §5-5 settlement 2 — if it records, it is a receipt as usual. If it discards, the turn ends with no echo for that message, which stays as acceptance unknown (gray · ✕) and is not sent again (accepted — §5-9). ★Does not block★ — fixture·check only (it needs a turn that ends in error, so it is hard to reproduce — write it down when the chance comes). If gray ones often remain and the feel changes, raise it to the user again |

## 4. Where to store it and when to hand it over

### 4-1. Storage location — the daemon's agent session layer

We compared three, including the user's proposal 「웹소켓에 보관」, and **the user fixed it as daemon authority · agent session layer (2026-09-25)**. The table below is the ground for that.

| | Daemon · agent crate (session + path) ← **recommended** | WS/net layer | Frontend·shell (per window) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Per-agent authority | ○ one per agent·incarnation | ✕ per connection — a popout · a second window · the CLI · the LLM see different queues | ✕ per window |
| Reconnect·restart | ○ it survives a client reconnect (daemon lifetime) | ✕ gone when the connection drops | ✕ gone when the window closes |
| Does it know turn·tool state | ○ the path holds the turn state (codex `TurnState`), and the output passes through the core | ✕ net was built not to know agents (ADR-0129 isolation gate) | ✕ it only receives and watches the ring |
| LLM-first control | ○ the command bus · CLI read the same registry | ✕ | ✕ a second control path |

★**Why the spot is the agent crate and not the daemon crate**★ — in codex, 「지금 곧바로 넘기나 · 쥐나」 (the idle decision · FIFO · the hand-over segments) has to be decided **under one lock** together with the path's turn state (ADR-0193 · §5-5), and the ring (`OutputCore`) is also in the agent crate. The daemon only routes commands.

### 4-2. Hand-over timing — the decision and the strategies compared

**Decision = 「에이전트가 받을 수 있는 가장 이른 때」 per backend** (user principle — PRD §3-1). We hold only while holding does not delay delivery. The window in which the boundary can be hit differs by vendor (M7), so the answer splits. ✕ is on every item of the list regardless of the hand-over time — while held it also withdraws the message, and after hand-over it only removes it from the list (§0 · §5-5).

- **claude = S2 (immediate hand-over).** If we held it and handed over after seeing the tool complete, we would miss that boundary (M7 0/10) — it has to be written to stdin as soon as it is received so that it is read at the end of the tool running now. ✕ is a vendor cancel request (§5-4).
- **codex = 「가장 이른 경계까지 붙듦」 — S1 widened to the segments outside tools.** We hold while a tool runs and hand over the moment the tool ends (M7 10/10 — window 6–64 ms). For the segment where the answer is written with no tools, we measure whether the same window exists (M16): if it does we hold until the end of the answer, and if not we hand over at once. For segments where holding could miss, such as right after a boundary, we hand over at once. A ✕ while held is erasing on our side, so there is no vendor call, and a ✕ after hand-over only removes it from the list (a bubble if received late). When to hand over is decided by a swappable policy inside the path, and the first landing is the policy that hands over at once — after measurement (M15·M16) we switch to the holding policy (§5-5 · §9). ★Edition 9 — switched: M15 · M16 green (§3-3) → operating default = the holding policy (both tool segment · answer segment) · the policy that hands over at once is left as a place to go back to (§9 P8)★

Notation: **boundary** = the point at which the vendor folds in the waiting items (claude = end of the tool batch · codex = end of sampling + tools). **Cancel window** = the span in which ✕ withdraws the message before it reaches the agent (✕ itself is always there — a ✕ outside the window only removes it from the list).

| | S1 hold → hand over on observing tool **completion** | S2 hand over at once | S4 boundary hook pulls it | S5 hold → hand over on observing tool **start** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Delivery timing claude** | ✕ **missed 0/10** (measured M7 — consistent with the source reading) — the end of the next tool batch, and if none, a new turn after `result` | ○ exact (the vendor folds it) | ○ exact (`PostToolBatch` right before the fold) | △ usually exact — if a tool ends faster than our round trip (Read·Grep kinds), one boundary late |
| **Delivery timing codex** | ○ **hit 10/10** (measured M7 — window 6–64 ms after the tool completion notification) · **our daemon's reaction delay not measured** (M15) | ○ exact | — (§3-2: hook trust = the path ADR-0216 rejected) | ○ usually exact — codex tools are shell launches, usually 100 ms or more |
| **Cancel window claude** | ○ until tool completion (in our hands) | ○ **until right before the fold** (`cancel_async_message` — measured up to ~540 ms after the tool result) | ○ until it is pulled | ○ in our hands until tool start + a vendor cancel after that |
| **Cancel window codex** | ○ until tool completion (in our hands — no vendor call) | ✕ **none** (once handed over, it is over — there is no API that removes one handed-over item, §3-2 · ✕ only removes it from the list) | — | △ only while no tool is running |
| **「전달됨」 signal** | claude `started` (@internal) · codex `clientId` echo | same | the moment we pulled it (vendor-independent) | same as S1 |
| **Resistance to vendor change** | claude leans on the @internal lifecycle · codex leans on the window between 「도구 완료 알림 → 대기분 확인」 (if the window narrows, one boundary late — nothing is lost) | claude leans on the @internal lifecycle (delivery signal) + the control protocol `cancel_async_message` (cancel — no @internal marker) | leans on the hook contract (public) but **the model does not take it as the user's words** (context attachment) · user·managed settings can turn it off | same as S1 |
| **Cost** | medium — the path releases on seeing a tool completion told apart by the current turn id (§3-2) · segment tracking | **small** — claude = write path branch · decoder translation · cancel line / codex = steer while Active · end-of-turn disposition (§5-5) | large — control route · hook in the settings fragment · end-of-turn fallback · launch per tool batch or local round trip | medium (codex) — the path sees tool start and `turn/steer` |
| **Decision** | **codex adopted** (tool segment — the skeleton of 「가장 이른 경계까지 붙듦」) · claude **rejected by the user** (「한 걸음 늦다」 — consistent with measured 0/10) | **claude adopted** · codex only in segments where holding could make it late (§5-5 segment table) | **rejected by the user** (「훅마다 매번 뭔가 실행하면 안 된다」) + the difference that it does not mean a user turn | **rejected** — S1, which holds until the tool *ends*, has the same delivery timing and a longer cancel window (PRD §6) |

- **Follow-up on the S1 source-reading ground:** the claude-side source reading (「접기가 도구 결과 직후 돈다」) has reached a conclusion — there is an asynchronous wait inside the fold but the snapshot is before it (§3-1 row 1 · M7 0/10). The codex-side source reading (「`has_pending_input` 이 도구 완료 알림을 보내는 그 프로세스 안에서 곧바로 돈다」) was **wrong** — there are 6–64 ms in between (§3-2 · M7 10/10). That window is the ground of the codex decision.

### 4-3. Implementation conditions of the decision

- **claude = S2.** As today, write to stdin the moment it is received. What changes is only **display and cancel** — mid-turn, emit a list event instead of a synthetic echo, and decide the outcome by the lifecycle (§5-4).
  - **Detection:** the claude backend fills the backend-neutral three values `DeliveryAck` (Unknown / Available / Unavailable — §5-0). `Available` if `msg_lifecycle_v1` is in the `capabilities` of `system/init` or **the first `command_lifecycle` line comes**, and `Unavailable` if init comes without that capability. Handling during `Unknown` = §5-4 (M4 — the gap early in the first turn).
  - **If `Unavailable` (an old CLI · the vendor withdrew it), today's behavior as it is** (PRD R6 · user decision N1 (a) 「알아서 업데이트」) — a bubble in the sent place with no list·✕. We put in no machinery for old versions and leave that fact as one log line (decision). An item that already went onto the list before it was determined becomes a bubble in place (§5-2 `AckUnavailable`).
  - ★The case where the lifecycle comes out but only cancel does not work★ did not arise in Phase 0 (M3 green). If it arises, claude's ✕ cannot withdraw the message and only removes it from the list (the same outcome as a handed-over codex item), and that is a change in feel that alters PRD §3-6, so it is raised to the user (PRD R1).
- **codex = 「가장 이른 경계까지 붙듦」** (0.140.0 or higher — `turn/steer` + `clientUserMessageId`). When to hand over is answered per segment by the swappable policy inside the codex backend (`HandOverPolicy` — §5-5): the tool segment is held and handed over at the end of the tool · states where handing over is impossible (connecting · waiting for the turn id · end-of-turn settlement · halt after error · a turn where steer was rejected) are held (both policies) · and the other segments are handed over at once (`turn/steer` if a turn is in progress, `turn/start` if not). **Floor decision** = `thread.cliVersion` (M8) → if absent, the first semver of `userAgent` → if both fail, treat it as below the floor (decision: if we send `clientUserMessageId` to an unknown version, the echo check silently breaks — we fall to the safe side).
  - **If below the floor, today's behavior as it is** (user decision N1 (a)) — held until the end of the turn and shown only by the vendor echo (`delivery_ack = Unavailable`). Items are not put on the list before the floor decision, so there are no items whose status is not yet determined (§5-5).
  - **The landing is two steps** (§9): first the `Immediate` policy (hand over at once if possible · hold while handing over is impossible — it does not look worse than today · the ✕ of an item handed over at once mostly only removes it from the list), and after measurement (M15 · M16) `AtEarliestBoundary` (holding in the tool segment — and in the answer segment if M16 is green · the ✕ of that segment withdraws the message). ★If the tool-segment measurement (M15) cannot hit the window, stop before designing the holding policy and ask the user again (PRD R8)★. ★Edition 9 — both steps were taken: P2 (`Immediate`) → M15 · M16 green → P8 (`AtEarliestBoundary` · including the answer segment) is the operating default★
- **The spot where the feel of the two backends diverges (PRD R3)** — ✕ is on every item on both backends and pressing it removes it from the list — here they are the same. What differs is whether the message is withdrawn: claude withdraws until right before the fold, and codex withdraws only while we hold it. A message typed in a segment where codex hands over at once (right after a boundary · while an item not confirmed as a tool is running · the answer segment if M16 is red) reaches the agent even if ✕ is pressed, and if received it stands as a bubble at the end of the conversation (outcome (b) — user: 「상관없어」). ★This divergence is a subject of ADR-0192 「못 맞추면 보고」 (PRD R3)★ — write it in the new ADR as a difference in feel between the two backends (§8).
- **S4 is dropped (one-line reason)** — even though there are forms with no process (`http`·`mcp_tool`), it is invoked per tool batch (user rejection), and the message it puts in is hook context and not a user turn, so what the model takes it to mean diverges from the 「평범한 사용자 말풍선」 of PRD §3-4.
- **codex `thread/queue/*` is not used** — it is an experimental API and it drains **when the thread becomes idle**, so tool-boundary delivery does not happen. Holding and end-of-turn disposition (§5-5) are already held by our path, so there is no value in bringing in one more durable store of the vendor's.

---

## 5. Design

### 5-0. The shape of the input path — source · mid-turn input policy · one-line order

- **The source is taken by the write API.** The session write verb takes `InputOrigin::{User, Mail}`. WS `WriteStdin` (`connection_core.rs:1080-1094` → `manager.write_stdin` `manager.rs:2671`) = `User` · mail delivery (`manager.write_stdin_observed`·`submit_stdin_observed` — `manager.rs:2675-2694`) and other non-human callers (`saturation_pilot`) = `Mail` (today's path). Today all three entrances join into `session.write_input_observed` (`session.rs:220`) and the session does not know the source — so we bring the source in as an argument of the write API (★implementation reflected — the `origin` argument was added only to `AgentManager::write_stdin`: WS = `User` · the `*_observed` path and the pilot · smoke are all `Mail` · the line numbers in this line are as of edition 8★). ★**`Mail` does not go on the list on either backend**★ — claude tells it apart at the classification stage and codex in the path `TurnInput.origin`, by the same value (PRD §2 · AC22).
- **The mid-turn input policy is reported by the backend (ADR-0004 shape).** Two backend-neutral fields in `SpawnedSession` (★implementation reflected — there is no such type: the fields were added to the `SpawnParts` the backend returns · the first-turn port `first_turn_sink` goes into the `open_spawn` argument of every backend and only the codex app-server plugs it into the path (§5-8)★):
  - `mid_turn: MidTurnPolicy` = `SessionClassified` (claude JSON — the session classifies and the vendor releases) · `TransportOwned` (codex JSON — the path classifies and releases) · `None` (terminal · shell · gemini — today's path as it is).
    - ★`TransportOwned` leaves hand-over timing and cancel to the path★ — when to hand over (codex's swappable hand-over policy `HandOverPolicy` — §5-5) is decided inside the path and the session does not know it. The session's cancel command only passes it on with the path verb `withdraw` — whether it still holds that item or has already handed it over is known only to the path (§5-5 「✕」). So it does not take this lock (below), and the order is carried by the path state lock.
  - `delivery_ack: Arc<DeliveryAck>` = three values shared by the incarnation (`Unknown`·`Available`·`Unavailable`) — the backend fills in **whether it can give a per-item receipt notification** (claude = §5-4 detection · codex = floor decision §5-5). If `Unavailable`, it is today's behavior as it is (user decision N1 (a) — §4-3).
  - `SessionClassified` gives one more piece the backend makes — **making a cancel line** (`cancel_line(id) -> Vec<u8>`). There is no separate turn-signal piece — claude `Delivered` is written in the turn table as in progress and gives the same value at the same moment (§5-4). The session splits only by these values and **does not branch on the encoder tag or the backend name** — splitting that way leaks backend knowledge into the session (ADR-0004 · §10-5).
  - ★Why we do not carry receipt-possibility per item★ — once `Available` stands it is not reversed, so the `Unavailable` decision applies equally to **all items that went on the list by then** (= all classified under `Unknown`). One incarnation value determines the per-item decision. ★「그때까지」 is **ring order**★ — an item classified under `Unknown` but that stood on the ring **later** than the decision is rewritten by the core according to the decision rule (§5-3).
- **One-line order — the session input lock.** One `input_order: Mutex<()>` per session. The **user input** of `SessionClassified` (classification → `Queued` emit → `send_input`) and **cancel** (registry check → `CancelRequested` emit → cancel line `send_input`) run as one unit holding this lock. ★Implementation reflected — the registry check of cancel and the `CancelRequested` record are atomic in **one** replay span (`OutputCore::emit_cancel_request` → `Recorded` · `AlreadyCancelling` · `NotListed`) — if the pump's `Delivered` is reduced first it is `NotFound` and no line is written, and if after, a line is written as `Requested` and the item is closed as `Delivered` (P3b · P3 review)★.
  - ① **Classification and emission are atomic** — even if two inputs come at once, the idle/waiting decision does not diverge from the emission order.
  - ② ★**The cancel line of id X is written only after the write of X's message has ended (`send_input` has returned)**★ — even if another window or the LLM sends a cancel the moment it sees `Queued`, it waits at the lock. The accident this prevents: if the cancel reaches stdin before the message, no reservation is set mid-turn so the message is **delivered** (M3 c2) while the screen trusts the cancel. If the write fails, it is closed as `Dropped{Rejected}` inside the lock (when receipt-possibility is `Unknown` it never went on the list — below), so a following cancel is `NOT_FOUND`.
  - `Queued` is emitted **before** `send_input` (the lifecycle comes within 1 ms of writing — so that the receipt does not overtake the emission). ★**But only when receipt-possibility is `Unknown` is it emitted after the write (reason §5-4)**★.
  - Lock order = **`input_order → replay → queued_inputs`** (emit does not take `input_order`, so there is no reverse order) — add one line to ADR-0006. ★Implementation reflected — a fourth stood: **`input_order → replay → 명부(QueuedInputs) → 대기 목록 표(InputsPendingTable — 잎)`** (`session.rs` `input_order` field doc) · `StatusSink` is called without the core lock · the pump · emit · `finish` do not take `input_order` · the emission line inside the codex path is `Announcer.order → {상태 락, replay → 명부 → 대기 목록 표}` (announce does `core.emit` holding `order` · unrelated to the session lock)★.
  - ★**`send_input` does not block**★: both paths put it into a bounded queue and return at once, and if full or closed it is `Err` (`transport/input_queue.rs` head — the console family has a byte limit · the codex path has a dedicated writer of the same shape). So the time this lock is held is only classification·emit·queue insertion, and the long one is one latch commit of the incarnation's first submit (the ADR-0006 exception clause below).
  - ★**Only the session's user input·cancel path takes `input_order` — the output pump (decoder) does not**★. Ground: this lock contains the ADR-0226 latch commit (profile lock + disk write) — if the pump waited, **output would stand behind disk I/O**. The order of pump-side events (the cannot-receive decision etc.) and classification is matched by **the core's replay lock** (§5-3 · §5-4), so there is no reason to tie the pump to the input path to obtain ring order = decision order. (「stdin 쓰기와 순환 대기」 is not a ground — a write only puts into a queue.)
  - ★**One ADR-0006 exception**★: because the session emits while holding `input_order`, the subscriber fan-out (`OutputSink::send`) runs **while holding that lock** — an exception to ADR-0006 「lock 미보유 send」. The ground for there being no deadlock = the operating sink is `try_send` and does not block (when full, `SinkError` — the `FrameSink::try_send` of daemon `agent_conn.rs`). We do not leave that property to chance and raise it to the **trait contract of `OutputSink::send`** (「막히지 않는다 — 못 보내면 기다리지 말고 `SinkError`」 · `types.rs` `trait OutputSink` doc — landed · §8). The ADR-0226 latch commit (profile disk write) of the first submit also runs inside this lock — it is once per incarnation and makes only that incarnation's input·cancel wait briefly, so it is harmless.
  - Mail (`Mail`) does not take this lock (it has no list·cancel). The order of codex (`TransportOwned`) is carried by the path state lock (§5-5).
- ★**Mail's idle = the same as user input — no turn running and the user list is empty · and not a halt after error** (PRD §3-9 · user decision N6 · N11)★. User input comes first — if there is a user item in the list, mail waits until the turn those items went into ends, and may be later than today (the user accepted that). ★**Halt after error**★ = after a turn ended oddly (a turn the vendor closed with an error · codex's request-stage failure) until the user's next turn ends in success — meanwhile mail does not go in on its own (user: 「진짜 에러면 강제로 쑤셔넣으면 안되잖아」). The fact that blocks mail is, on both backends, one field of the turn observation table (「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」 below) — the path's `halted` on the codex path blocks only turns the path opens by itself (§5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」). Mail in terminal (PTY) mode is today's as it is (with `mid_turn == None` the list is always empty and the fact below is always false — the halt also does not stand in terminal mode, which has no decoder). The same definition is used in two layers:
  - **Messaging kernel (common to both backends — before reaching the path):** the kernel's busy decision reads only through the turn observation port (`TurnFacts` — daemon adapter `ManagerTurnFacts`, `messaging_host.rs:240-270`). To the fact the port returns (`TurnFact` — `busy.rs:76-81`) it adds **`inputs_pending: bool`** (that incarnation's user list is not empty) and **`last_end_failed: bool`** (that incarnation has not yet had a cleanly successful turn end after a turn that ended in error — both backends, 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」 below), and the decision `busy = in_turn ∨ inputs_pending ∨ last_end_failed` is made by the kernel's `BusyPolicy` (decision — the adapter is 「얇음이 계약」 and puts in no decision · policy lives in the lib · ADR-0110).
  - **The source of that fact = the waiting list table (decision)** — the manager holds a leaf table of the same shape as the turn observation table (`TurnObservations`) and injects it into the core. It writes only when the registry changes between empty↔full, **inside the very emit** in which the core reduced the registry (the writing spot = 「적는 순서」 below). The reason not to add a field to the turn observation table = the spots that write the `in_turn` of that table must be only two, the classifier and cleanup (ADR-0127 「세 번째 호출자」) — a fact with a different writing spot goes in a separate table (`last_end_failed` below has the classifier as its writing spot as before, so it is a field of the turn table). The adapter reads the two tables without the sessions lock (today's discipline as it is).
  - **Doorbell:** the moment the registry **empties**, the core rings the flush doorbell by the same road as a turn end — today a turn end is the core's `StatusSink::turn_ended` push → the daemon adapter (`turn_ended` `messaging_host.rs:879` → `enqueue` `:368-372` — only coalescing + a non-blocking send) → the flush lane. So that parked mail is not tied up until the next turn end in the case where the turn does not end and only the list empties (codex where an acceptance-unknown item was removed with ✕ · an item whose receipt came after the turn end). Even if it rings during a halt after error, mail does not go in — the kernel's busy reads the halt (`last_end_failed` — both backends) (below). Whether to add one more verb to `StatusSink` or widen the same verb is decided at implementation (decision candidate = add a verb — 「턴이 끝났다」 does not blur the name · the flush side asks busy again anyway). The 「표 갱신 → 통지」 order is the same as turn observation, so no wake-up is lost.
  - ★**Writing order — 「비었다」 comes after turn observation (decision)**★. The registry reduction is inside the replay lock, but the progress of turn observation is written after that lock is released (`output_core.rs:340-344`). If 「비었다」 were written inside the lock within a single claude `Delivered` that empties the list (progress of the drain turn — §5-4), there would be a moment when both facts are 「한가」 at once, and mail that asks busy then (including a doorbell rung in between) would be folded into the user's drain turn (PRD §3-9) — and user input that came in at the same moment would also be misclassified as Direct and become a bubble in the sent place (PRD AC3 — §5-4). So:
    - ① **Full** is written inside the replay lock that reduced it — mail reads busy from the moment `Queued` stands on the ring (claude's `Queued` is before the write, so if postponed, in the gap overlapping a turn end mail could reach stdin before the user message). The table's lock is a leaf (it calls nothing outside).
    - ② **Empty** and the doorbell are written and rung **after** the turn observation update of that emit. An emit that does not pass through turn observation (`emit_without_turn_observation` · the termination synthesis chunk) goes right after the lock is released. ★Implementation reflected — the termination synthesis chunk (`finish`) is the exception: it neither writes 「비었다」 nor rings the doorbell (`inputs_drained`) and calls `forget` on that incarnation's table field together with the turn observation field — mail held flows on in the next incarnation (P1 review)★.
    - ③ When the table writes, it carries that event's seq and **drops a write with a smaller seq** (the same rule as the turn observation table) — so that a 「비었다」 postponed outside the lock does not overwrite a 「찼다」 another thread wrote inside the lock after it.
    - ④ The adapter reads **the list table first and the turn observation table second** — the mirror of the write order (progress → empty), so a read that saw 「비었다」 also sees the progress before it. ★The claude session classification also reads the same two tables in the same order — it does not read the registry directly (§5-4)★.
  - **No fail-open ceiling is put on `inputs_pending`** (user decision N8) — that mail also cannot go in while an item whose receipt never comes remains is a result PRD §3-9 (N8) accepted — the bad case of a slash command is R7 (the item is discarded when the agent ends, and then the list empties). The same holds for an item in codex that was handed over but whose acceptance became unknown (user decision N10 · §5-5 「수락 모름」) — that item is visible in the list and can be removed with ✕. The kernel's 30-minute ceiling (`BUSY_MAX_TURN` — `busy.rs:44-57`, based on the last turn signal) applies only to turn observation, as today. ★It is an intended exception to ADR-0104 「늦게 가는 것 < 안 가는 것」, the ground of that ceiling★ — write it in the new ADR as a user decision (§8). ★We also put no ceiling on the halt after error (`last_end_failed` — both backends) (user decision N11)★ — mail waits until the user types something and the screen has no reason for it (an accepted cost · a mail notification is backlog outside this feature). It falls under the same ADR-0104 exception.
  - **Codex path:** the same rule stands even while mail sent when the kernel was idle is held in the path — at Idle, if there is a user item it opens the user turn first, and the follow-up turn (debt) for a user message that got no answer is also opened before mail (§5-5 「넘기기 규칙」·「우편」).
