The debt is not added to the waiting list table (`inputs_pending`) — it is the path's turn field and not the registry, and the path's Idle order blocks it (§5-5 「빚이 우편보다 먼저인 이유」). ★The halt after error is different★ — the door that lets mail in is the kernel's `last_end_failed` (「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」 below · the codex classifier also sets that field). The path's `halted` blocks only turns the path opens by itself — the empty turn · the `Held` from before the halt · mail the kernel had already released just before the halt (§5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 · the remaining gap = §5-9).
  - ★**Halt after error — mail's door is one field of the turn observation table (decision · both backends)**★. It halts by the kernel not releasing mail — during the halt, mail is parked in the kernel by name so the ledger stays true (`delivered` = the actual injection time — ADR-0104) and when the agent ends it goes to the next incarnation. claude has no path to hold mail (when the kernel reads idle it goes straight to stdin), and for codex, if we make the path hold it, the kernel does not know the halt and keeps putting mail in — the ledger says `delivered` while the path holds it without a ceiling, so it can be lost on agent exit·restart and block the shared input ceiling (`INPUT_QUEUE_LIMIT = 32` — `transport.rs:266`) (round-6 review · §10-5). ★It does not tell versions apart (both backends)★ — it reads only our side's turn-end fact, and what it blocks is our mail write (the same for an old claude CLI that does not advertise the lifecycle · codex below the floor — only the list and the codex path are today's path, N1 (a) · §5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 · §5-9).
    - **The writing side = that agent's one turn classifier (claude `backend/claude/mod.rs:506` · codex `backend/codex/mod.rs:1164` — ★the `:NNNN` in this line is the edition 8 reading baseline. The present spots = `classify_turn` of the two files · turn table = `turn.rs` `TurnObservations::observe_at` · codex exits = `transport.rs` `end_turn_if`★).** claude — on a failed `result` (`is_error:true` or a `subtype` starting with `error` — today's decision, `backend/claude/mod.rs:806-819`), the decoder emits `Error` right before `MessageDone` (`:827` → `:829`). The classifier reads that `Error` as the 「이 턴 오류」 signal (★implementation reflected — only the one `Error` of a failed `result`, see the line-buffer overflow line below★ · one variant in `TurnSignal` — `in_turn` is not touched), and the turn observation table holds it and folds it at the turn end (`Ended`). codex — a `TurnEnd{outcome: Failed}` attributed to our turn is itself an error end: the translated `turn/completed(failed)` (`backend/codex/decoder.rs:764-772` · including releasing an early exit) and `end_turn_if` (`transport.rs:1228-1262`) of the five exits — the five callers `:1278`·`:1701`·`:2111`·`:2180`·`:2191` (the outcome of this road is always `Failed`). So `Ended` carries the kind of end (clean · error · other) (★landed shape = `TurnSignal::{Progress, Failed, Ended(TurnEndKind::{Clean, Failed, Other})}` · turn table field `TurnObservation.last_end_failed` · kernel `TurnFact.last_end_failed`★). ★**Fold rule (both backends)**★: an error end (for claude, an end that came holding 「턴 오류」) → true · a cleanly successful end (codex `Completed` · for claude, a `result` that came without the signal) → false · any other end (codex `Interrupted`·`Unknown` — an interrupt neither sets nor releases) → as is. ~~The same lock · the same seq rule (it drops a write with a smaller seq — `turn.rs`).~~ ★**Edition 9 — the same lock but the cursors are separate (P1 review · implementation reflected)**: the halt field filters by its own cursor (`last_end_seq` · the `error_seq` of the error held). If it filtered by the progress cursor (`last_seq`), then when the pump receives `Failed` as seq N and another thread's progress (N+1) is written first before it is written to the table, that `Failed` would be dropped and the later `Ended(Clean)` would fold a turn that ended in error as clean (mail goes in with no halt after error). So `Failed` · `Ended` drops only when its seq is smaller than the `Ended` folded last, and `Failed` is held at the earliest seq and then folded by the `Ended` of a larger seq — `in_turn` · `last_signal` stay as they are, with the progress cursor changing only on the newest signal (the doc of `turn.rs` `observe_at` is the source of truth)★. The `TurnEnd{Failed}` (`transport.rs:1582`) of an incarnation whose connection could not stand is also set, but that incarnation ends soon and `forget` erases it.
    - **Release = the next cleanly successful end** covers it with false (codex `turn/completed(completed)` · claude's non-error `result` — in JSON mode today that is in effect `success`, below) — there is no separate release action. Since the kernel does not write mail during the halt, that turn is usually a turn the user's message opened (a message typed while idle · waiting items the vendor ran on · for codex, the path opens only user turns during the halt) — if there is a turn the vendor opens by itself in claude without user input, its end also releases the halt (we do not know whether such a turn exists · accepted — §5-9). If that turn is also an error, it is true again. ★An interrupted end releases on neither backend (ADR-0192 — matched)★ — codex `interrupted` is 「그 밖」, and claude's interrupted `result` (`subtype:"interrupted"`) does not occur in JSON mode today (claude's JSON path has no interrupt — `transport/stdio.rs:371`) · the JSON interrupt feature will carry that end as 「그 밖」 (§5-4 「끊기」). A claude subtype that is not an error and not known releases the halt — it follows the decoder's allow-list (`backend/claude/mod.rs:811-818`) and releasing = mail flows as today (accepted). It is a new item per incarnation (`register`), so it disappears together when the agent ends (the `forget` of `finish` — the two cleanup points of ADR-0127 as they are).
    - **There is no new doorbell** — the moment of release is the turn end, and the turn end already rings `turn_ended` **after** updating the table (`output_core.rs:338-366`). The adapter (`ManagerTurnFacts`) reads this field in the same single query as `in_turn` — an intermediate value such as 「턴은 끝났는데 오류는 아직 안 적혔다」 is not visible.
    - **The trigger in codex is not `Error` but `TurnEnd{Failed}`** — codex's `Error` also carries retry errors inside a turn (`backend/codex/decoder.rs:728` — the classifier ignores it as today). The path's `halted` also stands on the same trigger but blocks only turns the path opens by itself (§5-5) — the one that writes the turn table field is still only the classifier. **The list·session classification does not read this field** — a message typed during the halt is also classified as usual (if idle, Direct — that turn is the turn that will release the halt · §5-4 classification). The disposition of claude list items follows the vendor (PRD §3-10 「억지로 구현하지 마」 · §5-4 「오류로 끝난 턴」).
    - ~~★The same `Error` also arises on line-buffer overflow (`backend/claude/mod.rs:714`)★ — that turn is also counted as an error (the side that auto-injects less · a rare accident where one line exceeds the ceiling). The error decision is not split by string.~~ ★**Edition 9 — reversed (P1 review · implementation reflected)**: a line-buffer overflow `Error` is **not** a turn error — if it occurs outside a turn, the table holds it and folds **the next clean turn** as an error end, and that incarnation's mail halts until the user makes the next turn succeed (the halt has no ceiling). So only the `Error` of a failed `result` is `TurnSignal::Failed` and it is **told apart by the string prefix** (`RESULT_FAILURE_DETAIL` = 「claude stream-json result reported failure」 · `backend/claude/mod.rs` `classify_turn`). The reason it is a prefix and not a field: `Error` is one string, so adding a marker field would change every consumer's match, and the side that makes that string and the side that reads it live in the same folder★.
    - Why we chose it: the writing side is the same classifier as `in_turn`, so **no second writing side arises**, and the release is the same moment as the turn end, so **doorbells do not increase**. Alternatives rejected: add it to the waiting list table (a second writing side different from the registry reduction · a third doorbell on release — the same reason we did not add the debt, §5-5) · the kernel receives turn outcomes and holds them as its own state (the `StatusSink` verbs grow and the kernel doubles as the fact layer — ADR-0110 「포트는 얇게」) · the session rejects mail writes (there is no event to wake rejected mail again) · block only codex with a path field (before round 6 of the edition 8 review — the kernel does not know the halt and the ledger becomes false, above · §10-5).

### 5-1. State machine (one sent input)

id = the uuid the session mints when sending — today's `msg_uuid` that `write_input_observed` mints, as it is (claude `uuid` · codex `clientUserMessageId`).

```
            ┌─ 한가(도는 턴 없음 ∧ 목록 빔 · 이어받기 중 아님) ──▶ Direct (목록 없음 · 즉시 말풍선) ▣ ──(백엔드 거절)──▶ 말풍선을 지운다
보냄(User)─분류┤
            └─ 턴 중 / 목록 있음 / 연결 중 ─▶ Queued ──(벤더의 받음)──────────────────────▶ Delivered ▣ (대화 끝 말풍선)
                                               │
                                               ├──✕(codex · 통로가 쥔 항목)──────────────────────────────▶ Cancelled ▣
                                               ├──✕(claude · codex 넘긴 항목)───▶ Cancelling ──▶ Cancelled ▣ / Delivered ▣(늦음) / Discarded ▣
                                               ├──(끊기 · 에이전트 종료 · 거절 · claude 벤더 cancelled)──▶ Discarded ▣ (버림)
                                               └──(codex 수락 모름)──▶ Queued 그대로 — 나가는 길 = 늦은 받음 · ✕(곧바로 Discarded) · 에이전트 종료 (§5-5)
보냄(Mail) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶ 오늘 경로(목록 없음 · 사용자 목록이 빌 때까지 기다림 — §5-0)

원인이 Unknown 인 묘비 ──(그 뒤 벤더의 받음)──▶ Delivered  (되살림 — 정의 한 벌 = §5-2 「되살림」)
Delivered 묘비 ──(그 뒤 백엔드 거절)──▶ Discarded{Rejected} · 그린 말풍선을 지운다  (받음 뒤 거절 — §5-2)
(종결을 뒤집는 전이는 이 둘뿐이다)
```

| State | List (windows) | ✕ | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Direct` | None — a bubble at the end of the conversation at once | — | A message sent while idle (PRD §3-5). If the backend rejects it (`Dropped{Rejected}`), that bubble is erased without a notification (§5-2) |
| `Queued` | Visible | Present — both backends · in any segment (pressing it removes it from the list) | claude = in the vendor queue (it was already written to stdin) · codex = held by us (tool segment · answer segment (M16 green) · connecting · waiting for the turn id · settlement · halt after error · a turn where steer was rejected — §5-5 segment table) or handed over to the vendor (steer · `turn/start`) — even if it was handed over and its acceptance became unknown, it stays here (no event — it leaves only by late receipt · ✕ · agent end, user decision N10 · §5-5 「수락 모름」 — messages typed after it are handed over in order as usual (user decision N13 (b)) and only mail waits · the LLM list row is `unconfirmed` — §5-6). The difference between held and handed over is path-internal state and is not shown in the list — it only splits the outcome of ✕ (a held item = cancelled at once · a handed-over item = cancel-pending → a bubble if received late · an acceptance-unknown item = discarded at once, §5-5) |
| `Cancelling` | ★**Not drawn — in every window**★ (only the LLM list `cancelling` — §5-6) | None | A cancel request was received and we await the outcome — claude = collects the vendor cancel response·lifecycle (it stays here even if the cancel request itself fails — the outcome is the lifecycle · if the response is `true` it is cancelled at once, §5-2 · §5-4) · codex = the ✕ of an already handed-over item (the path decides the outcome — if its acceptance becomes unknown, discarded at once · the ✕ of an item that is already acceptance-unknown does not stay here: it chains the three events at once, user decision N12 (a) · §5-5). ✕ is not a hiding only in the window that pressed it but a request that changes the state of the daemon registry, so every window stops drawing it from the same reduction result (user decision — PRD §3-6) |
| `Delivered` · `Cancelled` · `Discarded` | Removed | — | Terminal. ★None of them returns to the input box★ (PRD §3-7). ★There is no notification★ (PRD §3-7) — the cause `Discarded` holds is material for logs·tests. If a rejection (`Dropped{Rejected}`) comes after `Delivered`, that bubble is erased and the item is rewritten and closed as `Discarded{Rejected}` (§5-2) |

- **Codex's hold/hand-over is not shown in the list (decision)** — no new list state, no field, no re-emission. One exception — the LLM list row carries an acceptance-unknown item as `unconfirmed` (it is a marker the path is asked at the moment of the query, not a registry state or a ring event · the window draws it as an ordinary gray item — §5-6). Since ✕ is always there, there is no reason for the list to know hold/hand-over, and only the outcome of a pressed ✕ is decided by the path (`withdraw` — §5-5).

**Owner of transitions** (who puts that event on the ring):

| Transition | claude (`SessionClassified`) | codex (`TransportOwned`) |
|---|---|---|
| Direct/Queued classification | **Session** — inside the input lock (§5-0) · reads the waiting list table → the core turn observation (in this order) + receipt-possibility (§5-4) | **Path** — under the state lock (ADR-0193) · hold or hand over = the decision of the hand-over policy (§5-5 — not shown in the list) |
| → Delivered | decoder `started` · the reducer's `AckUnavailable` handling · the core rewriting a `Queued` that stood late after the decision (§5-4 — N1 (a) user decision) — ★both reach only items whose write succeeded (§5-4)★ | decoder `clientId` echo (including the late echo of an acceptance-unknown item — an item that was standing in the list, §5-5) · the late echo of an item that was ✕-ed and then closed as acceptance unknown (revival — only the bubble · no turn to answer is opened, §5-5) |
| Queued → Cancelling | Session (cancel command — inside the input lock) | path `withdraw` — the ✕ of an already handed-over item (`TooLate` → `CancelRequested` + `CancelAnswered{removed:false}` — §5-5) · the ✕ of an acceptance-unknown item chains `Dropped{Unknown}` right onto it (it does not stay — N12 (a)) |
| → Cancelled | our cancel response `cancelled:true` (the vendor removed it from its own queue — it does not wait for the decoder's lifecycle `cancelled` · even if that line comes first, the same outcome, §5-2) | path `withdraw` — a held item (`Dropped{Withdrawn}` · no vendor call) · the vendor turned out not to have accepted an item that was ✕-ed after hand-over (steer error response → `Dropped{Withdrawn}`) |
| → Discarded | decoder `cancelled` (from Queued · or from Cancelling when our cancel response is `false`·an error — closed by a failed turn)·`discarded`·`refused` · session (write failure) · core termination synthesis · core seal (§5-3) | path (interrupt · connection end · `turn/start`·steer write failure and `turn/start` rejection = `Rejected` · the acceptance-unknown of a ✕-ed item = `Unknown` — §5-5) · core termination synthesis · core seal. ★The acceptance-unknown of an item that was not ✕-ed (`turn/start` response deadline·decode failure·long turn id · settlement deadline · a success response with no echo at turn end) is not discarded — it stays in the list (user decision N10 · §5-5 「수락 모름」)★. ★The held items of a halt after error are not discarded — they wait (PRD §3-10 · §5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」)★ |

### 5-2. Ring event vocabulary (backend-neutral)

One variant in the agent `OutputEvent`, one mirror in the wire `StructuredEvent` (decision: we do not use the `Structured{kind}` escape hatch — that field is for per-backend originals, and the claude turn classifier counts `Structured` as a progress signal — `backend/claude/mod.rs:506`).

```rust
// agent types.rs — ADR-NNNN
pub enum QueuedInputEvent {
    Queued { id: String, text: String },          // 목록에 오른다 — id 하나에 한 번(재방출 없음)
    CancelRequested { id: String },               // 취소 요청 접수 — 결말 대기(claude 전부 · codex 는 이미 넘긴 항목의 ✕)
    CancelAnswered { id: String, removed: bool }, // 취소 요청의 성공 응답 — claude = `response.response.cancelled` 값 · codex 넘긴 항목의 ✕ = 늘 false. `false` 는 결말이 아니라 `cancelled` 의 원인 귀속 재료 · `true` 는 곧바로 결말 (가)(아래 표)
    CancelFailed { id: String },                  // 취소 요청 자체가 실패(오류 응답 · 취소 줄 쓰기 실패 — claude) — 되돌리지 않는다: 취소 대기 그대로 「못 뺐다」, 결말은 수명주기
    Delivered { id: String },                     // 벤더가 턴에 넣었다 → 목록에서 빠지고 대화 끝 말풍선
    Dropped { id: String, cause: DropCause },     // 전달 안 됨 — 해석은 아래 환원 규칙 · `Rejected` 는 그 id 로 그린 말풍선도 지운다(아래)
    AckUnavailable { delivered: Vec<DeliveredCopy> }, // 이 화신은 항목별 받음을 못 준다(§5-4 — 남은 항목을 그 자리 말풍선으로) · `delivered` = 이 사건이 받음으로 닫는 항목의 본문 사본 — 디코더는 비워 내고 코어가 채운다(아래 「`Queued` 가 링에서 밀려난 뒤의 받음」)
}
pub enum DropCause { Withdrawn, Interrupted, AgentEnded, Rejected, Unknown }
pub struct DeliveredCopy { id: String, text: String }        // `AckUnavailable` 이 싣는 말풍선 사본
```

- **`Queued` has no ✕-flag field — ✕ is on every item of the list** (user decision 2026-09-25 · §0). The `Queued` of the same id comes only once and there is no re-emission (a second `Queued` is a no-op or is discarded as in the table below — the second defense). A Direct item does not go on the list, so it has no ✕. If you meet edition 1's vocabulary: there is no `Released` (§5-1) · `Returned` → `Discarded` (the meaning changed from 「입력창으로」 to 「버림」). There is also no waiting-reason field (`QueueReason{MidTurn, Connecting}`) — the list header line that read it is gone (PRD §3-2 · §10-5). The ✕-flag field (`cancellable`) and its re-emission were also withdrawn (§10-5).
- ★**Cancel-pending (`Cancelling`) is drawn in no window** (user decision — PRD §3-6)★. ✕ is not a hiding only in the window that pressed it — the cancel command changes the state of the daemon registry (claude `CancelRequested` · for codex a held item goes straight to `Dropped{Withdrawn}` · a handed-over item to `CancelRequested`), and every window removes that item from drawing by the same reduction result. So there is no hiding state a window holds separately, and the result is the same even after an app restart (ring replay · re-attach reconciliation — §5-7). Only the LLM list (§5-6) sees `cancelling` through to the outcome. If the outcome is (b), a bubble at the end of the conversation stands in every window · if (a)·(c), it does not appear again.
- **The ✕ of an item already handed over in codex does not add vocabulary (decision)** — the path chains `CancelAnswered{removed:false}` right onto `CancelRequested` (§5-5 — the same whether it was handed over an instant ago or long ago). In the list it leaves at once as `CancelRequested`. Then the table below splits the outcome through claude's 「못 뺐다」 branch: echo = `Delivered` (b) · the vendor turned out not to have accepted it and the path withdraws = `Dropped{Withdrawn}` → `Cancelled` (a) · unknown = `Dropped{Unknown}` → `Discarded{Unknown}` (c — revival possible · at the very moment acceptance becomes unknown: since ✕ is already the reason to remove it from the list, we wait no longer, §5-5 「✕ 한 항목의 수락 모름」 · user decision N12 (a)). The ✕ of an item that is already acceptance-unknown chains the same three events at once — it also leaves the LLM list at once.

**Reduction rule (shared by registry·accumulator — one set in two languages · state-based)**

The place of one id is one of three — **item** (non-terminal: `Queued` · `Cancelling{응답, 벤더 닫음}`) · **tombstone** (terminal — no body, only the id and one 「되살림 가능」 mark remain, spec below) · **unknown** (neither). The two fields of `Cancelling`: `응답` ∈ {none · could not remove} (the result of our cancel request — a success response `false` · if the request itself failed (`CancelFailed`) it is could-not-remove · removed (`true`) does not stay in the field: that response closes it at once as `Cancelled`, table below) · `벤더 닫음` ∈ {no · yes} (we have already seen that id's vendor `cancelled`). One incarnation field of the reducer = the **「받음 불가 판명」 marker** (we saw `AckUnavailable`) — the material by which the core rewrites a `Queued` that stood late after the decision (§5-3). ★There is no reduction row that reads the marker★ — at the moment of determination, all remaining items are closed as terminal (the two `AckUnavailable` rows below), and an item that stands after that has already been rewritten by the core as `Queued`+`AckUnavailable` (a copy of that one item — §5-3). So only the registry holds the marker, and the accumulator receives the rewritten lines as two ordinary events.

| Current place | Event | Result |
|---|---|---|
| unknown | `Queued` | goes on the list (a `Queued` that stood **after** the cannot-receive decision was already rewritten by the core — §5-3) |
| unknown | `Delivered` · `Dropped` | leave **only a tombstone** (so that a late `Queued` does not make a permanent item). If the cause is `Unknown` (`Dropped{Unknown}`), revival is possible (definition = the tombstone spec below) |
| unknown | other | discard |
| tombstone | `Queued` | discard |
| tombstone (revival possible) | `Delivered` | **`Delivered`** (a vendor echo came late to an item discarded by 「모름」 · clear the revival mark). The bubble is drawn by the vendor echo that follows (§5-5 · §5-7 · there is no notification — PRD §3-7) |
| tombstone | `Dropped{Rejected}` | tombstone as is · clear the revival mark — rewrite the outcome as `Discarded{Rejected}` (**rejection after receipt** — `refused` also comes as a terminal after `started`, §3-1). The accumulator, regardless of the reduction, erases the bubble drawn with that id (below · §5-7) |
| tombstone | other | discard — the second `Delivered` of the same id ~~(the codex decoder emits from both `item/started`·`item/completed`)~~ (★edition 9 — the codex decoder emits once · twice only when the item id exceeds the ceiling★) also ends here |
| `Queued` · `Cancelling` | `Queued` (same id) | no-op — the producer emits `Queued` only once per id (no re-emission · second defense) |
| `Queued` | `CancelRequested` | `Cancelling{없음, 아니오}` |
| `Cancelling` | `CancelRequested` (same id) | no-op — the producer emits `CancelRequested` only once per id (claude = the registry check inside the input lock · codex = the path's `cancel_asked` — §5-5 「✕」 · second defense) |
| `Queued` | `Delivered` | `Delivered` |
| `Queued` | `Dropped{Withdrawn}` | `Cancelled` |
| `Queued` | `Dropped{그 밖}` | `Discarded{cause}` |
| `Cancelling` (the two fields irrelevant) | `CancelAnswered{removed:true}` | `Cancelled` (outcome (a)). `true` = the vendor removed that message from its own queue (§3-1 handler decision ②) — it cannot be drained again, so no `started` can come afterward. Whether we have already seen the lifecycle `cancelled` (`예` — the normal order: it precedes the response in the same ms, M3 ⓐ) or not yet (`아니오`), we close here — if we waited for that line, on the day it never comes an item that left every window with ✕ would stay holding `inputs_pending` so mail is blocked without a ceiling (N8), and a message sent while idle would all pass through the list (loss of PRD AC1) — and the cause is not visible in any window. A late lifecycle `cancelled` is a no-revival tombstone and is discarded |
| `Cancelling{_, 아니오}` | `CancelAnswered{removed:false}` · `CancelFailed` | `응답` field = could not remove. ★`CancelFailed` also does not return it to `Queued`★ — if returned, an item that left every window with ✕ would be drawn again (PRD §3-6 「다시 나타나지 않는다」 · AC13). The outcome is decided by the rows below through the lifecycle (§5-4) |
| `Cancelling{_, 예}` | `CancelAnswered{removed:false}` · `CancelFailed` | `Discarded{Unknown}` (outcome (c) — the vendor closed it but it is not our cancel) |
| `Cancelling{못 뺐다, 아니오}` | `Dropped{Unknown}` | `Discarded{Unknown}` (outcome (c) — a failed·interrupted turn closed it) |
| `Cancelling{없음, 아니오}` | `Dropped{Unknown}` | `Cancelling{없음, 예}` — leave it as cancel-pending in the list and wait for the response (if our cancel removed it, the response **follows in the same ms** — M3 ⓐ) |
| `Cancelling` | `Delivered` | `Delivered` (a cancel too late — PRD §3-6 outcome (b)) |
| `Cancelling` | `Dropped{Withdrawn}` | `Cancelled` (outcome (a) — codex: the vendor turned out not to have accepted an item that was ✕-ed after hand-over, and the path withdrew it, §5-5 · irrelevant to the `응답` field) |
| `Cancelling` | `Dropped{Interrupted · AgentEnded · Rejected}` | `Discarded{cause}` (outcome (c) — interrupt·agent end·rejection·seal) |
| all `Queued` items | `AckUnavailable` | all `Delivered` — a bubble at that ring place (user decision N1 (a) — the vendor already received it · only the place is the moment of determination). The body of the bubble is a copy carried in that event — the core fills in the (id, body) of the items this reduction closes as `Delivered` (this row and the cancel-pending row below) (「`Queued` 가 링에서 밀려난 뒤의 받음」 below). If the id in the copy is **unknown** (an accumulator whose ring window lost that `Queued`) it is the same as the `Delivered` of an unknown id — only a tombstone (the registry fills the copy with its own items, so it never meets this branch). Sets the **「받음 불가 판명」 marker** (the material by which the core rewrites a late `Queued` — §5-3 · if already set, no-op). ★`Delivered` reaches only items whose write succeeded (§5-4)★ |
| `Cancelling{응답, _}` | `AckUnavailable` | In this incarnation the vendor `cancelled` does not come, so **our cancel response that has come by then is the outcome** — if it was removed (`true`), it was already closed as `Cancelled` at the moment of the response, so the remaining cancel-pending are only `못 뺐다`·`없음` and → `Delivered` (N1 (a) — the vendor already received it · the copy is carried together with the row above). ★`없음` also does not wait for the response★ — an old CLI can silently drop a cancel line, and if we waited, that item would remain until the agent ends and block mail (no ceiling — N8). A response that comes late after determination is a terminal tombstone and is discarded (after determination no cancel-pending remains in the list). `벤더 닫음 = 예` does not exist at this time (if the lifecycle line had been seen, it would be `Available`) |
| (when an item reaches terminal) | — | erase the item and **fold it into a tombstone** (we do not hold the body for the whole incarnation) |

- **The rule stands on current state and not history** — since it does not retrace 「앞에 `CancelRequested` 가 있었나」, the golden sequence stands deterministically without history search. The two fields of `Cancelling` are also state.
- ★**Cause attribution of `cancelled`**★: a vendor `cancelled` coming from `Cancelling` cannot be closed unconditionally as 「취소됨」 — `cancelled` also comes when a failed turn closes the waiting items (§3-1) — and if that closing happens **before** our cancel line is read, our cancel answers `cancelled:false`. So the outcome is still decided by the **lifecycle** (`cancelled` / `started`), and our cancel response only tells apart whether that `cancelled` is **ours** — if removed, 「취소됨」 (a), if not, 「버려짐」 (c). The rule of not using the response value as the outcome (§3-1 · §10-5) stays as it is for `false`·errors: `false` is still not 「전달됨」 (if `started` comes later, (b)). ★The exception is `true` alone — it is outcome (a) by itself★: it is an answer given after the handler removed that message from the queue and emitted the lifecycle `cancelled` (§3-1 decision ②), so that `cancelled` is certainly ours and that message cannot be drained again. So even if the lifecycle line has not come yet, we close without waiting (the `CancelAnswered{removed:true}` row of the table above — if we waited, it would become a blockage with an invisible cause on the day that line does not come). The cause label is `Unknown` — claude's handling of a turn that ended in error follows the vendor (PRD §3-10 · user decision Q8), so we do not set a failed-turn-only cause separately (M12 only checks whether that branch closes according to this attribution). If the response never comes (agent end), the termination synthesis `Dropped{AgentEnded}` closes it with `Discarded`.
- **Tombstone spec (the same in both languages, decision):**
  - **Folding:** an item that reaches terminal is erased and folded into a tombstone — the body·cause were already written by the event handling of that moment (the accumulator's bubble), so we do not keep them. Tombstone = id + one 「되살림 가능」 mark.
  - ★**Revival definition — one set**★: **revival possible ⇔ the terminal cause of that id is `Unknown`** — whether an item was closed by `Discarded{Unknown}` (whichever event closed it: claude vendor `cancelled` · cancel response `false`·error · for codex the acceptance-unknown of a ✕-ed item) or `Dropped{Unknown}` came for an unknown id. Rust·TS use the same one line (`resurrectable = cause == Unknown`). The reason we fixed it by cause: the ground for revival (「벤더가 준 사실이 모름을 이긴다」) is independent of which event made it unknown. A `Delivered` that comes to a no-revival tombstone (cause `Withdrawn`·`Interrupted`·`AgentEnded`·`Rejected`, outcome `Delivered`) is discarded. ★The place where revival really works is one: the acceptance-unknown of a ✕-ed item in codex★ (it only fixes the list outcome and the bubble — no turn to answer is opened: that message is seen by the next user turn in the history, §5-5) — the acceptance-unknown of an item that was not ✕-ed stays in the list with no event (§5-5) and the late receipt is an ordinary `Queued` → `Delivered`, and after claude's vendor `cancelled` no `started` of the same uuid comes (the vendor removed it from the queue — §3-1). We leave the definition as the one cause line — it does no harm even if it reaches claude's side, and if we split by the closing event the definitions of the two languages would diverge again (§10-5).
  - **No duplicates:** the tombstone is a set — making a tombstone again of an id that already exists is a **no-op** and does not move its FIFO place either. Normal flows where several events come to the same id: ~~the codex decoder's `Delivered` twice (`item/started`·`item/completed`)~~ ★edition 9 — the codex decoder emits `Delivered` **once** per item (whichever of `item/started`·`item/completed` comes first · the second drops out by item id check — `CodexAppServerDecoder::user_message` · implementation reflected). Coming twice is only the defect branch where the item id exceeds the ceiling and cannot be paired, and the tombstone takes it★ · the `started`·echo of a message sent while idle·of mail (an unknown id so only a tombstone remains — mail also carries a uuid·`clientId` on both backends).
  - **Ceiling and eviction:** **1024** per incarnation, and when it overflows, tombstones are discarded starting from **the one that went in first** (FIFO). The constant is placed next to the reducer (agent crate) and the same value is written at the head of the golden file — TS has a test that checks that value against its own constant.
  - **Golden boundary sequences:** 1024 tombstones + 1 → the oldest tombstone drops out · the `Queued` of the id that dropped **goes on** the list and the `Queued` of the second-oldest id is discarded · two `Delivered` of the same id are one tombstone · `Delivered` on a revival-possible tombstone · ★`Queued`→`CancelRequested`→`Dropped{Unknown}`→`CancelAnswered{false}`→`Delivered` = `Delivered` (a `Unknown` closed by the cancel response is also revived) · `Queued`→`Dropped{Rejected}`→`Delivered` = tombstone as is · `Queued`→`Delivered`→`Dropped{Rejected}` = tombstone (no revival — outcome `Discarded{Rejected}` · rejection after receipt) · `Queued`→`CancelRequested`→`CancelFailed`→`Delivered` = `Delivered` (even if the cancel request fails we do not return it — it does not stand on the list again in between) · `Queued`→`CancelRequested`→`CancelAnswered{true}` = `Cancelled` (even if the lifecycle line never comes — if it comes late the tombstone discards it) · a `AckUnavailable` carrying an unknown id = a tombstone of that id (list unchanged)★ · codex: `Queued`→`CancelRequested`→`CancelAnswered{false}`→`Delivered` = `Delivered` (the ✕ of a handed-over item — outcome (b)) · `Queued`→`CancelRequested`→`CancelAnswered{false}`→`Dropped{Withdrawn}` = `Cancelled` · `Queued`→`CancelRequested`→`CancelAnswered{false}`→`Dropped{Unknown}`→`Delivered` = `Delivered` (revival — a late echo after a ✕-ed item was closed as acceptance unknown) · `Queued`→`Queued` (same id) = one item (the second is a no-op).
  - ★**Even if the tombstone sets of the registry (whole incarnation) and the accumulator (ring window) differ, the result does not diverge**★ — the ground is not 「같은 규칙이 같은 결과」 (if the sets differ, that alone does not hold) but reachability of order: the order in which a tombstone actually blocks something (the `Delivered`/`Dropped` of the same id standing on the ring **before** `Queued`) **cannot be reached** given the producer-side order — claude emits `Queued` before `send_input` inside the input lock (§5-0), codex has no hand-over before announce and chains the disposition after `Queued` by the death mark (§5-5), and after termination the seal, and after the cannot-receive decision the core's rewriting, stand in for `Queued` (§5-3 · §5-4). ★One exception★: while claude's receipt-possibility is `Unknown`, `Queued` is emitted **after** the write (§5-4), so the order in which the vendor `started` stands first is rarely reachable — the results on the two sides are the same (tombstone → discard the late `Queued` → it does not go on the list and the echo-back draws the bubble · since that id is not waiting, it is not suppressed). The order in the same window where only the echo-back overtakes `Queued` and `started` stands after it (echo-back → `Queued` → `Delivered`) is not blocked by the tombstone — the reduction result is the same on both sides (it goes on the list and leaves as `Delivered`), and the accumulator's drawing prevents the bubble from becoming two copies (§5-7 「이미 그린 uuid」). The premise itself that the two sides receive **the same order** stands on §5-7 「라이브 배달 순서」. So the tombstone is a second defense against producer defects, and in a normal ring the difference between the two sides' tombstone sets does not reach the result. The revival rule is the same — an accumulator whose revival-possible tombstone was pushed out of the ring window receives that `Delivered` as an unknown id and leaves only a tombstone, and the bubble is drawn by the vendor echo either way.
- ★**The turn signal is only claude `Delivered`**★ (ADR-0113/0127): the claude classifier counts `QueuedInput::Delivered` as **progress (Progress)** — that event is a 1:1 translation of the vendor `command_lifecycle` `started` line, so 「벤더가 이 입력을 지금 턴에서 돌리기 시작했다」 is **a fact of vendor output** (ground·what we gain = §5-4). All other events and all codex events are `None` — if `Dropped`·codex `Delivered` comes **after** the turn end and turns 「턴 중」 on again, that incarnation has mail blocked until the 30-minute fail-open valve (codex's `Delivered` also comes after the turn end — the late echo of an acceptance-unknown item · revival, §5-5). ★The two counterparts of the same danger are outside this vocabulary and blocked separately★ — the bubble the path synthesized (Direct echo) is emitted without turn observation (§5-5), and the vendor echo that comes attached to that `Delivered` (`userMessage` — the codex classifier counts it as progress) is emitted as `emit_without_turn_observation` by today's path-reader attribution gate if it is a line outside our open turn (`claims_our_turn` — `transport.rs:2039-2084`, written in `:2250-2260` · §5-5 「벤더가 보낸 에코」). An event the core **rewrote** (the seal's `Dropped` · the `AckUnavailable` chained to `Queued` after the cannot-receive decision) does not pass through the classifier (§5-3 · §5-4). Pin it in the classifier test.
- The ring weight (`estimate_cost_bytes`) counts `text`.
- **The bubble body is our local copy** (ADR-0198 — the vendor may normalize). `Delivered` carries no body — the accumulator draws it with the body of `Queued` (an item that lost `Queued` through re-attach and is **still waiting** gets its body from the row of the §5-7 reconciliation).
- ★**Receipt after `Queued` has been pushed off the ring — the bubble is redrawn by a ring event (decision)**★. If the ring window loses `Queued` (ring ceiling — §5-7 re-attach reconciliation), the accumulator does not know that id and has no body to draw from that id's receipt — if the receipt is **before** the snapshot, it is terminal so it is not in the snapshot rows, and if **after**, the re-reduction of the reconciliation only fixes the list state (§5-7). So it splits by whether, with each receipt, **a line that redraws the bubble also stands on the ring**.
  - **Receipts that are redrawn:** a receipt that a vendor echo precedes or follows (claude `started` — echo-back, M2 · codex `clientId` echo · revival) draws the bubble from that line even without `Queued` (it is an unknown id so it is not suppressed — §5-7). If the ring head cuts between that line and the receipt (the echo-back of the fold path is 1 ms before `started` — M2), that bubble is cut together with the earlier history — the same grade as today's overflow disposition.
  - **Receipts drawn only with our copy = the one claude cannot-receive determination:** items that `AckUnavailable` closes (`Queued` · cancel-pending could-not-remove·none — table above) and items the core rewrote after the decision (§5-3). We do not lean on the echo-back of an old CLI (we did not check).
  - **How to close = the closing event carries the copy:** inside the very replay lock in which the core reduces `AckUnavailable`, it fills the `delivered` of the event it writes to the ring with the (id, body) of the items that reduction closes as `Delivered` (the decoder emits it empty — the same place as the rewriting in §5-3). The post-decision rewriting also chains, after `Queued`, a `AckUnavailable` carrying a copy of that one item (§5-3). The accumulator draws a bubble at that ring place for each copy — if that uuid has already been drawn, it does not draw (§5-7). So that bubble stands again by one ring event, and the re-attach reconciliation is today's rule of only matching list state. The ring weight also counts the body of the copy. Alternatives rejected = §10-5 (recent receipt rows in the snapshot · a body field in `Delivered`).
- ★**Rejection of a message sent while idle (Direct) = `Dropped{Rejected}` — that bubble is erased without a notification** (PRD §3-5 · user decision Q1 ④)★.
  - **We do not add vocabulary (decision)** — claude's rejection is the lifecycle `refused` and is translated by the stateless decoder, so the decoder does not know whether that id is a message sent while idle or a list item. We keep the event as one and the receiving side tells the meaning apart. codex's rejection = the write failure·error response of a Direct `turn/start` (§5-5 five exits).
  - The **registry** leaves only a tombstone because it is an unknown id (or a tombstone that the `started` of that message already left) (if a tombstone, it rewrites the outcome as `Discarded{Rejected}` — table above) — the list does not change.
  - The **accumulator**, **regardless of** reduction state, erases the user bubble drawn with that id if there is one (drawing rule — §5-7). Since ring replay is deterministic, every window·app restart erases it the same way. ★The same for an item that passed through the list★ — if rejected before receipt, there is no bubble yet so it is a no-op, and if rejected **after receipt** (`Queued`→`Delivered`→`Dropped{Rejected}` — in the vendor lifecycle `refused` also comes as a terminal after `started`, §3-1) it erases the bubble `Delivered` drew (user principle 「거절하면 우리쪽 채팅도 지워져야」 — Q1 ④). A rejection after receipt did not come in M13 (Phase 0b — slash commands mid-turn 6/6 · `refused` 0) — the failed-turn side is written down by M12.
  - **Only a rejection is erased** — if a message sent while idle meets an interrupt·agent end·failed turn, the bubble stays (what the PRD decided is only rejection · it stays today too · on the same side as 「이미 말풍선이 된 답 없는 글은 그대로」 (PRD §3-10)). The interrupt·agent end side also leaves it (user confirmation N9 — 「그냥 별도로 뭐 해야되는게 있음? resume 하면 어차피 갱신될텐데」). We did not check whether the bubble of a message the vendor did not record drops out on a resume reload.
  - **Does that bubble stay today (PRD R9 — source reading):** claude's write failure does not draw a bubble today either (the synthetic echo is after a successful write — `session.rs:230`·`:241`) · the vendor `refused` leaves the bubble today because the decoder discards the lifecycle (§1-1 — the branch where `refused` actually comes for a message sent while idle is unconfirmed) · codex has no synthetic echo today (ADR-0198 not implemented), so the bubble of a rejected message does not stand in the first place. So what is newly erased arises from claude `refused` and from the codex synthetic echo this feature brings in.
- **A turn that ended in error (PRD §3-10 · user decision Q8) does not add vocabulary** — codex leaves the held items in the list with no event (§5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」), and claude is the translation of the vendor lifecycle as it is (if the vendor discards it, `Dropped` · if it puts it into the conversation, `Delivered`). We do not put a mark on a message that already became a bubble in that turn. ★The halt after error is also not vocabulary★ — mail = the turn observation table field `last_end_failed` (both backends · §5-0) · codex automatic turns = the path field `halted` (§5-5).
- **The home of the golden sequence file = the agent crate, next to the reducer** (the side that has the reducer owns it). The TS test reads that file by path (the protocol crate has nothing to do with this file — neither side increases a dependency). ★Implementation reflected — file = `crates/engram-dashboard-agent/src/queued_input_golden.json` (`tombstone_cap` 1024 · event seq = sequence number + 1) · TS reads it with `src/components/slot/testing/queuedInputGolden.ts`. The first landing filled the fields the table left empty and the golden pinned them: a `CancelAnswered`/`CancelFailed` that comes to `Queued` without a request → no-op · `Cancelling{Unanswered, vendor_closed}` + a second `Dropped{Unknown}` → no-op · a `Dropped{Withdrawn}` of an unknown id → tombstone · outcome `Cancelled` · tombstone + `Dropped{Rejected}` → always `Discarded:Rejected` · a `AckUnavailable` copy is applied with the meaning `Delivered` after closing the open item (among copies, a revival-possible tombstone is revived). The TS reducer does not know seq — the seq of the re-attach reconciliation is held by the accumulator (§5-7)★.

### 5-3. Authority registry — `QueuedInputs` (decision)

- **A new module** `crates/engram-dashboard-agent/src/queued_input.rs` — one per incarnation, a pure reducer + `Mutex<Registry>` (items + tombstones). ~~Owned by the session and plugged into the core **as an observer**~~ (the same shape as the precedent of injecting the turn observation table `TurnObservations` into the core). ★Implementation reflected — **the core holds `Arc` and the session reaches it through the core**: the manager plugs in the registry and the waiting list table together through `OutputCore::with_queued(QueuedWiring{registry, pending})`, and `AgentSession::queued_inputs` is delegated to the core (no session builder — by construction there is one `Arc`). A core built without `with_queued` uses a private registry and has no table writes·doorbell (test bench). If the registry lock is poisoned, it continues with `into_inner` (differently from the replay lock's `expect` — decision)★.
- **The update is at one place, `OutputCore::emit_inner`, inside the replay lock** (edition 8 source reading `output_core.rs:203` · `:310` · `:328-336` — ★implementation reflected: list events go from `emit_inner` through `emit_list_event` → `record_list_event` (inside the replay lock — seal → rewriting after the decision → filling the body copy → reduction → 「찼다」) · the cancel record goes the same road in one replay span through `emit_cancel_request` · list events do not enter `seed` and `emit_batch_without_turn_observation` (`debug_assert`)★) — so registry state = the reduction value of the ring prefix does not diverge. We do not make another road that changes the registry (the session's cancel·classification also only **emit events**).
- **Lock order: `input_order → replay → 명부(queued_inputs) → 대기 목록 표(잎)`** (§5-0 · the registry reducer calls nothing outside, and what it takes under the registry is only the waiting list table — because 「찼다」 is written inside the replay lock (§5-0 writing order ①) · ★edition 9 — edition 8 was `input_order → replay → queued_inputs` (the registry was a leaf)★). The reading side (LLM list · cancel validation) takes only `queued_inputs` — the registry also writes the seq of the last list event it reduced, so a list query gives it together with the rows in one go (snapshot seq — §5-6). ★The 「목록이 비었나」 of the claude classification reads the waiting list table and not the registry (§5-4)★ — the registry empties inside the replay lock but that emit's progress is written to turn observation after the lock is released, so if it read the registry, both facts would be 「한가」 in between. The session does not emit while holding `queued_inputs`. Add one line to ADR-0006.
- **A reduction in which the registry changes between empty↔full** is written to the waiting list table (the 「사용자 목록이 비지 않았다」 source of mail's busy decision), and the flush doorbell is rung the moment it empties — 「찼다」 inside the same replay lock, 「비었다」 and the doorbell after the turn observation update (definition·shape of the table·doorbell·writing order = §5-0 「우편의 한가」). The registry only writes the table and does not decide (the decision is the kernel's policy — ADR-0110).
- **Incarnation termination synthesis:** `OutputCore::finish` emits `Dropped{AgentEnded}` for each remaining non-terminal id **before** the terminal-point transition (our version of 「호스트가 미종결 uuid 를 `discarded` 로 합성하라」 in the claude description) → `Discarded` (PRD §3-4 「받음이 끝내 안 오면 에이전트가 끝날 때 버린다」). It does not add a turn-observation cleanup point — it is a door that does not write observation (ADR-0127).
  - ★**Synthesis and seal are one unit**★: take the replay lock **once** and inside it ① sweep the registry's non-terminal ids ② issue a seq to `Dropped{AgentEnded}` for each id and put it on the ring ③ reduce into the registry ④ set the 「봉인됨」 mark → release the lock and fan out all at once (the same shape as `emit_batch_without_turn_observation` — `output_core.rs:253`). A single emit takes the lock separately per call, so a `Queued` that stands between the sweep and the seal could slip past both (§10-5).
- ★**Seal (a late-intruding `Queued`)**★: a `Queued` that comes to `emit_inner` after the 「봉인됨」 mark set inside the unit above is written on the ring as **`Dropped{id, AgentEnded}`** instead of `Queued` (→ tombstone). What it blocks: the race in which a `Queued` emitted by the session·path **after** the synthesis swept the list remains as a permanent item — we close, on the list side, a gap of the same shape as the one ADR-0127's finalize recheck closed in turn observation.
- ★**`Queued` after the cannot-receive decision — a second rewriting of the same shape**★: when the registry reduces `AckUnavailable` it sets the 「받음 불가 판명」 marker (§5-2 table). A `Queued` that comes to `emit_inner` after that is rewritten by the core **inside the same replay lock** — it writes `Queued` and **right away chains a `AckUnavailable` carrying a copy of that one item** (a bubble in that place — the same result as today's 「보낸 자리 말풍선」 · user decision N1 (a) 「옛 버전은 오늘 동작」). The reduction is the same as chaining `Delivered{id}` — after the determination there is no non-terminal item other than that one (the determination closed everything, and what stands afterward is closed right away in the same lock). The reason for using this event instead of `Delivered` = even if the ring ceiling cuts between the two lines, the closing line holds the body copy (§5-2 「`Queued` 가 링에서 밀려난 뒤의 받음」). ★The copy of `AckUnavailable` is always filled by the core★ — a determination that the decoder emitted is also filled by the core with the (id, body) of the items that reduction closes as `Delivered` and written to the ring (same lock). What it blocks = the decision race of §5-4. ★The ground for this rewriting not turning a message that could not be written into a bubble★: the `Queued` that stands **after** the decision was emitted by a session that read `Unknown`, and the `Unknown` branch emits `Queued` **only after the write succeeds** (§5-4 — if emitted before the write, the `Dropped{Rejected}` of a failed write would be swallowed by a tombstone that is already terminal (`Delivered`)). The marker is a **decision in ring order** — it does not read the atomic value `DeliveryAck`: a `Queued` that comes in the gap where the atomic value has flipped but `AckUnavailable` has not yet stood on the ring is carried by the `AckUnavailable` that follows, so there is nothing separate to handle, and if we read the atomic value, the ring replay (accumulator) and the registry could see different orders. The seal comes first (a `Queued` after the seal is `Dropped{AgentEnded}`). A rewritten event does not pass through the classifier (§5-2).
- **Ownership split update (CLAUDE.md core invariants):** session += waiting-input registry · input lock (the core feeds it on emit, makes termination synthesis and seal one unit, rewrites `Queued` after the decision, and fills the body copy on determination). ★Implementation reflected — the landed split: **core** += the waiting-input registry `Arc` (+ the waiting list table `Arc` — `QueuedWiring`) · seal · rewriting after the decision · body copy · **session** += `input_order` · `mid_turn` (mid-turn input policy) · `delivery_ack` (whether receipt notification is possible — incarnation-shared `Arc`) · the registry is reached through the core · **codex transport** += `pending` (held items) · settlement · debt · halt after error · floor decision · steer-response obligation. The CLAUDE.md 「소유권 분할」 line is fixed by P6 (outside this document)★.

### 5-4. The claude path (`SessionClassified`)

- **Receipt-possibility detection (decision):** the backend fills the incarnation-shared `DeliveryAck` (§5-0). `Available` if the decoder sees `msg_lifecycle_v1` in the `capabilities` of `system/init` or **sees a `command_lifecycle` line for the first time**, and `Unavailable` if init comes without that capability (once `Available`, it is not reversed — `compare_exchange`). ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26 — orchestrator decision · P3 review)** — 「`command_lifecycle` 줄」 is **only a line it can recognize**: a line that has both a non-empty string `command_uuid` and a known status word (`queued` · `started` · `completed` · `cancelled` · `discarded` · `refused` — the same set as the translation table) (`backend/claude/mod.rs` `is_recognisable_lifecycle`). Reason: if the vendor changes the line shape (key name · status word), the receipt never comes and the row never closes — if it is a line it cannot recognize, it stays in `Unknown`, and if an init without the capability comes, it goes to `Unavailable` (today's path). Limit (→ §5-9): a vendor that keeps carrying the capability and changes only the key is not blocked by this door★. ★Implementation observation — CLI 2.1.280 emits `command_lifecycle` for **every** input that carries a uuid (including a Direct sent while idle · and mail) → in operation `Delivered`/`Dropped` flow even for ordinary input (registry = unknown id so a tombstone · frontend = no-op if the echo is already drawn · if `refused`, it erases the drawn bubble). An old CLI (no capability) gets `AckUnavailable{[]}` once per incarnation (`info!` logged on the first reduction)★.
  - ★**During `Unknown` we assume `Available`**★ — init is absent at startup and comes **only 0.6–0.9 s after the turn starts** (M4). So a message typed early in the incarnation's first turn is before detection. Reason: the write goes to stdin at once either way, so **delivery does not change**, and when the lifecycle comes (today's CLI emits it even for the first message sent while idle — M1), that item's outcome closes as it is. If a ✕ pressed during `Unknown` reaches an old CLI, an error response → `CancelFailed` — the item stays cancel-pending (could not remove), and the following cannot-receive determination (`AckUnavailable`) closes it as a bubble in place (N1 (a) — the vendor already received it · §5-2 table). ★The same even if an old CLI silently drops the cancel line and the response never comes★ — the determination also closes a cancel-pending item with no response as a bubble in place. If we waited for the response, that item would remain until the agent ends, the list would not empty, and mail would also be blocked (no ceiling — N8) — it contradicts 「옛 버전은 오늘 동작」 (N1 (a)). Cost: a `cancelled:true` that comes late after determination is a terminal tombstone and is discarded, so even if the old CLI actually removed it, a bubble stands — today there is no ✕ and a sent message is always a bubble, so it is the same kind.
  - ★**The one that moves the remaining items when `Unavailable` is determined = the reducer**★ — the decoder is stateless and does not know the items. Instead, the decoder that won the `Unknown → Unavailable` transition emits **one `AckUnavailable` event** on the ring → the registry·accumulator move all the remaining items into bubbles at that ring place by the same rule (§5-2 table) (user decision N1 (a) — an old version gets today's behavior · the vendor already received it · only the place is the moment of determination). After that this incarnation does not use the list (classification below — today's code as it is).
  - ★**The race between the decision transition and classification is closed by the core**★. The race: while the session reads `Unknown` inside the input lock and classifies as Queued, if the pump's decoder flips it to `Unavailable` and stands `AckUnavailable` on the ring first, then the list is empty at that time and there is nothing to move, and **the `Queued` that stands right after remains on the old CLI forever** (the receipt does not come). How to close = the second rewriting of §5-3 — when the registry reduces `AckUnavailable` it sets the 「받음 불가 판명」 marker, and a `Queued` that comes to `emit_inner` after that is rewritten by the core inside the same replay lock into `Queued` + a `AckUnavailable` carrying a copy of that item (the reduction is the same as `Delivered`) (ring order = decision order · N1 (a)). **「고름」 — we do not move the decision transition into the input lock:** the transition arises in the pump (decoder), and the input lock contains the latch commit (disk write), so if the pump waited for that lock, output would stand behind disk I/O (§5-0). The core's replay lock is a place both sides **already** pass through, so ring order = decision order stands without tying the pump to the input path.
  - ★**So that a message that could not be written does not become a bubble after the decision — the `Unknown` branch emits `Queued` after writing (decision)**★. The race: if, after the session stands `Queued` on the ring and before the write returns, `AckUnavailable` stands (the pump does not take the lock), N1 (a) closes that item as `Delivered`, and if the write fails, the following `Dropped{Rejected}` is swallowed by the terminal tombstone and **a message that was not sent remains as a bubble** — the same for the branch where the core rewrites a `Queued` that stood late after the decision (§5-3). Since the only items for which a synthetic `Delivered` can arise are those classified under `Unknown` (`Available` is not reversed), only that branch emits `Queued` **after the write succeeds** — the same order as today's Direct echo standing after a successful write (`session.rs:230`·`:241`). If the write fails, nothing is emitted (a message that never went on the list — `Err` as today). Cost: in that short window (new CLI = from the incarnation's first write to the first lifecycle line — the lifecycle comes within 1 ms of writing, M1 · old CLI = until the first `system/init`, M4) a vendor line can overtake `Queued` — ① if `started` stands first, the tombstone discards the late `Queued` so it does not go on the list and the echo-back draws the bubble (§5-2 — the result is the same for the registry·accumulator) ② if only the echo-back stands first (on the fold path the echo-back is right before `started` — M2), there is no waiting uuid to suppress yet so the echo-back draws the bubble, and the following `Queued` → `Delivered` (vendor `started` · or N1 (a)'s `AckUnavailable`) tries to draw a second bubble → the accumulator's 「이미 그린 uuid」 rule blocks it (§5-7). ★What remains (honestly)★: in that window that message does not pass through the list and is drawn once at the echo-back place — the echo-back comes at consumption (M2), so that place is the delivery place, and a bubble standing at once without a list is the same kind as today's behavior. In ②, that item remains in the registry and the LLM list until `Delivered` (until `AckUnavailable` for an N1 (a) old CLI). We did not find a cheap order that removes this window — if `Queued` is moved back before the write, the race above returns. Alternatives rejected: add a 「썼다」 confirmation event (vocabulary grows) · a transition in which `Dropped{Rejected}` reopens the synthetic-receipt tombstone (transitions grow and the drawn bubble has to be erased). Codex has no such race — it does not announce before the floor decision (§5-5).
- **The gap of the drain turn — we count claude `Delivered` as progress (decision).**
  - What it blocks: since the Queued branch emits no synthetic echo, nothing is left in the turn table, and the **drain turn** the CLI opens with the waiting items right after `result` (1.5 ms — M7) looks to the core like 「한가」 until the first output comes. A message typed in that gap is classified as Direct and becomes a bubble in the sent place, and the vendor folds it into the drain turn's tool boundary — PRD AC3 (「보낸 자리엔 말풍선이 없다」) breaks **systematically on every long turn**. ★In the same gap the **mail busy decision** (the messaging kernel — ADR-0127 turn observation) also reads 「한가」★ so mail is folded into the user's drain turn — the length of the gap is until the first progress signal (`system/init` 0.6–0.9 s · echo-back 1.5–2.1 s — M2·M4, review estimate 0.6–2.1 s).
  - How to close: the claude classifier counts `QueuedInput::Delivered` as **progress (Progress)** (§5-2). The decoder makes that event **only** from the `command_lifecycle` `started` line — 「벤더가 이 입력을 지금 돌리기 시작했다」 (folding into a running turn · or the start of a new turn) is **a fact of vendor output**, and the `started` of the drain turn is that turn's first line. So the core turn observation sees the drain turn as 「턴 중」 at the moment of `started` — the session classification and the mail busy decision look at **the same table** — the gap of session classification closes (from `result` to the drain `started`, ≈1.5 ms, the list is not empty so it is Queued — the drain turn is opened by the waiting items of the list). ★Mail's ≈1.5 ms (M7) is covered by mail's idle rule★ — for the same reason the user list is not empty during that time, so the kernel's busy decision is 「바쁨」 (§5-0 「우편의 한가」 · PRD §3-9). Even within the single `Delivered` that empties the list, there is no moment at which both facts are 「한가」 at once — 「목록이 비었다」 and the doorbell are written **after** the progress of that `Delivered` is written to turn observation (§5-0 「적는 순서」) — and since the session classification also reads the same two tables as mail in the same order (classification below), it is the same. The `started` of a message sent while idle (1 ms after the synthetic echo) is progress that already comes mid-turn, so it is harmless. One remaining risk — if there is a command after which `result` does not come after `started` (a mid-turn slash command, etc. — M13), the turn table stays at 「턴 중」. That risk **exists today too** (the synthetic echo of the same command typed while idle is already written as progress) and is not a newly made kind — M13 looks at the lifecycle and `result` together.
  - ★**Ground in light of ADR-0127**★: that ADR's turn table stands only on **the fact that the classifier read the vendor output flow** and two cleanup points (`finish` · the finalize recheck). `Delivered` is the classifier reading **a translation of a vendor line**, so it is inside that frame — different from the input path **writing** progress (「Queued 갈래에서 진행 적기」). We reject that alternative — the input path would become a third kind of writer (§10-5). Codex is `None` as it is — that `Delivered` also comes **after** the turn end (§5-2 · §5-5).
  - **We do not put in a second, overlapping turn table** — the classification-only 「벤더 턴 신호」 (a value turned on at the drain turn's `started` and off at `result`) gives **the same value at the same moment** as the core turn observation, whose progress is `Delivered` (=`started`) and whose turn end is `result` (both are stood by the pump at the same line — §10-5).
- **Classification (session — inside the input lock, §5-0):** `mid_turn == SessionClassified` ∧ `origin == User` ∧ `delivery_ack ≠ Unavailable` ∧ (**the waiting list table is 「찼다」** ∨ the core turn observation is **mid-turn**) → Queued branch — read the two tables **in this order**. Otherwise **today's code as it is** (send and echo — `session.rs:230`·`:241`) — if `Unavailable` it takes this road even mid-turn (user decision N1 (a) — an old CLI gets today's behavior · §4-3). ★We do not read the halt after error (the turn table's `last_end_failed` — §5-0)★ — a message typed during a halt also follows this rule (if idle, Direct · that turn releases the halt).
  - ★**We do not read the registry directly — the list table first, turn observation second (decision)**★. The registry empties inside the replay lock but the progress of turn observation is written after that lock is released (`output_core.rs:340-344`). If an input that comes in while the drain turn's list-emptying `Delivered` emit sits in between reads the registry, it is 「비었다」+「턴 없음」 so it goes Direct and becomes a bubble in the sent place (PRD AC3). The waiting list table writes 「비었다」 **after** that progress (§5-0 「적는 순서」 ②), so if we read the table first and turn observation second, a read that saw 「비었다」 also sees that progress — the same order as the kernel adapter (§5-0 ④). 「찼다」 is written inside the replay lock that reduced it, so the `Queued` emitted by the earlier input under the input lock is already visible to the next input's read.
  - The core turn observation covers the 1 ms right after a message sent while idle (Direct) (the synthetic echo is written as progress — `backend/claude/mod.rs:506` — the vendor `started` is after that) — that synthetic echo is **today's behavior** and this design does not change it.
  - 「목록이 비지 않음」 is the idle definition of PRD §3-5 (where it is read = the waiting list table — above). In claude 「턴 없음 + 목록 있음」 is usually a moment — the CLI opens a new turn with the waiting items right after `result` (1.5 ms) (M7). The case where it stays long = an item whose receipt does not come (a slash command — PRD R7 · M13, §5-9).
  - ★We do not violate ADR-0193★ — what that ADR blocked is using the echo for the **release decision**. Here turn observation is used only for **display classification**, and release is done by the vendor. The remaining misclassification is in only one direction: classify as Queued in a turn that just ended → the CLI starts a new turn → `started` comes soon and the list shows for a moment (the wobble PRD §4 「턴이 끝나는 찰나」 accepted). ★The opposite direction (「관측이 늦어 한가로 오분류 → 보낸 자리 말풍선」) is closed by `Delivered` = progress★ — what remains is not the incarnation's first-turn `Unknown` segment (that is the `Available` assumption) but only old versions where the CLI does not emit the lifecycle, and that is today's behavior as it is (N1 (a)).
- **Queued branch order (inside the input lock):** ① latch count (ADR-0226 — at today's place as it is) ② `Queued{id, text}` emit (the Queued branch stands only when it is not `Unavailable` — classification above) ③ `send_input` (a `user` line with a uuid) ④ we **do not emit** a synthetic echo. The reason ② comes before ③ = if the echo-back·lifecycle overtakes `Queued`, the accumulator discards it as an unknown id (the lifecycle comes within 1 ms of writing — M1). ③ failure → emit `Dropped{Rejected}` and then `Err` (the WS reply is an error as today). ★If `delivery_ack == Unknown`, swap ② and ③ — ② only after ③ succeeds, and if it fails emit nothing and `Err` (the 「쓰지 못한 글」 item above)★.
- **Decoder translation (stateless):** `command_lifecycle` (key = `command_uuid`) `started` → `Delivered` · `cancelled` → `Dropped{Unknown}` · `discarded` → `Dropped{AgentEnded}` · `refused` → `Dropped{Rejected}` · `queued`·`completed` → none. The `started` of input sent as Direct also goes out as `Delivered` (M1 — the lifecycle comes even when idle) but the registry·accumulator do not know that id so only a tombstone remains. A `cancelled` after `started` (cancelled-over-completed — §3-1) is also a terminal id so the registry discards it, and the accumulator does not touch the bubble either — a message that already became a bubble in a turn that ended in error is left as it is (PRD §3-10 · user decision Q8 · no marker either — §5-2). ★A `refused` after `started` is different★ — a rejection erases the bubble drawn with that id, and the registry rewrites the outcome as `Discarded{Rejected}` (§5-2 「받음 뒤 거절」). A `control_response` whose `request_id` is `cancel:<uuid>` → if success, `CancelAnswered{uuid, removed: response.response.cancelled}` · if `subtype:"error"`, `CancelFailed{uuid}`.
- **Cancel (inside the input lock):** the session checks `Queued` in the registry (if already cancel-pending, it ends with neither an event nor a cancel line — idempotent, §5-6) → `CancelRequested{id}` emit → `send_input` the backend's `cancel_line(id)` = `{"type":"control_request","request_id":"cancel:<uuid>","request":{"subtype":"cancel_async_message","message_uuid":"<uuid>"}}`. The lock guarantees 「글 쓰기 뒤에만 취소」 (§5-0 ②). ★**The outcome is decided by the lifecycle — the one exception is the response `true`**★ — `started` → Delivered (late) · `cancelled` → cancelled **or** discarded. If the `response.response.cancelled` value of a `control_response` success (M3 — nested) is `false`, **we do not use it as the outcome**: on the boundary `started` comes after `false` (M3 ⓑ), and a reservation cancel also answers `false` (§3-1). `true` doubles as outcome (a) — it is an answer after the handler removed that message from the queue (§3-1 decision ②) and it cannot be drained again, so even if the lifecycle `cancelled` has not come yet, we close it at once as cancelled (§5-2 — if we waited, it would become an invisible blockage on the day that line does not come). ★**That value also tells apart the cause of `cancelled`**★: if `cancelled` comes and our response is `true` (removed), outcome (a) 「취소됨」 · if `false` (could not remove) or an error, that `cancelled` is not ours (a failed turn closed the waiting items — our cancel afterwards met a uuid not in the queue and answered with a reservation) → outcome (c) 「버려짐」 (`Discarded{Unknown}` — follow what the vendor closed, PRD §3-10 · user decision Q8). The order of the two events is accepted either way (§5-2 table — if our cancel removed it, the lifecycle comes before the response in the same ms, M3 ⓐ). The decoder strips the uuid from `request_id` and emits the two response events (carrying the uuid in the request id is the means of keeping the decoder stateless — decision).
  - ★**If the cancel line `send_input` is `Err` → emit `CancelFailed{id}` and then return that error**★ (inside the lock, so no other cancel intervenes in between). The vendor's error response is the same event (decoder — above).
    - ★**`CancelFailed` does not return the item to `Queued`**★ — it stays cancel-pending and the reducer writes the response field as 「못 뺐다」 (§5-2). If returned, an item that left every window with ✕ would be drawn again — it breaks PRD §3-6 「다시 나타나지 않는다」 · AC13 「셋 중 정확히 하나」 (user: 「x 누르면 바로 빠지는 거잖아」).
    - The old reason (「되돌리는 사건이 없으면 응답을 영영 못 받는 `Cancelling` 으로 남는다」) does not stand — the outcome stands not by the response but by the lifecycle, and since that message has already been written to stdin (the lock sends the cancel only after the message write — §5-0 ②), the vendor lifecycle comes: `started` → (b) an end-of-conversation bubble · `cancelled`·`discarded`·`refused` → (c) discarded (since it is not what our cancel removed, `cancelled` is also 「버려짐」 — cause attribution, §5-2) · cannot-receive determination → a bubble in place (N1 (a)) · agent end → (c).
    - The command returns an error (§5-6), and in the LLM list that item is `cancelling` until the outcome. If it is a message whose receipt never comes (PRD R7 — M13), it stays cancel-pending and is discarded when the agent ends — the same kind as when a list item does not get its receipt (§5-9). What stays this long is only a cancel-pending that is could-not-remove — an item the vendor answered it removed (`cancelled:true`) closes at once as cancelled without waiting for the lifecycle line (§5-2).
  - The cancel window is **until just before the fold filter** — in measurement a cancel sent 540 ms after the tool result also took effect (§3-1 row 1 · that length differs by environment).
- **Interrupt (the next feature — PRD §2 · §7 · user decision Q5):** outside this document's scope and written only as that feature's premise. When claude JSON interrupt is brought in, the disposition of the waiting items splits on the `cancel_queued` of `interrupt` — discard = `cancel_queued:true` (the waiting items `cancelled` → at `Queued`, `Dropped{Unknown}` → `Discarded`) · to the next turn = without `cancel_queued` (the waiting items survive — §3-1). Either way this document's reduction rule takes it. ★That feature must carry the interrupted `result` in the turn observation table not as a clean end but as 「그 밖」 (as is)★ — today's decoder turns an interrupted `result` that is `subtype:"interrupted"` into a `MessageDone` without `Error` (edition 8 source reading `backend/claude/mod.rs:818-829`), and if the classifier stays as it is it reads it as a clean end. ★Implementation reflected — this implementation is as it is: an interrupted `result` is still folded as a clean end (`Ended(Clean)`) and releases the halt after error — fixing it is the interrupt feature's share★.
- **Resume restore (M5 green):** the transcript seed moves a `{"type":"attachment","attachment":{"type":"queued_command",…}}` line into a **user bubble** (★implementation reflected — only those with `commandMode: "prompt"` are moved: what other modes carry was not read in source · `ClaudeStreamDecoder::consume_queued_command`★) — uuid = `source_uuid`, body = the text fragments of `prompt`. `queue-operation` lines are skipped. Cancelled input leaves no body so it does not come back to life (correct behavior — it is a discarded message). The hole that arose today from `consume_line` discarding `attachment` (PRD R5) is closed by this — the effect on restore order is read in source at implementation (§9).
- **A message sent while loading a resumed conversation (PRD §3-11 · AC28 — user decision Q6: keep it in the list and hand it over at once)** — ★**On our side there is no signal to tell 「불러오는 중」 from 「한가」. We leave that window as today's behavior (user decision N7 (a) — the claude part of PRD §3-11 · AC28 is an exception)**★.
  - **Where we looked for a signal** (source reading — this document's branch HEAD, `crates/`·`src/` unchanged versus the reading baseline commit):
    - ① The daemon's transcript seed finishes **before** the session goes on the registry — so before the pump starts (ADR-0079 「seed-before-publish」 — `manager.rs:1902` · `output_core.rs:161`). When the incarnation receives input, the daemon-side loading has already ended, and the restored part is closed by one turn end so that incarnation is immediately 「한가」 (`backend/claude/mod.rs:1042-1060` — that synthetic end does not pass through turn observation).
    - ② The CLI does not emit a line saying its own loading (`--resume`) has ended — `system/init` comes only after the first turn starts (M4) and there is nothing on stdout before the first input.
    - ③ ADR-0226's 「이어받는 화신」 marker does not know the end — `continues_conversation` is 「이 spawn 이 이어받을 손잡이를 실었다(성공 여부가 아니다)」 (`manager.rs:579` · value = `:1496`) and does not change for the whole incarnation. That ADR's loading panel is something the frontend draws as 「이력 행이 아직 없다」 (`RichSlot.tsx:308` — `continuesConversation && !hasSent && !hasHistoryRow`) so it has nothing to do with the CLI's loading — for claude the seed is already in the replay, so it usually lifts at once.
  - **The present design = today's behavior (classification rule as it is):** that window has turn observation idle and the list empty, so it is Direct — write to stdin at once (the path queue·pipe holds it until the CLI reads) and draw a bubble in the sent place (`session.rs:230`·`:241`). The following `started` is an unknown id so only a tombstone remains (§5-2). **Delivery is the same as the PRD** (hand over at once and the vendor handles it after loading — that premise is M14). **What differs is one visible thing** — instead of the list a bubble stands at once and there is no ✕ meanwhile (the claude part of PRD §3-11 · AC28 — an exception by N7 (a)). If mail comes to that window, mail also goes in as today (the list is empty).
  - **The order is right — the user chose (a) on this ground (N7):** the bubble of a message sent while loading stands **after** the restored history — since the daemon's seed goes onto the ring before putting the session on the registry (① above — `manager.rs:1902`), it is earlier on the ring than any input after that, and even if events arrive reversed the client matches them by seq (§5-7). User: 「그냥 내가 채팅 치다가 오면 병합 잘 되는거잖아. 이번에 순서 삐뚤어져도 맞추는거 추가하잖아.」 · 「애초에 코덱스쪽에서 리스트를 먼저 보내주겠지 시작하자마자」 (codex already holds a message typed while resuming in the list until the connection ends — §5-5). The alternative of always putting the resuming incarnation's first user input on the list (N7 (b)) was rejected (§10-5).
- **Several left at the end of a turn (claude side — PRD §3-1 · AC27 · user decision Q9):** the vendor decides the draining (M11). Our code does not change either way — if the vendor behavior differs from 「한 다음 턴에 함께, 각자 따로」, report to the user (ADR-0192).
- **A turn that ended in error (claude side — PRD §3-10 · AC25 · user decision Q8 「억지로 구현하지 마」):** follow what the vendor does — the decoder translates the lifecycle as it is (if the vendor discards it, `Dropped` → it leaves the list · if it puts it into the conversation, `Delivered` → a bubble) and we add no cancel·resend·marker on our side. The failure decision is already made by today's decoder (`is_error`·`subtype` of `result` — `backend/claude/mod.rs:806-819`) — list handling does not use that decision. ★**The one place that uses that decision is mail (user decision N11)**★ — that turn end sets the halt after error (`last_end_failed`) in the turn observation table, and the kernel does not let mail in until the user's next turn ends in success (§5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」). Mail is something we put in, so we apply it regardless of vendor behavior — list items still follow the vendor. If the vendor closes the failed turn's waiting items with `cancelled`, the list item becomes `Discarded{Unknown}` — an item that was cancel-pending is also 「버려짐」 and not 「취소됨」 by the cause attribution above. If the waiting items go to the next turn (`still_queued`), that turn's `started` is the receipt. M12 checks that those branches close as stated — if the list stays diverged from the vendor, report.

### 5-5. The codex path (`TransportOwned` + ADR-0198 implementation)

> ★**Superseded (2026-09-26 simplification · user decision → ADR-0235) — most of this section's codex hand-over design has been withdrawn. What follows is left as the edition 9 record and is not corrected.**★ Withdrawn: the hand-over policy (`HandOverPolicy` · segment table · holding in the answer segment · `AtEarliestBoundary`) · end-of-turn settlement · follow-up turn debt · acceptance unknown (`Unconfirmed` · `awaiting_reply`) and the anomaly counter · the disposition that erases the messages of a turn that ended in rejection·failure. The source of truth for the current behavior = ADR-0235 and `backend/codex/transport.rs` — a held user message is handed over by steer when the running turn's tool item ends (if there is no tool, all together in the next turn after the turn end) · only the echo is a receipt · a message of a turn that ended with no echo goes back to waiting regardless of the shape of the end · a failed end sets the halt (`halted`) and waits until the next user message enters (an interrupt does not set it). What stands as it was: the two path verbs (but a row that is `TooLate` stays in the list and is drawn as a handed-over row) · the floor decision · two-stage emission · first echo-back persistence (ADR-0233).

- **Two path verbs (with default implementations — `pty.rs`·`stdio.rs` do not change by a single line):**
  - `AgentTransport::send_turn(&self, turn: TurnInput{ id, body, origin }) -> Result<(), PtyError>` — the default implementation delegates to `send_input(InputEvent::Raw(body))` (adding a variant to `InputEvent` would break the irrefutable pattern of the two files, so we do not use that road — decision). The session calls this verb only when `mid_turn == TransportOwned` and carries `origin` as it is (§5-0).
  - `AgentTransport::withdraw(&self, id) -> Withdraw` — `Withdraw::{Withdrawn, TooLate, NotHeld}` (the held item was erased · already handed over — only removed from the list · an id the path does not know) · default implementation = `NotHeld`. When the session's cancel is `TransportOwned`, it goes by this verb (§5-0). Disposition = 「✕」 below.
- ★**When to hand over = the swappable policy `HandOverPolicy` (decision — name · place)**★. 「지금 넘기나 · 쥐나」 is answered by one policy inside the codex backend (`backend/codex/`), and the path's classification and writer follow only that answer. The session·manager does not know the policy — all the session sees is `TransportOwned` (ADR-0004 — backend knowledge in one place).
  - **Two implementations:** `Immediate` — hand over at once if it can be handed over (the first landing — §9 P2). `AtEarliestBoundary` — hold only in the segments where measurement stood 「붙들어도 늦지 않는다」, and hand over at once on a boundary signal (landing after measurement — §9 P8). ★Edition 9 — **the operating default is `AtEarliestBoundary`** (M15 · M16 green → P8 landed · it holds the answer segment too). `Immediate` is left as a test seam and a place to go back to★. ✕ is on every item under both policies — what the policy divides is only whether a pressed ✕ withdraws the message (held item) or only removes it from the list (handed-over item) (「✕」 below). The latter is the codex edition of the user principle 「받을 수 있는 가장 이른 때」 (PRD §3-1).
  - **Where it is chosen** = one constant of the codex backend (decision — we do not add a user settings surface).
