Tests swap it through the seam that builds the path. ★Implementation = `backend/codex/mod.rs` `HAND_OVER_POLICY` (= `AtEarliestBoundary`) · answer-segment field = `transport.rs` `ANSWER_SEGMENT_HOLDS` (= true — M16 green) · seam = `CodexAppServerTransport::with_hand_over` · a path built without the seam also has that constant's value★
  - **Segment table — the whole of the policy:**

    | Segment (the path decides from its own state) | `Immediate` | `AtEarliestBoundary` | Ground |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | **Cannot hand over** — connecting · **waiting for the turn id** (`Active{turn_id: None}` · including items behind the head of a turn about to open — 「턴 id 대기」 below) · end-of-turn settlement · Idle during a halt after error with no user message arrived since the halt (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below) · a 「steer 거절」 mark on that turn (steer pairing below) | holds | holds | The vendor cannot receive now or we have no means of handing over yet (waiting for the turn id = there is no `expectedTurnId` to put in the steer) — holding does not delay delivery (PRD §3-10 · §3-11) |
    | **Boundary open** — right when the turn id has just come (the turn has just opened) · right after a boundary signal — until that turn's next `item/started` · the head of a **turn about to open** (Idle — below) | at once | at once | If handed over now it can get into the coming check of waiting items — holding could miss it · the head of a turn about to open goes out at once as `turn/start` |
    | **Tool segment** — the current turn has a **tool item** (below — only known tool variants) for which `item/completed` has not come after `item/started` | at once | holds → at once at `item/completed` when all tools have ended | M7 10/10 (window 6–64 ms — §3-2) · our reaction delay = M15 |
    | **Answer segment** — no tool is running, and after boundary-open has closed, the most recently stood item of the current turn is an **output item** (below) | at once | **M16 green** = holds → at once at the end-of-answer signal · **M16 red** = at once | M16 (not measured). Even if red it is not a user question — PRD §3-1 decided it as 「붙들면 늦어지는 구간은 곧바로」 |
    | **Other** — no tool is running, and the most recently stood item is neither tool nor output (「그 밖 항목」 below — including unknown variants) | at once | at once | There is no ground (a measured boundary) that holding is not too late — if ambiguous, at once (below) · ★implementation reflected (P8) — if a held answer-segment item becomes 「곧바로」 through the `item/started` of an item in the Other segment (it is not a boundary signal), the reader wakes the writer: if it did not, it would stay until the next sweep (`SWEEP_INTERVAL`) and could miss the check of waiting items in between (`Reader::track_segment`) · the tracking label of a steer released by that wake-up = `hop="zone"` (P8-fix — a new Hold segment or boundary signal erases that mark · `State::zone_opened`)★ |

  - ★**✕ has nothing to do with the segment — the segment table decides only when to hand over**★ (user decision 2026-09-25 — §0). Every codex item of the list has a ✕, and when it is pressed the path decides the outcome by its stage at that moment (「✕」 below): if the item is held, the message is withdrawn, and if it is handed over, it is only removed from the list. So even when the segment changes or an item is handed over, no event goes out to the list — there is no such thing as ✕ turning on and off.
  - ★**Waiting for the turn id is cannot-hand-over — both policies hold**★. The writer cannot steer a held item before the `turn/start` response gives the turn id (hand-over rule `Active{turn_id: None}` below). A ✕ pressed meanwhile withdraws the message (`Withdrawn`), and the write and ✕ are split under the same state lock — a ✕ that comes after the id has come and the writer has moved `Held` to `InFlight` is the ✕ of a handed-over item (`TooLate`) (「✕」 below).
    - **A turn about to open** (Ready ∧ Idle ∧ no settlement ∧ (not a halt after error ∨ a user message has arrived since the halt) — the moment a user message arrives after the halt · the moment settlement closes · the moment the connection gate opens · the moment a Direct item waits for the `turn/start` write) is **boundary open**: the writer hands the head over at once as `turn/start`. ★What Idle hands over is the head alone★ — the items behind wait for the id of the turn that `turn/start` opens, so the decision function already sees them at Idle as waiting for the turn id (they hold — they become that segment the moment the head is written).
    - ★**Deciding a new item during a halt — order (decision)**★: if a user message U arrives during a halt after error, within one lock span the path puts U into pending → re-decides by the present state whether there is a turn to open under the halt (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below — U opens that turn) → and decides whether to hold or hand over U by the state **after** that. That state is a turn about to open, and if there are items that had been held from before the halt, U is behind the head (the remaining items first — PRD AC24) → waiting for the turn id · holds. Even if it judged that there is no turn to open, the answer is the same (holds) — nailing down the order is so that the decision is always read from a single state.
  - ★**Handing over early is harmless and only handing over late is harmful**★ — a message handed over before the boundary waits for that boundary in the vendor's waiting box (all that is lost is the window to withdraw the message by ✕ — ✕ still removes it from the list afterward). So when the segment decision is ambiguous, it always falls to the 「곧바로」 side — counting as tools only the variants confirmed as tools below and leaving the rest as 「그 밖」 (at once), and dragging boundary-open to that turn's next item, are that rule. ★**Counting an item as a tool is the holding side**★ — `AtEarliestBoundary` holds the tool segment, so if an item for which we do not know whether the vendor's check of waiting items comes after it ends is counted as a tool, we could wait for that end signal and miss the check (the late side).
  - ★**Segment tracking — the path reader, under the state lock, looks only at lines of the current turn id**★ (the late completion of an interrupted turn's tool comes with the old turn id — M6). Item classification (decision) — three, reading the decoder's two vocabularies as they are (the item vocabulary `KNOWN_ITEM_TYPES` — `decoder.rs:215-235` · the tool-call vocabulary `TOOL_ITEM_TYPES` — `decoder.rs:270-277`) (the path does not copy the list separately):
    - **Tool item** = the six variants the decoder turns into tool calls (`commandExecution`·`fileChange`·`mcpToolCall`·`dynamicToolCall`·`collabAgentToolCall`·`webSearch`). To widen this list, first there must be a ground (source or an M7-style measurement) that the vendor's check of waiting items comes after the `item/completed` of that variant.
    - **Output item** = `agentMessage`·`reasoning`·`plan`. `plan` is model output — it flows in as deltas (`item/plan/delta` — `decoder.rs:156`). It is not a tool so it is treated the same as an answer.
    - **Other item** = everything else — the echo `userMessage` · `hookPrompt` · `contextCompaction` · review-mode markers · known variants for which we did not look at the vendor ground on whether it is a tool (`imageView`·`imageGeneration`·`sleep`·`subAgentActivity`·`functionCallOutput`) · variants not in the vocabulary. It puts the segment at 「그 밖」 and does not enter the tool set.
  - Three boundary signals:
    - ① **Tool end** — `item/completed` when the running tool set empties. With parallel tools, it is when the last one ends — the vendor checks the waiting items after all the tools of that sampling have ended (§3-2 row 1). The window for the parallel case is not measured, so it goes into M15.
    - ② **End of answer** — the `item/completed` of a `agentMessage` or `plan` that came when no tool is running (a steer sent at that moment got into the follow-up sampling of the same turn — M6 T3, once · the end of `plan` is measured together by M16). The end of `reasoning` is not a signal — the same sampling continues the answer.
    - ③ **End of sampling (backup)** — `thread/tokenUsage/updated`. It comes after the tools have all ended, **right before** the check of waiting items (the check is 2–9 ms after that — §3-2). That window is too narrow to lean on, and it is used only as the last chance when the first two were missed. At this signal the tool set is emptied — so that an item that does not announce its end does not remain and make us misjudge the next segment as a tool segment.
    - When a signal comes, the segment becomes boundary-open and the writer is woken. Boundary-open closes at that turn's next `item/started` — if the vendor has started the next item, the check has passed. The segment after it closes is as that item's classification says (tool · answer · other).
  - **`Immediate` also tracks segments and wakes the writer on boundary signals** — it just does not use them in the hand-over decision. That wake-up is where the first leg of M15 (reader signal → writer wake — separately at the two signals, tool end and answer end · reader lag is written down together) is measured, and where the turn-id hop (`turn/start` response → steer of held items) and the steer handed over at once while a running command pours output are measured on the real path route as the second leg (writer wake → steer write — the latter under a delta flood) — in `Immediate` there is no steer for the tool-end signal to release, so the two legs cannot be measured in one go (§3-3 · §9 P2).
- **The user item the path holds (`pending`)** — stages: `Held` (not yet handed over) · `InFlight{gen, turn_id, awaiting_reply: bool}` (a steer or `turn/start` was written and we wait for the echo — `awaiting_reply` = are we still waiting for that request's response. Both a success response and a deadline expiry move it to `false` — if there is no echo at turn end both are acceptance unknown, so there is nowhere to tell them apart, settlement 2 below) · `Unconfirmed` (handed over but acceptance unknown — with no request outstanding, it waits for a late echo · ✕ · agent end, 「수락 모름」 below). Three item fields: `cancel_asked` (a ✕ that came after hand-over — 「✕」 below) · `listed` (is it a list item — Direct is `false`, 「분류와 방출」 below) · arrival number (a number the path increments each time it puts a user item into pending — read by the hand-over rule (is there an older item — below) and the halt-after-error decision (below)). Two turn-level fields: **end-of-turn settlement** (`settling: Option<{gen, 시한, 기다리는 응답}>`) · **follow-up turn debt** (`follow_up_owed: Option<gen>` — that turn has a receipt it gave no answer to). Two path fields: **segment** (above) · **halt after error** (`halted: Option<도착 번호>` — 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below). ★Four old fields were withdrawn★ — the failure marker of the wait after an error turn (`parked`) and the generation of the last `failed` turn were folded into one halt, and the generation that issued the last user·empty turn and the hidden record after ✕ then unknown (`withdrawn_unknown`) lost their use, since no debt is set after the end of a turn (「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」 below). Pending holds all non-terminal codex user items of the list and **Direct items before their outcome** (outside the list) — the path does not read the registry and decides **from its own state** (ADR-0193). ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — the landed fields**★ (`State` · `PendingItem` · `Stage` of `backend/codex/transport.rs` — the field docs are the source of truth): a fourth field `steered: bool` on `InFlight` (is that request `turn/steer` — read by the debt decision, 「답 없는 항목」 below) · on the item, `announced` (has emission finished — 「분류와 방출」 below) and `death: Option<DropCause>` (the cause of a disposition that arrived before announce — the announcing side chains `Dropped` right after `Queued`) · an item with no identifier (`id: None` — the old `send_input`) drops out at the opening place and is outside the list · ✕ · settlement. The settlement field is `Settling{gen, deadline, owes}` and ★**the response being waited for is held not inside the settlement but by the path field `steers_owed: Vec<SteerOwed{gen, item, echoed}>`**★ — the response obligation outlives the item (even if the echo comes before the response and the item leaves pending, settlement still waits for that response · the first landing that read the wait only at the item stage `awaiting_reply` was withdrawn by the P2 review (codex FIX)). `owes` = does it set a debt when it closes (the decision already stood at the turn end). Debt material = `unanswered: Option<gen>` (a message handed over by steer to an open turn was received but that turn's sampling start has not happened yet) · the turn that cleared the debt = `debt_paid_by: Option<gen>` (if that turn ends oddly, only a warning + counting — 「내는 순간 푼다」 implementation reflected below). On the path fields: `floor: Floor{Pending, Above, Below}` (floor decision — below) · `steer_refused: Option<gen>` (「steer 거절」 mark) · counter `anomalies: Anomalies{unechoed, late_echoes, unpaid, refused_after_echo}` (the per-incarnation cumulative count of a branch that should not exist per the source — `total` of the warning log). The value of `halted` = the next arrival number at the moment of the halt (after the marker = `arrival >= 표식`). The shared input ceiling `INPUT_QUEUE_LIMIT` (32) counts **all** items of pending — including acceptance unknown (it is visible in the list and can be removed with ✕ · orchestrator decision).
  - ★**Generation discrimination**★: `gen` = a number the path increments each time it sees a new active turn id (★implementation reflected — we do not keep a separate counter: `gen` = the turn mark `TurnState::Active.seq` of the turn that request went into. The turn id comes only through that turn's `turn/start` response so it is the same value and there is no place for the two to diverge★). Each time we handle a steer response · echo, we compare **(id, gen) with that item's present stage**, and if they do not match it is a **no-op** — a late response cannot move twice an item that has already moved to another stage.
- **Classification and emission (inside the path, two stages):** under the lock, if `Ready ∧ Idle ∧ 정산 없음 ∧ pending 빔` it is Direct (~~if end-of-turn settlement is open, pending is not empty~~ ★edition 9 — wrong: a settlement waiting only for the response of an echoed steer stands with pending empty (`steers_owed`) — so the Direct decision looks at 「정산 없음」 separately · implementation reflected · P2 review★ · even during a halt after error, if pending is empty it is Direct — the turn of that message is the user turn it opens under the halt, 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below), otherwise it decides Queued and puts the item in as `Held`·`announced=false` — ★Direct is put in too (`listed=false`)★ → release the lock and emit (Direct = ADR-0198 synthetic echo `Structured{kind:"user", {type:text,text,uuid:id}}` · Queued = `Queued{id, text}`) → take it again and `announced=true` + notify. The writer **waits if the head is before announce** (it does not skip — FIFO). Reason: emit has to be outside the lock (ADR-0006), and if hand-over overtakes emission, the accumulator discards `Delivered` as an unknown id.
  - ★**Direct items are also put in pending (decision)**★ — if not, a second input that came in the same idle moment also sees `pending 빔` and becomes Direct, and if the first message's `turn/start` opens a turn and then goes out by steer and is rejected (−32600) it returns to `Held` and becomes a held item outside the list — it is in no window and not in the LLM list, yet fills pending, turning every new message into waiting and also blocking mail. If put in, the second input stands as Queued behind the first message (the turn the first message opened is running soon — the second is steered at once to that turn id).
    - **A Direct item always goes out as `turn/start` as the head of Idle and is not steered** — pending was empty at the moment of classification, and the Idle order opens the user `Held` first (below). So there is no branch that returns by a steer error.
    - ★**We never emit `Queued` with a Direct id**★ — emission is done only for `listed` items. A Direct item has no branch to be held again — it is not steered (above), and if there is no echo at turn end it is removed from pending with no event (the bubble already stands · a late echo has the same uuid so it is deduped · outcome below). If held again, an item that is neither in the list nor has a ✕ would fill pending and turn every new message into waiting (Direct cannot stand), and would go out again at the next user message (§10-5).
    - Outcome: echo = `Delivered` (an unknown id in the registry so only a tombstone) · `turn/start` write failure·error response = `Dropped{Rejected}` → erase the drawn bubble without a notification (PRD §3-5 · AC26 — five exits below) · acceptance unknown = removed only from pending with no event (the bubble stands · we do not leave it as `Unconfirmed` — if an item outside the list fills pending, it turns every new message into waiting with an empty list and blocks mail, 「Direct 항목도 pending 에 둔다」 above) · interrupt·connection end = removed with no event (the bubble stays — §5-2 「거절만 지운다」). ✕ does not reach it (it is not in the registry — `NOT_FOUND`).
  - ★**Hold or hand over = one decision function that answers by the policy and the segment table**★ — the classification and the writer use it together (decision). That answer exists only inside the path and does not go out as a list event — neither when handing over, nor when a handed-over item returns to `Held` by a vendor rejection and is held again, is there re-emission (✕ is always there — above).
  - ★**The user bubble the path synthesized (the Direct synthetic echo) is emitted as `emit_without_turn_observation`**★ — if emitted as the usual `emit`, the classifier would write it as progress and turn 「턴 중」 on again, and if `turn/start` is rejected it would leave a turn that was never opened as 「진행 중」 (an ADR-0198·0193 regression — the ADR-0127 danger of mail being blocked until the 30-minute fail-open). The codex idle decision is carried by the path state (ADR-0193), so there is no reason to write it in the turn table. The probe 「있음」 bubble that was synthesized after the end of a turn was withdrawn together with the probe (settlement 2 below).
  - ★**The echo the vendor sends (`userMessage`) passes through the path reader's attribution gate as today — a late echo after the end of a turn does not turn the turn table on again**★. The decoder turns the echo into `Structured` and the codex classifier counts that branch as progress (`backend/codex/mod.rs:1164-1168` · the only thing that enters that branch on this backend is the echo-back — `:1144-1146`). So if an echo that came after the turn ended (an acceptance-unknown item · revival · during a halt after error · after the five exits · after the follow-up turn ended) were emitted as the usual `emit`, an incarnation for which `TurnEnd` does not come again would stay at 「턴 중」 and mail would be blocked until the next turn ends or the 30-minute fail-open (that ADR-0127 danger). What blocks it = the decision 「이 줄을 지금 열린 우리 턴의 것으로 셀 수 있나」 the reader asks on every vendor line (`claims_our_turn` — `transport.rs:2039-2084`, written in `:2250-2260`): if there is no open turn (Idle) or the id of the open turn and the `turnId` of the line disagree, it is emitted as `emit_without_turn_observation` — the bubble stands as is and only the turn table is not touched. This gate is today's code as it is and this feature does not change it. ★Two conditions this design must keep★: ① end-of-turn settlement · halt after error · follow-up turn debt are fields **outside** the path `TurnState` — when T ends, `TurnState` is Idle (if those fields were expressed as `Active`, this gate would count a late echo as our turn) ② the window in which the gate counts a late echo as our turn is only while a new turn waits for its id (`Active{turn_id: None}` — 「모순되지 않으면 우리 것」), and that turn is actually running and its own end (`turn/completed` · the `TurnEnd` of `end_turn_if` — `transport.rs:1251`) closes it.
  - ★**We do not announce before the floor decision**★ — the decision comes inside the handshake (the `cliVersion` of the `thread/start`·`thread/resume` response — M8). Items that came before that (all are connecting — Direct stands only after `Ready`) are held as `announced=false`, and **the side where the decision came announces**: floor met → `Queued` · below → **today's path** (user decision N1 (a) — not put on the list, and held until the turn end with no synthetic echo, `delivery_ack = Unavailable`). So the 「이미 목록에 오른 연결 중 항목이 미달 판정을 만나는」 case does not arise. The only window in which the list appears late is the handshake round trip (about 130 ms measured — ADR-0190).
- **Hand-over rule (writer `next_job`) — as the policy answers, only in the order typed:** if there is an older (smaller arrival number) `Held`, the later items are also held (PRD §3-5 · AC2 — the no-overtaking ban is among `Held` · bubble order = order typed). ★**An acceptance-unknown item (`Unconfirmed`) does not block — later messages are handed over in order as usual (user decision N13 (b))**★: that item is a message already handed over to codex, so handing over later-typed messages after it is the order typed (C → D → E). That item stays gray with a ✕, and mail waits until it leaves (「수락 모름」 below · N8 — it is visible in the list so the reason it is blocked is on screen). If a late echo comes, that bubble may stand after the bubbles of the messages that were handed over in between — accepted (the acceptance-unknown branch itself is a defensive branch never observed · user: 「음? 뭔소리야 순서대로 들어가는거잖아. 앞지른다는게 뭔소리? 또 발생되지도 않는걸 가정함?」). The alternative of holding later messages behind that item is §10-5.
  - `Ready ∧ Active{turn_id: Some(t)}` ∧ no 「steer 거절」 mark on t → while the policy answers 「곧바로」, from the `Held` at the head in order `turn/steer{threadId, expectedTurnId:t, clientUserMessageId:id, input}` → `InFlight{gen, t, awaiting_reply:true}`. If it answers 「쥔다」, it waits for a boundary signal (the reader wakes it). **One at a time** (PRD §3-8 — several are not loaded into one request).
  - `Ready ∧ Active{turn_id: None}` → wait — held items are waiting for the turn id (cannot hand over — above). ★The turn id comes **only through the `turn/start` response**★ (paired by our request id — `transport.rs:2086-2087`). `turn/started` has no identifier we issued so it cannot be attributed to a turn, so the path deliberately does not read it (`transport.rs:322` 「되살리지 말 것」) — this design also does not use it as an id source (§10-5).
  - `Ready ∧ Idle` ∧ no settlement → ★**the order of opening a turn = user first** (PRD §3-9 · user decision N6)★ — ★during a halt after error only the first branch, and that only runs when a user message that arrived after the halt is in pending (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below)★:
    - If there is a user `Held` (including Direct), `turn/start{…, clientUserMessageId: 머리 id}` with the head (ADR-0198) → the head is `InFlight`. The remaining `Held` are waiting for the turn id until then (held) and the moment the new turn id comes is boundary-open so they are steered at once — ★**the remaining items go together into one next turn, each separately** (PRD §3-1 · AC27 — user decision Q9)★.
    - If there is no user `Held` and there is a follow-up turn debt, an empty input `turn/start` once — the debt is released the moment it is issued (「답 없는 항목」 below) — ★even if there is mail held, this comes first★.
    - ★Only when there is no user `Held` and no debt and also no acceptance-unknown item (`Unconfirmed` — standing in the list)★ is the mail turn (「우편」 · 「수락 모름」 below).
    - ★**Why the debt comes before mail**★ — the debt arose because a **user message** got no answer (PRD §3-1 · AC6). If the mail turn opens first, the model answers the mail and that user message still gets no answer — it breaks 「사용자 입력이 먼저」 (PRD §3-9). So the mail turn does not release the debt (「답 없는 항목」 below). ★Where it is blocked = the path's Idle order (decision)★ — we do not add the debt to the kernel's busy fact (`inputs_pending`): the waiting list table is one set the core writes only from the registry (§5-0 「적는 순서」 · seq rule · doorbell) and the debt is not the registry but the path's turn field, so adding it would add a second writing side to that table and a third doorbell at the moment the debt is released (§10-5). Mail the kernel released earlier is held in the path anyway and takes this order.
    - ★In every case we do not load several items into one `input` of a `turn/start` (decision)★ — bundling them into one `userMessage` would make a single `clientId` and so break PRD §3-8.
  - `Connecting` → hold. When the gate opens (ADR-0204 — **after** the history chunk), follow the Idle rule. ★**Even if the history restore fails·is interrupted, the gate opens as long as the connection is alive — today's code already does that**★ (history restore is best-effort as a whole and the gate only looks at whether the connection stands — `transport.rs:42-44` · `:1491-1496`). So PRD §3-11 「대화는 이어받았는데 화면 이력 복원만 실패·중단되면 그대로 넘긴다」 (user decision Q6) stands with no new code.
  - ★**During settlement no turn is opened — user turn · mail turn · empty turn alike**★. When settlement closes the Idle rule runs again (「턴 끝 정산」 below). ★During a halt after error there is no turn opened on its own★ — what opens is only the turn of a message the user typed after the halt (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below).
  - ★「한 번에 턴 시작 하나」 (ADR-0193) is as it is★ — steer does not open a turn, and the request that opens one is still only `take_turn_locked`. What changes is one line of 「Active 동안 **아무것도** 안 보낸다」.
- **Mail:** `origin=Mail` (§5-0) is held until the end of the turn as today with no announce·steer, and at Idle ★**only when there is no user `Held`, no acceptance-unknown item, no follow-up turn debt, and neither settlement nor a halt after error**★ `turn/start` (carrying `clientUserMessageId` — the bubble appears with the consumption echo). Mail among itself is in arrival order. Since it is outside the list it does not fall under the user items' no-overtaking ban (mail held during Active does not block the steer of a user item).
  - ★**User input comes first (PRD §3-9 · user decision N6)**★ — if user items are held, mail waits until the turn those items went into ends, and if that turn has a user message it did not answer, until that follow-up turn ends (「빚이 우편보다 먼저인 이유」 above). If an acceptance-unknown item stands in the list, it waits until that leaves (late receipt · ✕ · agent end) — the kernel's busy decision reads the same fact (it stands in the registry as `Queued` · N8 no ceiling). Mail may be later than today, and the user accepted that delay. ★During a halt after error it waits until the user's next turn ends in success★ — the kernel holds it (`last_end_failed` — §5-0) and the path holds only mail it had already received before the halt · it is so even when the list is empty and the screen has no reason for it (user decision N11 · an accepted cost — 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below). Mail before reaching the path is held by the messaging kernel by the same idle definition (§5-0 「우편의 한가」).
- **`clientUserMessageId` in `turn/start` = id** (ADR-0198 · 0.156.1 measured — `clientId` is attached to the first input's echo). Direct · the next turn after a turn end · the first send after connecting · a turn the user opens during a halt after error take this road.
- **Steer pairing:** a new `Waiter::Steer{ id, gen }` is placed in the waiting table.
  - A **success response** (`{turnId}`) and ★a **deadline expiry** (`REQUEST_DEADLINE` 30 s — `transport.rs:194` · acceptance unknown)★ equally move it only to `awaiting_reply=false` — a success is an **acceptance** that comes 1–5 ms before the echo and is not a receipt (M6 — PRD §3-4), and an expiry is unknown. Either way, if the echo comes it is a receipt as usual, and if it does not it is acceptance unknown at turn end (settlement 2 below — the echo of a steer the vendor accepted comes before `turn/completed`, M10). Without the expiry branch, the item would stay waiting for a response forever and the whole codex queue would stop (§10-5). Even if an expired steer is processed late by the vendor, there is no double delivery — that request is tied to **that turn** by `expectedTurnId` and is rejected after the turn ends (−32600 · id mismatch — §3-2).
  - On an **error response** (only a steer's — an error response of `turn/start` is the `Dropped{Rejected}` of the five exits below), that item is returned to `Held` (to its place in typed order) and a 「steer 거절」 mark is put on that turn → during that turn we steer no more and go by `turn/start` at the Idle that follows. −32600 「no active turn to steer」 (the turn just ended — M6 measured) · review/compaction turns · id mismatch are all the same disposition (decision: on an id mismatch we could resend at once with the new turn id, but we do not build a retry loop without knowing that turn's state). **An item that returned with an error is not in the history** — the vendor rejected it (a steer after the turn's window has closed is rejected — vendor source 0.156.1 `turn_input.rs:634-638`). ★The error response may even come before `turn/completed` (M10 measured 0.1 ms earlier)★ — we do not assume the order. That turn is a segment where hand-over is impossible by the 「steer 거절」 mark, so both policies hold until the end of that turn — the same for items that newly came in during that turn (segment table first row · no list event). ★But if `cancel_asked` we do not hold it again and withdraw it (「✕」 below)★. A Direct item is not steered so it does not enter this branch (above). ★Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — if that message's echo has already come, a late error response cannot reverse the receipt: it does not revive and only releases the response obligation, with a warning + `refused_after_echo` counting (P2 review)★.
- ★**✕ — whatever stage the item is in, it is removed from the list (`withdraw` — both policies)**★. ✕ removes from the list and is not taking back from the agent (user decision 2026-09-25 — §0) — if it can be withdrawn it is withdrawn, and if it has already been handed over it is only removed from the list. Under the lock we look at that id's stage — hand-over and ✕ are ordered under the same state lock (a ✕ that comes after the writer moved `Held` to `InFlight` is on the handed-over side):
  - `Held` → remove the item → outside the lock `Dropped{Withdrawn}` → outcome (a) · command result `cancelled` (synchronous — §5-6). **There is no vendor call** — the vendor has never seen that message.
  - `InFlight` (already handed over — whether it overlapped the instant of hand-over or was handed over long ago in a segment that hands over at once) → set `cancel_asked` and `TooLate` → outside the lock chain `CancelAnswered{removed:false}` right onto `CancelRequested` → command result `requested`. ★If it is already `cancel_asked`, answer only `TooLate` with no event (command result `requested`)★ — codex's cancel does not take the input lock (§5-0), so if the window's and the LLM's ✕ overlap, both pass the registry check (「이미 취소 중」 — §5-6) and reach `withdraw`. The path field filters so that the second does not emit `CancelRequested` again, and the reducer's `Cancelling`+`CancelRequested` no-op row is the second defense (§5-2). In the list it leaves at once and is cancel-pending until the outcome (it is not drawn in windows — only the LLM list `cancelling`, §5-2). The outcome is decided by the usual road of a handed-over item: echo = (b) an end-of-conversation bubble · the vendor turned out not to have accepted (steer error response) = ★it is not sent again and is withdrawn★ `Dropped{Withdrawn}` → (a) · ★if acceptance becomes unknown (the five places of 「수락 모름」 below) at once `Dropped{Unknown}` → (c)★ — we wait no longer (「✕ 한 항목의 수락 모름」 below · if received late, revival → (b) · user decision N12 (a)) · interrupt·termination·rejection = (c). The reduction rule already tells those three apart (§5-2) — we add no new event for codex (decision).
  - `Unconfirmed` (acceptance unknown — below) → answer `TooLate` and, outside the lock, chain `Dropped{Unknown}` right onto `CancelRequested`·`CancelAnswered{removed:false}` → outcome (c) · command result `requested` · the path removes that id from pending and **wakes the writer** (mail held may flow — 「수락 모름」 below). If received late, revival stands an end-of-conversation bubble (outcome (b) — §5-2 · user decision N12 (a) — 「✕ 한 항목의 수락 모름」 below).
  - An id the path does not know (terminal or outside the list — an overlapping second ✕ that reached the `Held`·`Unconfirmed` that the first ✕ already removed) → `NotHeld` · no event (the command first filters unknown·terminal ids with `NOT_FOUND` at the registry check, and an id whose outcome came between that check and `withdraw` is also `NOT_FOUND` — §5-6).
  - ★**All three branches stand in the skeleton (`Immediate` — §9 P2)**★ — since ✕ uses neither a vendor call nor a boundary time, there is no reason to wait for measurement (P7). In `Immediate` most codex items are handed over at once, so ✕ is usually `TooLate` → outcome (b) (it leaves the list at once and if the echo comes, an end-of-conversation bubble), and the `Withdrawn` that withdraws the message arises only for held items in segments where hand-over is impossible (segment table first row — including items held during a halt after error). `AtEarliestBoundary` (P8) only widens the segments where `Withdrawn` arises to the tool segment (and the answer segment if M16 is green).
  - Even if the items held during a halt after error all leave by ✕, ★the halt is not released★ (user decision N11) — even if the list empties and the doorbell rings (§5-0), the kernel holds mail with `last_end_failed` (what has already entered the path is held by `halted`) and waits until the user's next turn ends in success (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below).
- **Consumption echo = `Delivered`:** the decoder `user_message` (`backend/codex/decoder.rs` `CodexAppServerDecoder::user_message` — edition 8's `:642` has shifted) uses the item's `clientId` **as the uuid** if it has one (and if not, as today the item `id` · ★implementation reflected — a `clientId` over 128 bytes is filtered out and not cut, and goes to the item `id` (`MAX_ID_BYTES`)★), and emits `Delivered{clientId}` **right before** the bubble event. It is the same pump thread so the order holds. The synthetic echo and the echo-back have the same uuid so the accumulator dedup leaves one copy. ★Edition 9 — `Delivered` is emitted once only from whichever of the two live doors (`item/started`·`item/completed`) comes first (if the item id exceeds the ceiling it cannot be paired and comes twice — its second is taken by the reducer's tombstone, §5-2) · the history door uses `clientId` only as the bubble uuid and does not emit `Delivered` (a past record is not a receipt event)★. The path reader also removes that `clientId` from `pending` (generation discrimination — whatever stage it came from, `Delivered` wins, turn-end-after rule below). The echo `item/started` also closes boundary-open (the vendor has passed the check) — it is neither tool nor output so the segment after it is 「그 밖」 (at once), and a following output item opens the answer segment (segment tracking above).
- **End-of-turn disposition (`turn/completed` — by state):**
  - ★**Where the disposition runs = the moment that `turn/completed` is attributed to our turn — two**★. ① If the turn id is already known, when that line is received (the reader's `turn/completed` branch → `end_turn_if`). ② ★**A termination that came before the response (an early termination) is also a turn end**★: a `turn/completed` that came while the turn id was not known is not ended by the path but held (`State.early_completions`), and when the following `turn/start` response fixes the id, if the id matches it ends the turn right there (`Reader::resolve_at`). ★The line numbers were the edition 8 reading baseline and shifted by several thousand lines after the implementation, so they were changed to symbols★. The dispositions below (interrupt discard · settlement · failure — including setting and releasing the halt after error) run **in that release too** exactly the same — if they did not, that turn's `InFlight` would remain with no outcome and pending would not empty (the same accident as the five exits below). For that, the held record must hold the state of that line (`completed`·`interrupted`·`failed`) together — today it holds only the translated boundary (`boundary`) (edition 8 source reading — whether to read it from there or add a field is for implementation). ★Implementation reflected — we added a field: `EarlyCompletion{turn_id, boundary, close: TurnClose}`★. The user item handed over in a turn ended by an early termination is only the message of that `turn/start` (no steer went out because it was before the id — the `Active{turn_id: None}` rule above).
  - **`interrupted`** → all `InFlight`·`Held` are `Dropped{Interrupted}` → discard (a Direct item is only removed from pending with no event — the bubble stays, §5-2 「거절만 지운다」). ★Implementation reflected (2026-09-26 — orchestrator source reading) — 「전부」 is **a list item**: held mail (`Held` · `origin=Mail`) and items with no identifier remain even through an interrupt and go out at the next Idle (「우편」 below — we never handed it to the vendor, and discarding it would lose the mail · this line and the 「버림」 wording of §5-9 diverged from the 「우편」 line, so the mail line was followed). Mail handed over in that turn is removed with no event. An item already disposed of before announce (death mark) does not overwrite that cause★. An acceptance-unknown item from a previous turn (`Unconfirmed`) is not touched — it is not an item of that turn, and an interrupt does not change whether the vendor has that message (there are only three ways out — 「수락 모름」 below). The halt after error is not touched either — an interrupt neither sets nor releases the halt (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below · the turn table also folds that end as 「그 밖」 — §5-0). The vendor discards what was handed over with neither echo nor history (M6), and PRD §3-7 also discards waiting items that could not be sent by the interrupt. Today an interrupt reaches only over the wire (§1-2 · the turn-interrupt operation is the next feature — PRD §2).
  - **`completed`** → **end-of-turn settlement** (below).
  - ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — an unrecognizable status · no status (including `inProgress`) → `TurnClose::Unknown`**★: the item disposition is the same as `completed` (a list item with no echo is acceptance unknown · if there is a steer waiting for a response, settlement) and ★**it neither sets nor releases the halt after error, and there is no follow-up turn debt**★ (even if a turn that cleared a debt ends this way, it is not counted in `unpaid`). Reason (P2 review doc-aware F2): the kernel classifier folds the same line as 「그 밖의 끝」 (`TurnEndKind::Other`) and leaves `last_end_failed` as it is, so if the path released the halt here, the halts of the two layers would diverge · if we issue an empty turn after an end whose outcome we do not know, the model may repeat the earlier answer (M9 control group). Edition 8 did not write this branch, and the first landing read it as `completed`.
  - ★**`failed` → run the same settlement but do not open a turn on its own (PRD §3-10 · user decision Q8)**★. A `InFlight` with no echo is decided by settlement (error response = `Held` — list items only · other = acceptance unknown — it stays in the list · a Direct is removed from pending with no event, 「분류와 방출」). Two differences from normal completion:
    - ① **We do not set a follow-up turn debt** — a message that became a bubble in that turn but got no answer (including a message sent while idle) is left as it is (PRD §3-1 exception). Only a list item that returned with an error response waits under the halt of ② below, as `Held`.
    - ② **We set the halt after error** (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below — including releasing an early exit). The `Held` that remains after settlement closes stands in the list as it is (a held item in a segment where hand-over is impossible so ✕ withdraws the message — both policies · from skeleton P2 · no list event), and if the user types a message after the halt, it goes into that turn together — **the remaining items first, the new message at the end, in one turn, each separately** (PRD AC24 · the decision order for that message = 「멈춤 중 새 항목의 판정」 above). A user message that arrives during settlement is also a message arrived after the halt — when settlement closes, that turn opens at once. ★The old ③ (the rule that told with 「마지막 `failed` 턴의 세대」 so as not to pay the previous turn's debt with an empty turn after a failed turn) was withdrawn★ — the debt never stands together with the halt (the 「오류 뒤 멈춤」 debt below).
    - ★What differs from today★: today after `failed` the held input goes out at once from Idle as the next `turn/start`. This feature stops that — user input · empty turn · mail, all of them, until the user's next turn ends in success (「오류 뒤 멈춤」 below · user decision N11).
- ★**A turn that ends on our side without `turn/completed` — the five exits**★. If `turn/start` ends with `end_turn_if`, `turn/completed` does not come so the disposition above does not run — if we do not close it separately, that `InFlight` item remains in the list with no outcome and **pending never empties** (new messages are all classified as waiting). The user item handed over in that turn is only the message of that `turn/start` (no steer went out because it was before receiving the turn id — the `Active{turn_id: None}` rule above). So we do not open settlement and close that one item at once. ★And we set the halt after error (below — user decision N11: a request-stage failure is also an odd end)★ — the same whether that `turn/start` is a user turn, a Direct turn, an empty turn or a mail turn.
  - (The `:NNNN` in the next two lines are the edition 8 reading-baseline lines — the present places are write failure = `turn_write_failed` · response branch = `Reader::resolve_at` · response deadline = `sweep_deadlines`, all closed with `end_turn_if` · the source of truth of the disposition table = the `State::close_turn_items` doc)
  - **Write failure** (`:1701`) · **error response** (`:2191`) → `Dropped{Rejected}` — it did not reach the vendor or the vendor rejected it. A write failure of `turn/steer` is the same.
  - **Response deadline** (`:1278`) · **response decode failure** (`:2180`) · **turn id ceiling exceeded** (`:2111`) → acceptance unknown → **acceptance unknown with no event** (`Unconfirmed` — it stays in the list, user decision N10 · 「수락 모름」 below · if the item was ✕-ed, at once `Dropped{Unknown}` → (c)). PRD §3-4 · AC9 keeps in the list an item whose receipt never comes, and PRD §3-7's causes of discarding do not include 「모름」. ★We do not probe★ (this design has no probe — settlement 2 below): if the message of `turn/start` was accepted, **at the start of that turn** an echo carrying `clientId` comes (M6) — the late echo closes it as a receipt · for a decode failure·long id the server already accepted so the echo comes soon. We also do not return it to `Held` — unlike a steer, `turn/start` is not tied to a turn, so if the server processes it late it becomes a **double delivery** with the message sent again.
  - ★Even if a late echo comes we do not set a follow-up turn debt★ (so it is for every acceptance-unknown item — 「수락 모름」 below) — the message of `turn/start` is answered by the turn that request opened. That turn may still be running on the server (we counted it as ended), and issuing an empty `turn/start` there would become an empty steer (§3-2).
  - A Direct message with a write failure·error response is `Dropped{Rejected}` — the accumulator erases the already-drawn synthetic echo bubble without a notification (PRD §3-5 · user decision Q1 ④ — §5-2). If acceptance is unknown, it is only removed from pending with no event (the bubble stands and the late echo is deduped — unrelated to N10: it never went on the list). Mail has no event since it is outside the list.
  - ★**Invariant: there is not one item waiting for a response (`InFlight`) in an Idle whose settlement has closed**★ — all roads by which a turn ends (`turn/completed` ~~three~~ four (★edition 9 — including an unrecognizable status `TurnClose::Unknown`★) — including the place that releases an early exit (turn-end disposition ② above) · these five exits · stream closed = `finish` synthesis) move that turn's `InFlight` to `Delivered`·`Held`·`Unconfirmed` (list items only — a Direct is removed from pending with no event, 「분류와 방출」)·`Dropped` or hand it to settlement, and settlement closes within the deadline. ★The `Unconfirmed` that remains in Idle waits for nothing★ — there is no outstanding request, and it is visible in the list with a ✕. What it blocks is mail alone (N8 — visible in the list · it does not block later user messages, user decision N13 (b) — hand-over rule above). It is released the moment that item leaves (the writer is woken — 「수락 모름」 below). The item disposition of these five exits differs from the 「오류로 끝난 턴」 of PRD §3-10 — we do not even know whether the turn stood, so we close that one item at once with no settlement (§3-10 is the case where the vendor told us with `turn/completed(failed)`). ★After that they are the same — both set the halt after error (below)★.
- ★**Halt after error (`halted`) — after an oddly ended turn, nothing is put in on its own until the user's next turn succeeds (user decision N11 (b) · the widened principle)**★. User: 「어쨋든 본질은 가능하면 자동으로 되고, 거기에 유저게 우선한다는 얘기지」 · 「어쨋든 모종의 이유로 이상하게 종료될때 채팅 입력이 성공하거나 어쨋든 재개 신호가 오면 뿌린다는거잖아」 (confirmed 「ㅇㅇ」). One field = `halted: Option<도착 번호>` (outside the path `TurnState` — condition ① of 「벤더가 보낸 에코」 above). ★This field blocks only turns the path opens by itself★ — the door by which mail enters the path is the kernel's `last_end_failed`, and the codex classifier folds the `TurnEnd{Failed}` of the same trigger (§5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」).
  - **When it is set (trigger):** the moment our turn ends oddly — `turn/completed(failed)` (including releasing an early exit — turn-end disposition above) · the five exits of `turn/start` (above). It does not tell turn kinds apart — user turn · Direct turn · empty turn · mail turn. Value = the arrival number at that moment (if already set, it is set again at that value). **What is not a trigger:** `interrupted` (today's behavior — neither sets nor releases) · a steer write failure·error response (that turn continues — steer pairing above) · agent end (discarded together with the path — closing below). ★The halt `halted` is not set for codex below the floor★ — that path runs today's behavior together with this whole feature (「하한 판정」 · `delivery_ack = Unavailable` below · user decision N1 (a)) · the mail halt stands regardless of version (a kernel fact — 「우편」 below).
  - **While it stands:** no turn is opened on its own at Idle — empty turn · mail turn · the user `Held` that had been held from before the halt. There is one turn that opens — **if a user message that arrived after the marker is in pending** (Queued or Direct), it is the user turn opened with that head (the remaining items first, the new message at the end, in one turn, each separately — PRD AC24 · the Idle order above). If that turn runs, the hand-over rule is as usual — what the halt blocks is only opening a turn at Idle. ★We decide by the present state (decision — the old rule of waiting after an error turn as it is)★: each time the Idle rule runs (when settlement closes · when a new message arrives · after ✕) we re-read 「표식 뒤 사용자 글이 pending 에 있나」 — if that message leaves by ✕ before the turn opens, no turn opens (if we opened a turn on the arrival event, the remaining items would go out on their own at that time and break PRD §3-10 · AC24 — §10-5).
  - **Release:** the moment `turn/completed(completed)` comes during the halt (including releasing an early exit). Since the only turn that opens during the halt is the user turn above, its end is 「사용자의 다음 턴이 성공으로 끝났다」 — there is no field to record the turn kind separately. When settlement closes, the Idle order runs as usual (user `Held` → debt → mail). If that turn also ends oddly, the marker is set again at the arrival number of that moment — the items left after that turn again wait for the next message. ★It is not released when the held items all leave by ✕ · nor when time passes and a limit lifts★ — the alternative of continuing on its own at a limit-release signal was rejected (user: 「아냐 일단 복잡하게 하지말자」 · §10-5). Agent end discards it together. The kernel side is also released at the same turn end (the classifier's clean end → after the table update `turn_ended` — §5-0).
  - **Debt:** it never stands together with the halt — the debt stands only in the settlement of a `completed` turn (settlement 3 below) and is erased the moment the Idle whose settlement closed issues a user turn or an empty turn (debt release below). If that turn ends oddly, the halt is set — the empty turn is not issued again, and since that message is already in the history the user's next turn sees it together (「내는 순간 푼다」 below). ★The old `failed` ③ (the rule that told with 「마지막 `failed` 턴의 세대」 so as not to pay the previous turn's debt with an empty turn after a failed turn) was withdrawn because it has nowhere to be used★ — the debt that rule blocked (what a late echo during or after a failed turn used to set) no longer stands (「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」 below).
  - **Mail:** the door by which mail enters is the kernel's `last_end_failed` (§5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」) — during the halt the kernel does not release mail to the path and holds it by name (the ledger is true · when the agent ends it goes to the next incarnation). ★The remaining gap (rare · accepted)★: mail the kernel had already released to the path right before the failure is held by the path (the 「우편」 Idle condition above) and goes out when the halt is released — only what was in flight at that moment (§5-9).
  - ★**Accepted cost**★: the state in which the list is empty and only mail does not go in continues until the user types something, and the screen has no reason for it. A mail notification (「우편 왔습니다 — 확인하세요」) is backlog outside this feature. Practice ground = `docs/research/queued-input-after-failed-turn-2026-09-26.md` (codex automatically submitting queued input after a failure was reported as a defect — openai/codex#24443).
- ★**End-of-turn settlement — once per turn**★. At the `turn/completed(completed · failed · ★9판 — 뜻 모를 status 도★)` of turn T (including the place that releases an early exit) we open one settlement, and **after deciding all of that turn's items** we decide the follow-up turn **once** — if we set a follow-up turn per item, when one turn has several items with no answer several follow-up turns would open and answers would overlap in the same history (§10-5). ~~★If T has no `InFlight` without an echo, settlement closes as soon as it opens★~~ (★edition 9 — wrong: even if the echo has already come, if there is a steer waiting for a response, settlement stands★). ★Implementation reflected (2026-09-26 · P2 review) — settlement **stands** only when T has a steer request that did not get a response (whether the echo came or not — `steers_owed`)★: an item with no echo that already got its response is decided at the turn end at once as acceptance unknown and closes · an unrecognizable status (`TurnClose::Unknown`) also runs settlement like `completed` · a turn opened by `turn/start` (five exits · early exit) has no steer waiting for a response so settlement does not stand · an interrupt does not open settlement. So settlement stands **even with an empty list** (when only the response of an echoed steer remains).
  - ★**One settlement deadline — measured from the turn end**★: response collecting (1) is within one deadline set at `turn/completed` receipt (the release if an early exit) — the value uses `REQUEST_DEADLINE` (30 s) as it is (decision — we do not make a new constant). Expiry = all items that still have no response become acceptance unknown (2 below · an item that was ✕-ed is `Dropped{Unknown}`, 「수락 모름」 below · list items only — a Direct is removed from pending with no event, 「분류와 방출」) → go to 3·4. A response that comes late after the deadline is discarded as a no-op by generation discrimination (a late echo is a receipt — 「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」 below).
  1. **Collect the responses.** If among T's `InFlight` there is one that is `awaiting_reply` (★implementation reflected — all of T's steer requests that did not get a response, whether the echo came or not★), wait for that response (or that request's deadline expiry) — error = `Held` (the vendor rejected it — it is not in the history so the `turn/start` of a later turn is the first send, not the double delivery of PRD §3-7 · if `cancel_asked` it is withdrawn and not held again — steer pairing above) · success·expiry = 2 below. Reason: before the response we do not know whether it was received — a late steer's −32600 sometimes comes before `turn/completed` (M10 measured 0.1 ms earlier — we do not assume the order).
  2. **The rest with no echo are acceptance unknown — we do not probe (decision · M10 green)**. The vendor records a steer it received before the turn ends (waiting items the sampling did not consume are written before `TurnComplete` — vendor source 0.156.1 `core/src/tasks/mod.rs:655`·`:673` · where the window closes `:640-650` · the last check of waiting items `core/src/session/turn.rs:555-567`) and that echo comes before `turn/completed` (M10 — all three 「기록만」 hits were 4.5–4.8 ms earlier · it carries the id of the turn that received it). So a handed-over item with no echo at turn end was either rejected by the vendor (1 above — error response) or we did not get an answer (deadline). **An item with a success response but no echo** is a branch that should not exist per the source — together with a deadline expiry we leave it as `Unconfirmed` (it stays in the list with no event · no resend · no debt · warning log + counting · an item that was ✕-ed is `Dropped{Unknown}`, 「수락 모름」 below · list items only — a Direct is removed from pending with no event: so that an item outside the list does not remain and block later messages·mail with an empty list). Alternative rejected: an end-of-turn probe (the old design that walked the history from the end with `thread/items/list` and told presence from absence with `clientId`) — the echo of a received message already comes before the turn end, so per the source nothing is left for a probe to tell apart, and walking · pages · stop conditions · truncation would greatly increase fields and rules (state `Probing` · waiting table `Waiter::Probe` · synthetic bubble). ★Candidate to add later★ — if the warning counter actually counts 「성공인데 에코 없음」, revive the probe then (in M10 that list carried the item in the head of the `limit:40` page and the `limit:2` walk passed that turn in 2 pages — the ground for when it is revived).
  3. **Decide the follow-up turn once — `completed` turns only.** If T has even one **receipt that got no answer** (decision = 「답 없는 항목」 below), set the follow-up turn debt `follow_up_owed = T` — **one per turn**, not per item. A `failed` turn does not set it (above). ★Implementation reflected — **only messages carried by `turn/steer`** enter the decision (orchestrator decision — 「답 없는 항목」 below) · an unrecognizable status also does not set it★.
  4. **Close the settlement** → the Idle rule runs again: if there is a user `Held`, that turn · if not and there is a debt, an empty input `turn/start` once (M9) · only when neither and there is no acceptance-unknown item, the mail turn. The first two are **a user turn·empty turn issued after T ended**, so the moment they are issued the debt is released (below) — the mail turn opens only after the debt is released (「빚이 우편보다 먼저인 이유」 above). If `failed`, the halt after error stands (above) so only the turn of a user message that arrived after the halt opens.
  - ★**Two reasons not to open a turn during settlement**★: ① for the follow-up turn decision to be once, all the decisions must be collected ② while the response of an item typed earlier returns late as `Held`, if a later item's `turn/start` went out first, **the order typed would reverse** (PRD §3-1 「친 순서대로」). The settlement length is 0 if there is no response being waited for (M10 — the success response of a received steer came before the turn end), and the worst is the settlement deadline. Mail also waits meanwhile (a delay the user accepted — PRD §3-9). User input that comes in during settlement is ~~Queued because the list is not empty (an item waiting for a response is in the list)~~ Queued per PRD §3-5 and stands behind the FIFO (it is a segment where hand-over is impossible so it holds — both policies). ★Edition 9 — the ground that was struck was wrong: settlement can stand with an empty list (settlement head above). The reason it is Queued is that the Direct decision requires 「정산 없음」 (`State::push_pending` · 「분류와 방출」 above)★.
  - ★**Ground = M10 green · if it collapses**★ — this settlement leans on 「받은 steer 의 에코가 `turn/completed` 앞에 온다」 (2 above — measured · vendor source). On a vendor version where that collapses, a received item stays in the list at turn end as acceptance unknown and closes as a receipt by the late echo — nothing is lost and nothing overlaps, and gray items and the warning counter increase (then we revive the probe — the candidate in 2 above).
- ★**If a receipt comes after the turn end — close it as a receipt and do not set a debt (decision — the simple side)**★: the echo of a received message comes before `turn/completed` (settlement 2 above — M10), so an echo after the turn end is only the late receipt of an acceptance-unknown item · the revival of a ✕-ed acceptance-unknown · a vendor that broke that order. If after T ended the echo (`clientId`) of some item comes —
  - if that item is in pending (a `InFlight` waiting for a response during settlement · a `Held` that returned with an error response and has **not yet gone out** · acceptance unknown) → close it as `Delivered`, remove it from pending, and **wake the writer** (held mail may flow by the rule — 「수락 모름」 below) — if it was `Held`, that `turn/start` does not go out. ★Implementation reflected — if it is the echo of a steer waiting for a response during settlement, the item leaves as a receipt but **settlement keeps waiting for that response** (a receipt does not release the response obligation — `steers_owed` · what closes settlement is a response · deadline · write failure · settlement deadline · path closing)★. It is an item that was standing in the list so it is an ordinary receipt (not a revival — the registry is `Queued` → `Delivered`).
  - if it is an item already closed by ✕, it is not in pending — the path has nothing to do and the bubble is set up by revival (「✕ 한 항목의 수락 모름」 below · §5-2).
  - ★**We do not set a debt — warning log + counting**★. The model already has that message, so the next user turn sees it together from the history. The old design (the idempotent rule that set T's debt because a receipt after the turn end had got no answer · the acceptance-unknown `debt_turn` · the hidden record after ✕) is a defensive branch that costs fields and rules, so it was passed on as a **candidate to add later** — if an empty turn is issued for a message already answered, the model repeats the earlier answer (M9 control group), so the debt stands only when we know it got no answer (settlement 3).
  - ★That echo itself does not touch the turn table★ — T has ended so the path is Idle or it disagrees with the new turn's id, so the attribution gate emits it as `emit_without_turn_observation` (「벤더가 보낸 에코」 above). The bubble of a revival is also this echo. ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — after a receipt that came when there was no open turn, one display-only boundary is emitted**★ (`TurnEnd{turn_id: None, Completed}` — also `emit_without_turn_observation` · if a new turn opened in between and the receipt was counted as that turn's progress, that turn's end is the boundary · `Reader::note_delivered` announces it). Reason: that bubble is an end-of-conversation bubble so the frontend turns on the waiting indicator (§5-7 — `placeUserBubble` opens a turn), and only a turn boundary turns it off, but a late receipt sets no debt so there may be no turn following — without a boundary 「Wait」 stays on. The reason for emitting through the door with no observation: if emitted through the usual door, the classifier folds it as a clean end and erases the earlier error end (`last_end_failed`) — the mail that should stay halted flows. A halted Idle incarnation is the same. An unsuppressed vendor echo of an unknown id (revival after ✕ · Direct · mail) is also the same.
  - **If that item's `turn/start` has already gone out** the vendor held the same message twice — it cannot be undone, so only a warning log + counting are left (a rejected steer is not in the history — vendor source `turn_input.rs:634-638` · this branch is the case where that is wrong).
  - The structure keeps two rules: **per item, a hand-over after the turn end is at most once** (stage check — generation discrimination) · **per turn end, a follow-up turn is at most one** (the debt is one field that stands only in settlement 3 of a `completed` turn and is erased **the moment** a user·empty `turn/start` is issued — irrelevant to the exit of that request · if that request ends oddly, the halt after error stops an empty turn from being issued again, debt release below).
- ★**Acceptance unknown (`Unconfirmed`) — it stays in the list (user decision N10)**★. The places where the acceptance of a handed-over list item becomes unknown are five — the response deadline · decode failure · long turn id of `turn/start` (the five exits above) · the settlement deadline (the steer response does not come) · a success response but no echo at turn end (settlement 2 above — a branch that should not exist per the source). There that item moves to `Unconfirmed` **with no event** — in the registry it is `Queued` as it is, so it stands gray in every window with a ✕. User: 「갑자기 목록이 사라져서 이상할것같은데 응답이 없으면 유저가 뭘 할 수있다고 스택에서 빼? 목록에서 뺀다는건 이유가 있어야되는거아냐」 (PRD §3-4).
  - **What the path does:** it does not send again (double delivery — PRD §3-7 · the reason is as in the five exits·settlement above). It keeps no request outstanding — there is no response being waited for. **User messages typed after it are handed over in order as usual** (user decision N13 (b) — hand-over rule above) — a new message stands at the end as Queued since the list is not empty (PRD §3-5) and is handed over after it by the usual rule. It does not block turns either — a user turn and the empty turn of a follow-up turn debt go out as usual. **What it blocks is mail alone** — an item stands in the registry so the kernel's `inputs_pending` is true (§5-0 · N8 no ceiling), and the path's Idle order also does not open a mail turn while an acceptance-unknown item exists (Idle order above · 「우편」).
  - **Three ways out — there are no others:** ① **late receipt** — the echo of that `clientId` → the decoder's `Delivered` + the vendor bubble · the path removes it from pending (「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」 above) ② **✕** — `withdraw` answers `TooLate` and chains `Dropped{Unknown}` right onto `CancelRequested`·`CancelAnswered{removed:false}` → outcome (c) · the path removes that id from pending (user decision N12 (a) — below) ③ **agent end** — the `finish` synthesis `Dropped{AgentEnded}` (§5-3). An interrupt (`interrupted`) discards only that turn's items — an acceptance-unknown item from a previous turn remains (turn-end disposition above). ★**The moment it leaves by ① · ② the writer is woken**★ — the path announces in that lock span and runs the Idle rule again so that held mail goes out by that rule (later user messages were never blocked and a late echo sets no debt — above). If we do not wake it, mail stops until new input comes — with the list empty there is no event to wake it on its own. ③ ends together with the path so there is nothing to wake.
  - ★**The period the path holds that id = until one of the three comes — no count·time ceiling (decision)**★. It is one body with the item standing in the list: the path holds only while the list holds, the count does not exceed the number of gray items the user sees, and ✕ and agent end empty both together. If we put a ceiling, only the path would forget an item standing in the list first, and while the list (the kernel's `inputs_pending`) blocks mail the path would issue the mail it holds — the answers of the two layers would diverge (§10-5).
  - ★**Follow-up turn debt — not set for acceptance-unknown items (decision)**★. The debt stands only when we know 「기록만 됐는데 답을 못 받았다」 — acceptance unknown does not even know whether it was received, so it cannot know that (if it was not received the model has nothing to answer in an empty turn, and if it was already answered the model repeats the earlier answer — M9 control group). We do not set it even if the late echo comes (「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」 above — candidate to add later). User: 「복잡한것보다 약간 양보하면서 단순한 방향으로 하되 나중에 문제되면 기능 추가하면 되지」.
- ★**Acceptance unknown of a ✕-ed item — at once (c), and revive if the echo is late (user decision N12 (a))**★. User: 「끝난걸로쳐. 유저가 x눌렀을때 우연찮게 들어가는건 그냥 그렇다 치겠지」. The two orders are the same — whether an item ✕-ed after hand-over (`cancel_asked`) becomes unknown in the five places above, or ✕ is pressed on an acceptance-unknown item, the path emits `Dropped{Unknown}` at once (→ `Discarded{Unknown}` — outcome (c)) and removes that id from pending — at the moment of ✕ it also leaves the LLM list (it does not pass through 「취소 중」) and when the registry empties, mail flows (unless it is a halt after error — above).
  - **Why we do not wait longer:** the user's ✕ is already the reason to remove it from the list (PRD §3-4), and if we waited, a cancel-pending item visible in no window would hold `inputs_pending` and block mail with the cause invisible (N8 — no ceiling · §10-5).
  - **Late echo = revival:** if the reducer that discards because it is terminal and the accumulator that draws the vendor bubble diverge, **the list outcome is 「버려짐」 but a bubble stands in the conversation**. So the reducer takes a `Delivered` that came to a tombstone whose cause is `Unknown` as `Delivered` (§5-2 — a transition that overturns a terminal · one definition set), and the bubble is drawn by the vendor echo (outcome (b) — PRD §3-6 「늦게 받히면 말풍선」 · the turn table is not touched — 「벤더가 보낸 에코」 above). Reason: `Unknown` was 「모른다」 and the echo is a **fact** the vendor gave — the side where a fact beats unknown makes the conversation screen (the message the model actually received) and the list outcome agree. Alternative rejected: suppress the bubble and keep 「버려짐」 (the message the model received disappears from the screen and the answer stands without context — §10-5).
  - ★**We do not put a debt decision — no hidden record (decision — the simple side)**★: the revived message was already held by the model, so the next user turn sees it together from the history (warning log + counting) — the same disposition as the late echo of an acceptance-unknown item that was not ✕-ed (「수락 모름」 above). The old design (moving an id removed from pending into an out-of-list hidden record `withdrawn_unknown` and opening an empty turn on the late echo) needed one field and three rules (writing · using at the echo · erasing at a user·empty turn), so it was passed on as a candidate to add later.
- **Item with no answer (PRD §3-1 · AC6 — codex):** decision = after the `Delivered` of that item, `turn/completed(completed)` came with **not one sampling start of that turn** (the `item/started` of output items `agentMessage`·`reasoning`·`plan` — told apart by turn id, M6) — the 「기록만」 branch has that shape (M10 — the echo comes 4.5–4.8 ms before the turn end and there is no sampling after). Disposition = that turn's **follow-up turn debt** (settlement 3 above). A message whose receipt came after the turn end is outside the decision (「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」 above). A `failed` turn is also outside the decision (PRD §3-1 exception — it is left as it is).
  - ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26 — orchestrator decision · delegated) — the debt decision is made only for messages carried by `turn/steer`**★ (`Stage::InFlight.steered` · `State::unanswered`). Taken literally, the decision above also catches messages carried by `turn/start` (Direct · Held head · a message resent after rejection) — if that turn ends after the echo with no output item, an empty `turn/start` would go out in operation and the model would repeat the earlier answer (M9 control group). Reason: a turn `turn/start` opened always samples its own input first, and M10's 「기록만」 is a steer phenomenon · even without this branch it is no worse than today. Where it is decided: if the receipt is of a message handed over by steer to **that open turn**, write `unanswered = gen` (`Reader::note_delivered`), and the output item `item/started` of that turn erases it (`Reader::track_segment`) · if it is standing when the turn end is `completed` it is a debt (`State::close_turn_items` — if settlement is standing, deferred to `Settling.owes` and set when it closes). With this decision the debt statements of §5-9 · §7 are also limited to messages put in by steer.
  - ★**Debt release = the moment a user turn or an empty turn is issued after T ended**★ — erase, in the very lock span in which `take_turn_locked` decides to write the `Held` item's `turn/start` · the turn of a newly sent Direct message · the empty input `turn/start`. That message was already in the history when T ended, so a turn of that kind issued after T sees it together. ★**A mail turn does not release the debt**★ — the model answers the mail and that user message still gets no answer (PRD §3-9 · 「빚이 우편보다 먼저인 이유」 above). The debt stands only in settlement 3 and the Idle whose settlement closed opens the debt before mail, so there is no case where a mail turn runs with a debt standing. ★The old generation decision (the check against the 「마지막 사용자·빈 턴을 낸 세대」 field) was withdrawn★ — the road by which a debt stands after the turn end (the late echo) is gone, so there is no need to ask whether the turn that would pay it has already gone out after the debt stood.
    - ★**Release the moment it is issued — if that turn ends oddly the halt after error takes over (decision)**★: if the turn that used the debt (empty turn · user turn) ends oddly (`failed` · five exits), the halt stands (above) so the empty turn is not issued again — that message is already in the history so the user's next turn sees it together. It is the same behavior as the user answer N11 (b) 「빚은 남고 다음 사용자 글의 턴이 갚는다」 — even if the debt field were left, nothing reads it during the halt, and the moment the user turn that releases the halt is issued it is erased anyway. The loop in which an empty turn goes out again without rest while the vendor rejects (§10-5) is blocked in two layers — release at issue · the halt. A turn of `turn/start` that ended with acceptance unknown may still be running on the server and issuing an empty `turn/start` there would become an empty steer (§3-2 · five exits above), and the halt blocks that too. If the request that used the debt ends oddly, a warning log + counting. Alternative rejected: release the empty turn's debt when that turn ends with `completed` — the behavior is the same but it adds one field to carry the generation that issued the empty turn until that turn's end. ★Implementation reflected — the release place is still 「내는 순간」. However, to emit the warning + counting of the sentence just before, we have to know the turn that cleared the debt until that turn's end, so that field stands **for warning only** (`debt_paid_by` — if that turn ends with `failed` · five exits, `Anomalies.unpaid` + warning · the empty turn is not issued again · cleared when it ends). It is the same field as the one the rejected alternative was said to add, but the behavior (the release timing) is not the rejected alternative★.
  - When a debt is standing and the path is **Idle, has no settlement, has no user `Held`, and is not in a halt after error** (the Idle order above — the debt stands when the settlement of a `completed` turn closes, so at that moment the halt is already released), an empty input `turn/start` once (the debt is released the moment it is issued — above) — it comes first even if there is mail held. ★**M9 green** (measured 2026-09-26 · codex-cli 0.156.1)★: `turn/start{threadId, input: []}` is accepted (4/4 — about 50 ms after the response `turn/started` → sampling → `turn/completed(completed)` · there is neither a `userMessage` item nor an echo). If a user message with no answer is in the history, the empty turn answers exactly that message (3/3). ★Control group — if an empty turn is issued when there is no message without an answer, the model repeats the earlier answer (a visible duplicate)★ — so the debt stands only when we know it really got no answer (settlement 3). ★**During Active we never issue an empty `turn/start`**★ — a `turn/start` in progress is handled as a steer (§3-2), so it would become an empty steer.
  - We do not hand over the same message again and do not draw a new bubble (it is already Delivered). Since M9 is green, §10-4 N3 (when there is no means to open a turn to answer) does not arise — not filling by resending is as it was. claude has no such branch (the fold is **before** the next model request so a folded message is answered by that turn — only an error turn is an exception, PRD §3-10).
- ★**Deadline and closing — one branch of the new waiting table**★: `Waiter::Steer` is placed **as a branch** in the `match` on both sides — `sweep_deadlines` (`transport.rs:1263-1283`) and stream closing (`transport.rs:1839-1848`).
  - Deadline: `Steer` → `awaiting_reply=false` (the same as a success response — steer pairing above) · `TurnStart` → the 「응답 시한」 of the five exits above. All with a warning log. ★The settlement deadline is not the waiting table's but the time of the settlement field — the same `sweep_deadlines` sees them together★.
  - Closing: we do not touch the items — the end of the stream is the end of that incarnation (the next incarnation does not take over this path — `transport.rs:1826-1828` comment · `finish` runs at the terminal point), and the remaining non-terminal items are closed by the `finish` synthesis with `Dropped{AgentEnded}` (we keep one owner of the end disposition — §5-3). An open settlement·halt after error is discarded (there is no turn to open after closing). debug log.
- **When a disposition meets a pre-announce item:** the interrupt·connection end·`turn/start` rejection dispositions do **not** emit directly `Dropped` for a `announced=false` item — they only attach 「죽음 표시(원인)」 to that item, and the announcing side chains `Dropped{원인}` **right after** emitting `Queued`. So `Dropped` never stands on the ring before that `Queued` (the reducer's tombstone is the second defense — §5-2).
- **Connecting (hydration):** input during connecting stands in the list as `Queued` (after the floor decision — above · it is a segment where hand-over is impossible so it holds — a pressed ✕ withdraws the message, both policies · PRD §3-11) → when the gate opens (ADR-0204 — **after** the history chunk) `turn/start` → consumption echo → the bubble stands **below** the restored history. ★What ADR-0198's 「영향」 section left open, 「에코가 복원 이력보다 앞이냐 뒤냐」, is closed by this★. The gate also opens when only the history restore fails and the conversation continued (hand-over rule above `Connecting`). If the resume itself fails, the incarnation ends at the terminal point (ADR-0082) and the `finish` synthesis discards it.
- **Floor decision (M8):** `thread.cliVersion` → if absent, the first semver of `userAgent` → if both fail, below the floor. If below the floor it is today's behavior as it is (user decision N1 (a) — PRD R6 · `delivery_ack = Unavailable`). ★Implementation reflected — floor = 0.140.0 (`CLIENT_MESSAGE_ID_FLOOR`) · where it is decided = after the writer writes the session id, before the history restore · gate opening (`writer_loop` → `settle_floor`) · user items that came in before the decision are held as 「아직 안 정함」 and the decision re-decides all of them under the same lock · if below the floor all items lose their identifier (0 events · no `AckUnavailable` · no halt after error either)★. The 「`turn/start` 를 쓰는 순간을 `Delivered` 로」 that edition 1 chose on its own was withdrawn because it diverged from PRD §3-4 (「써 넣은 것은 받음이 아니다」) (§10-5).

### 5-6. Control surface (LLM-first)

- **Command bus (`agent` crate `commands.rs` — the CLI `/control/agent` and the bus envelope take it together):**
  - `agent.listQueuedInputs` — `#[effect(Read)]` · args `{ target: String }` → `{ inputs: Vec<QueuedInputRow{ id, text, state, cancel }>, as_of_seq: Option<u64>, epoch: u32, stopped_after_error: bool }` (non-terminal only, state ∈ `queued`·`unconfirmed`·`cancelling`). ★`cancelling` is visible only in this list★ — windows do not draw cancel-pending (PRD §3-6 · §5-2). The LLM reads that item as `cancelling` until the outcome (PRD AC11). `unconfirmed` is also told apart only in this list (below).
    - ★**Snapshot seq `as_of_seq` (decision)**★ = the seq of the last list event the registry reduced (if none, `None` — all of that incarnation's list events are after the snapshot). Reduction runs in ring order inside the replay lock (§5-3), so the registry writes that seq together, and reads it in one go **under the same `queued_inputs` lock** as the rows — so the rows = the reduction value of ring prefix `as_of_seq`. It also carries the incarnation marker (`epoch`) — seq is compared only within one incarnation. Where it is used = re-attach reconciliation aligns the snapshot and live events by this seq (§5-7). The LLM need not read it (the same verb, so the two surfaces give the same shape).
    - ★The row carries the registry's reduction state without omission (decision)★ — `cancel` = the two fields of a cancel-pending item (`응답` · `벤더 닫음` — §5-2 · none if `queued`). Only then is the result of re-reducing later events over the snapshot equal to the registry (if the two fields were missing, the outcome of a cancel-pending item whose determination·response came late would diverge from the registry). The LLM may read only `state`. Exception = `unconfirmed` — not reduction state but a marker overlaid at the moment of the query (below).
    - ★**`unconfirmed` = an item the codex path holds as acceptance unknown (`Unconfirmed` — §5-5) (decision)**★. In the registry it is `Queued` as it is (that transition has no event — N10), so it cannot be produced by reduction. The list query reads the registry rows and, **after** releasing that lock, asks the path query `AgentTransport::unconfirmed_inputs(&self) -> Vec<String>` (default implementation = an empty list — claude · PTY are always empty), and overlays only rows whose registry state is `queued` (cancel-pending wins — `cancelling` as it is). It does not hold the two locks overlapping (ADR-0006).
