So the marker is the path state at the moment of the query and is not one atom with the row snapshot (`as_of_seq`) — an item received in between may be read without a marker and an item that just became unknown may be read with one (the next query corrects it). Where it is used = the LLM: it reads which gray item is acceptance unknown so that mail waits (N8 — later user messages are not blocked, user decision N13 (b)) and that the ✕ of that item closes at once as discarded (N12 (a)) (CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」). ★Windows do not draw it separately★ — it is an ordinary gray item (PRD §3-4). Re-attach reconciliation reads `unconfirmed` as `queued` (the same state in reduction — §5-7). The reason for carrying it now and the road of writing it into the registry as an event are in §10-5.
    - ★**`stopped_after_error` = that incarnation is in a halt after error (decision — CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」)**★ — it carries the turn observation table's `last_end_failed` as it is (that very field the kernel reads as mail's door — one source for both backends · false if the table has no entry · §5-0). The LLM knows 「우편이(codex 는 통로가 스스로 여는 턴도) 사용자의 다음 턴이 성공할 때까지 멈춰 있다」 in one list query — windows do not draw it (the accepted cost as it is). It is only one field — we add no new command·vocabulary and it rides on P4's `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 6 (§8).
  - `agent.cancelQueuedInput` — `#[effect(Write)]` · args `{ target: String, input_id: String }` → `{ outcome: String }` · errors `[NOT_FOUND]` (★implementation reflected (orchestrator accepted) — **both verbs announce `[NOT_FOUND, CONFLICT]`**: if the target resolution is ambiguous it is `CONFLICT` and a bus lease rejection also reuses `CONFLICT` · a cancel write failure (`Write(e)`) is `INTERNAL` as the value is · a list query of a sleeping agent is `NOT_FOUND` · an empty `input_id` is WS = `NOT_FOUND` · bus = `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (an accepted asymmetry)★). **On both backends, the LLM also cancels any item of the list (`queued`) with this command** (PRD AC18 — the same command as the screen ✕ · ✕ is on every item). After passing the registry check (an unknown·terminal id = `NOT_FOUND` · already cancelling = `requested` with no event — idempotent, decision), it splits by policy (§5-0):
    - claude (`SessionClassified`) → inside the input lock `CancelRequested` + cancel line (§5-4) → `requested`. The outcome is reflected in the list by the lifecycle (exception = cancel response `cancelled:true` — cancelled at once, §5-2). If the cancel line write fails, it returns an error but the item stays cancel-pending as it is (`CancelFailed` — not reverted, §5-4).
    - codex (`TransportOwned`) → path `withdraw` (§5-5 「✕」): `Withdrawn` (the item the path held was withdrawn · no vendor call) = `cancelled` (synchronous) · `TooLate` (already handed over — it leaves the list at once and awaits the outcome: a bubble if received late · if it is an acceptance-unknown item, at once (c) — not passing through 「취소 중」, it also leaves the LLM list at once (N12 (a)), §5-5 · if it is a second ✕ overlapping an item already ✕-ed, with no event) = `requested` · `NotHeld` (the path already gave an outcome between the registry check and `withdraw` — receipt·interrupt etc.) = `NOT_FOUND`, no event (that outcome event soon stands in the registry — decision).
    - The codex ✕ withdrawing the message (`Withdrawn`) happens only while the path holds it — under the `Immediate` policy (the first step of landing — §9 P2) only held items in segments where hand-over is impossible (connecting · waiting for the turn id · settlement · halt after error · a turn where steer was rejected) are withdrawn, and other codex items have already been handed over, so this command is mostly `requested` (→ outcome (b)). `AtEarliestBoundary` (P8) adds the held items of the tool segment (and the answer segment if M16 is green). A message sent while idle (Direct) is not in the registry so it is `NOT_FOUND`.
  - ★**`input_id` is required**★ — a default like 「없으면 가장 최근」 is for an Esc binding that does not exist yet, so we do not put one. We reopen it when Esc is brought in (PRD §7 · §10-5).
  - Naming rule = the existing `slot.assignAgent`·`agent.spawnInto` (noun.verbObject camelCase). `catalog_version: 4 → 5` (`commands.rs:39` — the names increased).
- **WS (`protocol` `AgentCommand`):** `CancelQueuedInput { agent_id, input_id: String, request_id }` · `ListQueuedInputs { agent_id, request_id }`. It is a new variant so `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is raised (§8). ★Implementation reflected — the answer is `AgentEvent::QueuedInputs { request_id, agent_id, inputs, as_of_seq, epoch, stopped_after_error }` · `AgentEvent::QueuedInputCancelReply { request_id, agent_id, input_id, outcome }` (since `outcome` may be `requested` it is 「CancelReply」 and not 「Cancelled」) · errors are the 「`NOT_FOUND: …`」·「`INTERNAL: …`」 of `Error{request_id: Some}` · ★a lease rejection is a bare wording with no code head (`INPUT_LOCKED_REFUSAL` — the same letters as `WriteStdin`)★ — the frontend converts it to `CONFLICT` when passing it to the LLM (「프론트 명령」 below) · the vocabulary is a string on both surfaces so it tolerates unknown words★.
- ★**Input lease — the two entrances follow the same rule**★:
  - **A list query is a read — it does not look at the lease.**
  - **Cancel affects input, so it goes through the same check as `WriteStdin`·`Interrupt`** (`multiview.check_input` — `connection_core.rs:452`, called from `:1071`·`:1085`) **on both WS and the bus**. Today the bus has **no command at all** that touches input (agent commands = `list`·`spawn`·`new`·`rename`·`move` — `commands.rs:95-156`), so the only precedent to follow is on the WS side.
  - ★**The place of the check on the bus side = the command table's common entrance `call_daemon_command` (`control/commands.rs:73`) — together with the ADR-0157 entrance check (`check_args`) (decision)**★. Ground: the roads by which a daemon command (`agent.*`) reaches the table are ★**three**★ and **all three pass through this function** — ① socket bus: `deliver(…, origin: ConnId, …)` (`command_delivery.rs:1551-1558`) → `LocalCommands::run` (`command_delivery.rs:1184-1189`) → `DaemonLocalCommands::run` (`control/commands.rs:160-167`) → common entrance ② ★CLI `/control/call`★: `catalog::handle_call` reaches the common entrance **passing through `LocalCommands::run`** by `locals.run(&name, &mut args, "cli")` (`control/catalog.rs:347`) — there is no connection number. ★§7-2 run 14 is actually this road★ (`engram call` — a new verb is not put in the verb list `CLI_AGENT_VERBS` (`types.rs:320`) of `engram agent`, decision) ③ CLI `/control/agent`: `handle_agent` (`control/agent.rs:156-168`) calls the common entrance **directly** with the entrance label `"cli"` — it does not pass through `LocalCommands::run`. So if the check were placed in `DaemonLocalCommands::run`, ③ would be missed — that the surfaces' checkpoints are in one place is the reason this entrance exists (`connection_core.rs:4995` comment). (The 「오늘 그 입구 라벨은 `bus` 하나다」 of the `control/commands.rs:154-156` comment is already stale because ② calls it as `"cli"` — P4 fixes it together.)
  - **Carry the caller's identity:** add an argument with `LocalCommands::run(…, caller: Option<ConnId>)` and add `caller: Option<ConnId>` to the common entrance too — ① bus = `Some(origin)` (`deliver` already holds it) · ② `/control/call` = `None` · ③ `/control/agent` = `None`. `None` is **not a lease holder** — if nobody holds the lease, pass; if someone holds it, reject. (A caller without a connection that enters through `CommandBus::invoke` gets a one-time number handed down from `u64::MAX` (`open_caller` — `command_delivery.rs:1996-2005`), and network row numbers rise from 1, so it **cannot equal any lease holder** — even on the day a daemon name came in by that road, it would naturally be a non-holder.)
  - **Shape of the check:** put the 「입력 영향」 marker on the command declaration (marker shape = a P4 interface — §9 · ★implementation reflected — instead of adding a field to `CommandSpec`, we attached it with a list constant next to the declaration, `agent` crate `commands.rs` `INPUT_AFFECTING` (= `["agent.cancelQueuedInput"]`): changing the macro would shake the command crate · orchestrator decision★), and for commands with the marker the common entrance ① resolves `target` to an agent id **once** (the very resolver the verb body uses) ② looks at the lease with that id (the lease port the daemon plugs in — the adapter of `multiview.check_input`) ③ **passes the resolved id to the verb body** (it replaces `target` with the canonical id). Without ③, the check and the execution would resolve `target` separately, and if the name changed in between, **the agent checked and the agent cancelled could differ**. Rejection = the lease rejection error (the same meaning as WS's `LeasePass::Denied` → 「input locked by another viewer; acquire first」 — `connection_core.rs` `INPUT_LOCKED_REFUSAL` · the bus error code name is a P4 interface · ★implementation reflected — bus code = `CONFLICT` (the same wording)★).
  - Note: today the frontend does not take a lease (`AcquireInput` zero cases in `src/`) — so in daily use both entrances pass.
- **Frontend command:** `agent.cancelQueuedInput` (`src/commands/agentCommands.ts`) — args `{ agentId, inputId }` (both required) → `agentClient.cancelQueuedInput`. ✕ **dispatches** this command (the button does not call the client directly). ★Windows do not draw from the command result★ — the result (`outcome`·error) is a value the LLM·CLI reads, and a window hides that item when the state of the daemon registry that the command changed comes back as a ring event (`CancelRequested` · `Dropped{Withdrawn}`) — the window that pressed it and other windows hide it by the same road (§5-7). If the command is rejected by the lease, there is no event so the item stays standing (`NOT_FOUND` is an id whose outcome has come or is about to, so that outcome event fixes the window). ★Implementation reflected — the result is `{ outcome }`, and this command converts the daemon's bare lease rejection wording to 「`CONFLICT: …`」 and gives it to the LLM (it does not lean on the code head · the operating carrier rejects with a string so it does not split by `Error` instance — QA fix `160ba1e`) · the rejection wording constant is moved to the frontend `agentClient.ts` with a byte comparison test against the daemon wording★. We do not attach `help` (★implementation reflected — a stronger reason: if `help` were attached, the shell's `RegisterCommands` would carry names the daemon answers, and the name collision check would reject **the whole bundle**, dropping every frontend command from the bus — P4 review★) — the surface outside windows is carried by the daemon commands above (the precedent of `agent.spawn` existing on both surfaces under the same name). So the list of `busCommands.test.ts` is as it was. **We put no key binding** (PRD §3-6).
- **No new global handle.**

### 5-7. Frontend

- ★**Live delivery order — the client keeps seq continuity (decision)**★.
  - **Defect:** the core issues seq and pushes to the ring inside the replay lock but does the fanout after releasing the lock (ADR-0006 — `output_core.rs:310` and below). If two threads emit almost simultaneously, a subscriber that is **already attached** can receive N+1 before N, and the client **drops** `seq <= 마지막` in the live phase (`protocolClient.ts:270` · `RichSlot.tsx:174`). ★The boundary where the buffer phase moves to live is the same★ — the daemon puts `ReplayComplete` into the connection queue **after** `subscribe_from` releases the subscribers lock (`connection_core.rs:1750-1810`), so in between, the reversed live frame of another emit can split before and after the marker. If the connection queue is `[…L, L+2, ReplayComplete, L+1]`, today's flush sorts and flushes up to L+2 and raises `마지막 = L+2` (`protocolClient.ts:397-403`), and the L+1 that comes next is dropped by live's dedup (`:270`). It is a defect that exists today (synthetic echo versus pump · history chunk versus pump — the `output_core.rs:253` document records that discard) but it was rare. This feature makes **producers outside the pump** (claude session input·cancel · codex announce·`withdraw`) emit list events **mid-turn** while the pump is flowing, so it becomes frequent, and one lost line becomes a **permanently wrong list** (if `Delivered{X}` is lost X remains and the echo-back is suppressed so there is not even a bubble · if `CancelRequested` is lost an item that was ✕-ed stays in that window alone). The registry is updated inside the replay lock so it is right — what is wrong is the accumulator.
  - **We fix it — one rule: the next seq delivered to the view is always `마지막+1`.**
    - **live:** if `seq > 마지막+1`, put it in the hold list, and when `마지막+1` comes, flush it and the held ones connected to it in order. If there is no hole, flush at once with no delay. ★Implementation reflected — the held count stands in the view state `ViewOutputState.held` (diagnostic). But the only road the LLM reaches is `window.__ENGRAM_AGENT__` (it is not in `__engramCmd` — the gap of global handles outside the bus in CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」)★.
    - **Buffer → live (the flush of a success marker):** only sort, and do not flush everything — flush **only as far as connected starting from `max(마지막+1, replay_from)`**, and move the rest after the first hole into the hold list and enter live. The only place the start point can skip `마지막+1` is **the replay head alone** — replay is everything from the oldest of the ring (the shell sends `Subscribe{after_seq: None}` — the three places that ask for replay `src-tauri/src/daemon_client/connection.rs:1142`·`:1471`·`:1556` · full-from-oldest) and the ring is continuous, so an empty slot before the head is ring eviction (a truncated replay) and not a reversal. Every empty slot after the head is a reversed frame that has not come yet.
      - ★**The head is received, not estimated from the buffer (decision)**★ — using 「버퍼의 최소 seq」 as the head is wrong **when replay is empty**: if two emits split around the marker for a new agent that subscribed with an empty ring (a new incarnation · last = −1) (seq 1 before the marker · seq 0 after the marker), the buffer minimum seq is 1 so flush flushes from 1 and live dedup drops the late 0. So the replay completion marker the shell passes to the webview carries the **replay head `replay_from`**. The wire's SubscribeAck already carries the value (`messages.rs:385` — the seq the core actually sent first, `output_core.rs:599-604`) but ★today the shell discards that field★ (`connection.rs:1444-1450` — pattern `..`) — so we **add** plumbing to the shell: receive it from SubscribeAck, hold it in that subscription's state, and carry it when making the replay completion marker. Only between the shell and the webview changes and the wire is as it is (`PROTOCOL_VERSION` unchanged). A new incarnation starts from `max(마지막+1, replay_from)` = `replay_from`, the same incarnation from `마지막+1` (the buffer minimum seq is not used to decide the start point). The marker grows by one fixed-width field and stays fixed-length (the actual layout of the marker is unconfirmed — to be confirmed at implementation · the reason we do not carry a variable-length list = re-attach reconciliation below). ★Implementation reflected — the layout was nailed down: **38 bytes** `[255][agent 16][epoch u32 BE][gen u64 BE][flags 1][replay_from u64 BE]` (shell `replay_flight.rs` `MARKER_FRAME_LEN` · frontend `decodeReplayMarker`) · a shorter marker (including the old 30 bytes) is **rejected** (the shell and the webview are deployed together) · the shell holds the head of SubscribeAck with `ReplayFlightSet::on_ack(…, replay_from, …)`. The line numbers of `messages.rs` · `output_core.rs` · `connection.rs` in this line are the edition 8 reading baseline★.
      - ★**The `replay_from` of an empty ring is one too large today — fix it in the core (decision)**★: when there is nothing to send, the core carries `latest+1` but reads the empty ring's `latest` as 0 (`output_core.rs:579`·`:604`) so it becomes 1 — the first issued seq of a new incarnation is 0 (`:330`). As it is, the empty-replay branch just above would skip seq 0. For an empty ring it carries the **next issue seq** read under the replay lock — today there is no operating consumer that reads that value (the shell discards it, and does not use it as a dedup baseline either — `protocol_state.rs:99-100`). ★Implementation reflected — landed (P5a — the line numbers of this line are edition 8's · now the subscribe replay branch of `OutputCore`)★.
  - **Premise — reversal exists but loss does not:** one incarnation's seq is continuous (`output_core.rs:330`), the ring push is before the fanout and subscribe takes the replay while holding the subscribers lock, so an attached sink receives **all** issued seq either as replay or live (the §7-1 two-thread test measures this) — but only on the condition that the sink and the shell relay do not skip that seq (the next two items).
  - ★**The sink does not skip seq — placeholder (decision)**★. A hole that is never filled arises only from the two discard branches of `FrameOutputSink::send` — a wrongly arrived `TerminalBytes` (an upstream wiring bug) and a serialization failure send nothing and return `Ok(())` (`agent_conn.rs:84-110` — `return Ok(())` is `:96`·`:109`). Every other loss closes the connection — when the connection queue is full, a `try_send` failure calls connection termination (`engram-dashboard-net` `ws.rs:246-252`), and a re-attached view comes back with a full replay. So the two branches, instead of discarding, send **a tag1 `StructuredEvent::Error` placeholder carrying that seq** (「이 사건을 싣지 못했다」 — the wording is a fixed string so its serialization does not fail).
    - ★We must not return it as `SinkError`★ — the core would take that sink as dead and remove it, and with the connection alive only that agent's output would silently cut off.
    - The `debug_assert` of the wrongly arrived `TerminalBytes` branch is left as it is (early detection) — the placeholder is release's disposition.
    - The chat view draws one error row, and the terminal view ignores tag1 (`TerminalSlot.tsx:261`) but seq advances (dedup does not look at the tag).
  - ★**The shell relay also does not skip seq — we withdraw two silent discards (decision)**★. The shell (`src-tauri` daemon_client → Tauri Channel) today silently drops output frames in two places: ① a frame whose decode was rejected (`src-tauri/src/daemon_client/connection.rs:1048-1049` — `Err(_) => {}` · rejection = a short frame `TooShort` · an unknown tag `UnknownTag`, `crates/engram-dashboard-protocol/src/codec.rs:67-74`) ② the whole frame when the window Channel registry lock is poisoned (`src-tauri/src/output_channel.rs:45-47`). The hold (the live rule above) has no deadline, so it holds the frames after the dropped seq up to the ceiling (8192 frames) and — for an idle agent, forever — that view stops. (The old reading 「그 폴더에 출력 프레임을 버리는 갈래가 없다」 was wrong — it looked only at the two places `try_send` on the command side and missed these two.)
    - ① **→ An unknown tag is confined to that agent — make a placeholder at that seq and flush it · only a short frame cuts the connection.** A daemon of the same `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not emit the two shapes (the encoder uses only two tags — `codec.rs:58-64`), so both are bug·version-mismatch paths.
      - **Unknown tag (`UnknownTag`)** — `decode_frame` only rejects at the tag first (`codec.rs:71-73`) and the agent id · incarnation marker · seq are at fixed places independent of the tag (1–28 bytes — `codec.rs:75-85` · header length = `:18`), so they can be read as long as the header length is met. The shell reads those fields and makes **the same placeholder as the daemon sink** (a tag1 `StructuredEvent::Error` carrying that agent·marker·seq — a fixed wording, the item above) and passes it through the usual road (progress signal · incarnation-marker filter · watching windows) — it is one error row in that agent's chat view and other agents·connections are as they were. We keep reading the header fields and the placeholder frame in one set in the protocol crate so the daemon sink and the shell use the same thing (decision). The reason we do not cut: the cause is usually deterministic — if the daemon carries the same event in that shape every time, then each time we cut, the full replay carries the same frame again, and every view of that shell carried on one connection repeats cutting·replay endlessly.
      - **Short frame (`TooShort`) → cut the connection** (take the connection loop out as `LoopExit::Disconnected` — today's backoff reconnect path · re-attached views come back with a full replay). We cannot read seq so we cannot make a placeholder. One connection carries all that shell's agents, so every view replays again. ★If the short frame is deterministic this cut repeats endlessly — accepted★: all the daemon's binary output frames pass through `encode_frame` (`agent_conn.rs:77`·`:113` → `codec.rs:48-56`) and write the 29-byte header first, so a short frame arises only from a producer that bypassed that encoder, and there is none today. If one arises it shows as repeated reconnects instead of a silent stop — we put no ceiling on the repeat count·deadline (a magic number that decides the answer — ADR-0038).
    - ② **→ Take back the lock and send as is** (`PoisonError::into_inner` — the shell's precedent `src-tauri/src/view_commands.rs:403`). The reason we do not cut: poison does not go away, so even re-attaching, it skips again from the next frame. That registry is a window label → Channel handle map, so there is no half state a mid-way panic could leave. ★For the same reason all three places that hold that registry lock take it back★ — poison stands on the lock so it stands at all three: frame pass-through (`src-tauri/src/output_channel.rs:45` — output frames · the replay marker · the no-progress expiry marker all pass through this function, `connection.rs:961`·`:1046`·`:1206`) · a window's Channel registration (`src-tauri/src/commands/agent.rs:164` `subscribe_output` — today it returns `Err` so that window cannot register and never receives output) · popout window cleanup (`src-tauri/src/commands/popout.rs:209` — today it skips removal so a dead Channel remains). If only the pass-through place takes it back, frames flow but a new window·reopened window cannot attach.
    - Other filtering does not make a hole in the seq stream that view will receive — a different incarnation marker (edition 8 reading `connection.rs:1036-1041` → now `src-tauri/src/daemon_client/frame_relay.rs` `relay_binary_frame` — the view also drops it and for a new incarnation re-attach asks for a full replay) · no watching window · a window not yet registered (the view asks for replay when it attaches) · a dead window.
    - ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26 · P5 review concurrency F1) — a replay request waits for that window's output Channel registration**★ (`TauriTransport.requestReplay` → `awaitOutputChannel()`). The 「아직 등록 안 된 창」 branch above was a race that already existed, but after the hold rule (P5b) a replay that went out before registration makes the view a **permanently blank screen** (replay skips that window and live waits for the head). So even when `daemon_ensure` returns already-connected with no emit, it waits for registration after `selfHeal()`, and `selfHeal` is told apart by generation so a `close()` in the middle of being pulled does not come back to life. A defense we did not take (b): carry the replay end in the marker and put the missing seq on a ladder — cheap insurance if a drop road comes back again (reviewer accepted). ★Source reading (the prototype accessor of the Tauri Channel source) — in a real `@tauri-apps/api` Channel, `delete channel.onmessage` is a no-op★: the old Channel keeps delivering until Rust changes it and frames crossing a re-registration lean on that (`tauriTransport.ts` `doRegisterOutputChannel` comment · the test's FakeChannel hides this).
  - **Ceiling — there is no deadline for waiting on a hole:** with the placeholder and the shell relay's cut, a hole on the normal path is always filled (if cut, it is refilled by a full replay), so a deadline would become a magic number that decides the answer (ADR-0038). The held amount ~~is bounded only by the existing view buffer ceiling (`protocolClient.ts:52-53` — 4 MiB · 8192 frames)~~ ★**edition 9 — the hold has its own ceiling separately: 2048 frames · 1 MiB** (`protocolClient.ts` `HELD_MAX_FRAMES` · `HELD_MAX_BYTES` — smaller than the ring ceiling 4096 · 2 MiB · the view buffer ceiling 4 MiB · 8192 is as it is · P5 review · orchestrator decision). The flush can move up to the buffer ceiling into the hold (we do not measure the ceiling until the next hold — accepted)★. If it overflows, we discard the held ones and **return to the buffer phase and request a full replay again** (`startBuffering` — the same incarnation so `newSession=false`: keep the cursor so the overlapping front is eaten by dedup, and `issueReplay` goes out inside). ★We do not call the re-request ladder (`ladderRerequest`) from live★ — that function drops a reserved re-request if `phase !== 'buffering'` (edition 8 reading `protocolClient.ts:418-437`). The replay that comes again fills the hole by the flush rule above, and if the hole place has already been pushed off the ring, it skips at the replay head (truncation — the same grade as today's overflow disposition). The hold list is emptied by `startBuffering` together with the buffer — it also discards an in-progress re-attach reconciliation (reconciliation generation below). ★This recovery stands only when the hole is a late reversed frame★ — if the sink·shell relay skips the same seq every time (a discard that bypassed the placeholder·cut — none since the two above were withdrawn), the same hole stands in the replay too and that view cannot draw behind the hole. So we raise 「sink 와 셸 중계는 seq 를 건너뛰지 않는다」 to an invariant (§8) and the daemon·shell tests keep it (§7-1).
  - **What cannot be closed:** a dead sink (connection cut) is filled as today by re-attach (full replay) and the re-attach reconciliation below. An event the sink could not carry is **visible as a placeholder error row but the event itself is lost** (if it is a list event, one transition of that item — a bug path). What is closed is the **discard caused by order reversal** (both live and the buffer→live boundary), and the same discard of conversation lines as well as the list is closed together.
  - **What we rejected:** (i) gathering list events into one producer — the line stands only among list events, and the reversal where a pump's conversation frame overtakes and makes a list event dropped stays · (ii) when we see a hole, reconcile only the list with `listQueuedInputs` — the list comes back but the bubble of the lost `Delivered` (the echo-back is already suppressed) and the conversation lines cannot come back · (iii) a road that changes the core invariant (fanout inside the lock) — the user chose to leave the core invariant as it is (user confirmation N5).
- **Accumulator (`structuredAccumulator.ts`):**
  - A new arm `QueuedInput` — the TS edition of the §5-2 reduction rule (state-based table · the two fields of `Cancelling` and `CancelAnswered` · tombstone spec (folding · no duplicates · FIFO 1024) · revival (cause `Unknown` — one set) · `AckUnavailable` (the determination marker is held only by the registry — §5-2)). State `queued: QueuedEntry[]` (id·text·phase) + tombstones + `snapshotQueued()`. ★It does not touch `turnDone`★ (it is not output).
  - **Placement:** if `Delivered{id}` is a waiting id, remove it from the list and add a user bubble (the body of `Queued`) at **that place (now in ring order)** and put that uuid into 「본 것」 · like the user arm `turnDone=false` — but if that uuid is already 「본 것」, do not add a bubble (「이미 그린 uuid」 below). The N1 (a) handling of `AckUnavailable` draws a bubble at that place for **each copy that event carries** (in list order) and puts that uuid into 「본 것」 — the same whether the accumulator knows that id (removes it from the list) or does not (the ring window lost `Queued` — only a tombstone), and if that uuid has already been drawn, it does not draw (§5-2 「`Queued` 가 링에서 밀려난 뒤의 받음」). The `AckUnavailable` the core wrote after `Queued` following the decision (§5-3) is the same — the accumulator receives the rewritten lines as two ordinary events. ★Implementation reflected — `turnDone=false` was left as it is (P2c decision — codex's `Delivered` is usually the head of the next turn and that turn's boundary follows). A late receipt that came at Idle after the turn end may have no turn following, so the daemon emits one more display-only boundary (§5-5 「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」) — the frontend did not change. The `feed` of a waiting list event (`QueuedInput`) returns true to turn off the waiting indicator of `RichSlot` (for an item waiting gray during a halt after error it is right not to have a spinner — P1b decision)★.
  - **Suppress waiting uuids:** if the uuid of a user echo (`Structured{kind:"user"}` text) is **waiting**, we neither draw it nor put it into 「본 것」. ★Measurement showed both orders (M2)★ — on the fold path the echo-back comes **right before** `started` (=`Delivered`) and this suppression takes it, and on the new-turn path it comes 1.5–2.1 s after `Delivered` and uuid dedup takes it. ★The old 「uuid 첫 항목이 이긴다」 narrows to 「**대기 중이 아닌** uuid 의 첫 항목이 이긴다」★.
  - ★**An already-drawn uuid — one bubble per id (decision)**★. In the window where receipt-possibility is `Unknown`, the session emits `Queued` **after** the write (§5-4), so the echo-back may come before `Queued` — then there is no waiting uuid to suppress yet so the echo-back draws the bubble, and the following `Queued` → `Delivered` (vendor `started` · or N1 (a)'s `AckUnavailable`) tries to draw a **second bubble** by the placement rule. Two rules: ① if the uuid of `Delivered{id}` is already 「본 것」, only remove it from the list and add no bubble ② if the uuid of a list item is already 「본 것」, do not draw it in `QueuedInputList` (a late-arriving `Queued` — so that a message that is already a bubble does not float overlapping the list).
    - ★We do not touch reduction state and filter **only in drawing**★ — the reducer does not know bubbles, so if we changed the accumulator's list state it would diverge from the registry·golden sequence (§10-5). So that item remains in the registry and the LLM list until the outcome event (`Delivered` · `AckUnavailable` for an N1 (a) old CLI) — on a new CLI it is the echo-back→`started` interval of the fold path (≈1 ms — M2).
    - What remains = the cost of the §5-4 「쓰지 못한 글」 item: in that window that message is drawn once at the echo-back place (at consumption — soon the delivery place) without passing through the list.
  - ★**Cancel-pending (`Cancelling`) is not drawn — every window** (PRD §3-6 · §5-2)★. The moment `CancelRequested` is reduced, that item leaves `QueuedInputList` — the reduction state is `Cancelling` as it is (awaiting the outcome · it also holds the body copy). There is no hiding state a window holds separately: the window that pressed it hides it the same as other windows, when the state of the daemon registry the command changed comes back as a ring event (§5-6). Ring replay is deterministic so it hides it the same even after an app restart (PRD AC17). Outcome: (b) `Delivered` → a bubble at that place by the placement rule · (a)·(c) → nothing is drawn. Even if the cancel request itself fails (`CancelFailed` — claude error response · cancel line write failure) the reduction state stays cancel-pending so that item is not drawn again — the outcome is decided by the vendor lifecycle (§5-2 · §5-4 · PRD §3-6 · AC13).
  - **Terminal `Cancelled`·`Discarded`:** remove from the list and fold into a tombstone (§5-2). It returns nothing to the input box and **draws no notification** (PRD §3-7 — the cause is material for logs·tests). The `Dropped{AgentEnded}` that the seal rewrote (§5-3) is an id that never went on the list so only a tombstone remains.
  - ★**`Dropped{Rejected}` — erase the user bubble drawn with that id** (PRD §3-5 · AC26 · §5-2)★. It is a drawing rule **independent of** reduction state — a message sent while idle (Direct) never went on the list so the reduction leaves only a tombstone, but the accumulator removes the row if there is a user bubble with that uuid (no notification). Ring replay is deterministic so every window·app restart erases it the same way. That uuid is left in 「본 것」 — a rejection is a no-revival cause (§5-2) so a late same uuid is not drawn again (decision). If an item that passed through the list is rejected before receipt there is no bubble so it is a no-op, and if rejected **after receipt** (`Dropped{Rejected}` after `Delivered` — §5-2), it erases the very bubble `Delivered` drew. **Only rejection is erased** — the Direct bubbles of interrupt·agent end·failed turn stay (§5-2).
  - **Revival:** when `Delivered` comes to a revival-possible tombstone (cause `Unknown`), the reduction is `Delivered` (§5-2), and the accumulator does not draw a bubble separately — that id is not waiting so it is not suppressed, and the vendor echo that follows draws it (§5-5).
  - replay idempotence: `reset()` empties everything (including tombstones) — it also discards an in-progress re-attach reconciliation (reconciliation generation — below).
- **`QueuedInputList` (a new component, `src/components/slot/`):**
  - **There is no header line** (PRD §3-2 · AC8) — whatever the turn state, only gray items are drawn. So the component does not read turn state·tool state.
  - **Items** — only the accumulator's `phase==queued` items (cancel-pending is not drawn · 「이미 그린 uuid」 filter — above) · a one-line gray preview `truncate` (「…」) · the full text in the tooltip for a truncated item · older ones on top (PRD §3-2 · AC7). Gray = the theme variable `--text-muted` (defined in all three themes — `src/styles/theme.css:1-3` · PRD AC19).
  - **Collapse** — over 3, it collapses with 「외 N개」: while collapsed the **three oldest** are visible, and pressing 「외 N개」 expands it (PRD §3-2 · AC7 · user decision Q3). ★The count·shape will be adjusted later (user 「나중에 조정」)★ — we put the visible count (3) · the visible side (oldest) · the way of expanding as defaults of the component props so adjustment ends in one place (decision). Whether it is expanded is per-window drawing state (not list content — unrelated to the registry).
  - **✕** — always on every item drawn (both backends · in any segment — user decision 2026-09-25 · §0). Pressing it removes from the list — whether the message is withdrawn is decided by the backend (§5-4 · §5-5) and the window does not know. It is reached with Tab (a button that enters the focus order) and the tooltip is 「목록에서 빼기」 — all items · the same wording on both backends, adding no warning (PRD §3-6 · AC10 · user answer 2026-09-26 — it changed the 「취소 — 글은 버려짐」 of Q7: it is a wrong statement for a handed-over codex item). Pressing it only dispatches `agent.cancelQueuedInput` and does not hide by itself (§5-6). Even when ✕ could not withdraw the message (a handed-over codex item) we do not announce it separately — the bubble of outcome (b) is all (user: 「상관없어」 · no guidance line — Q7).
  - The wording (tooltip · 「외 N개」) is `ko.ts`.
- **`RichSlot.tsx` layout:** wrap the list and the input box in **one relative bundle** and put the list **above** the input box. The identity label (today `-top-5` relative to the input box) is attached to the head of that bundle — the list does not cover the name tag (PRD AC20). The list place is made to occupy **always the same child index** with `{hasQueued && <QueuedInputList/>}` — if the textarea were remounted the IME·focus guard would break (the rule today's comment guards, `RichSlot.tsx:385-387`).
- **Gate fixes:**
  - add `&& queued.length === 0` to `showEmpty` (`:304`) — if there are waiting items sent by another window·the LLM, it is not the first screen.
  - Remove `!hasSent` from `historyPending` (`:308`) → `continuesConversation && !hasHistoryRow`. What ends loading by input is now **the delivered bubble** (the row) — a message typed during hydration stands in the list, and if the history comes, the history, and if it never comes, the delivered bubble after connecting lifts the loading (ADR-0226 D5 amendment — §8). ★**This one line lands not with P5 but with P2 (the codex producer)**★ — if it changes first without a producer, after sending a message to a resumed codex agent with no history, the loading sheet would not lift at once as today but remain until the vendor echo comes (and in the meantime there is no list either, so nothing shows — it looks worse than today). What needs this gate is only codex connecting items, so P2 is its place — for claude the history restore ends before registry registration (ADR-0079) and the first input is Direct so a synthetic echo row lifts loading at once, so it looks the same at either gate (§9). ★Implementation reflected — landed (P2c) · two residues (→ §5-9): ① if the first input of a resuming claude is Queued (e.g. when mail opened the turn first), the loading sheet remains until `Delivered` (P2 review A2 — the exception to 「같게 보인다」 above) ② a resumed agent of codex below the floor has no producer events so after sending, the loading sheet remains until the vendor echo (accepted)★.
- ★**There is no return to the input box** (PRD §3-7)★ — we withdrew edition 1's drain (`drainReturned`) · 「`'live'` 뒤 프레임만 입력창에」 · 「같은 에이전트를 보는 모든 창이 돌려받는다」 entirely. A synchronous send failure that arises because the window could not reach the daemon (`writeStdin` reject) is a message that never went on the list so it is outside this feature — today's behavior as it is (the body disappears).
- **Re-attach reconciliation (decision):** right after `'live'` call `listQueuedInputs` once and align the list to that snapshot (a row carries `text`·`state` — a cancel-pending row revives only as state and is not drawn, and holds the body copy so it can draw the bubble of outcome (b), PRD AC17 · `unconfirmed` is read as `queued` — §5-6). Reason: if re-attaching on a long turn that exceeds the ring ceiling (`REPLAY_MAX_EVENTS` 4096 · 2 MiB — `output_core.rs:890-893`), `Queued` is pushed out and an item still waiting vanishes from the list (in codex one delta is one event). The road of carrying the list in SubscribeAck was dropped because it would change the shell marker (fixed length). ★Implementation reflected — once per `'live'` (+ incarnation marker) of each json `RichSlot`, `ListQueuedInputs` (a sleeping agent's `NOT_FOUND` is swallowed) · accumulator `beginQueuedReconcile(epoch)` → `offerQueuedSnapshot(gen, …)` (`applied` · `held` · `stale`) · phases other than `'live'` and `reset` are `abandonQueuedReconcile` · `as_of_seq` is `bigint | null` in the `agent` crate binding (ts-rs `u64`), and `number | null` in the WS answer · the line numbers `output_core.rs:890-893` are edition 8's (now = `REPLAY_MAX_EVENTS`)★. ★A window's connection loss is not an interrupt★ (PRD §3-7) — the registry is in the daemon, so a re-attached window recovers the real state through this reconciliation (AC16).
  - ★**The aligning rule — later events over the snapshot in seq order (decision)**★. A live event can come after the query read the registry and before the answer arrives — if the query read `Queued{X}` and a `CancelRequested{X}` that stood through another window's ✕ afterward arrives before the answer, the window hides X, but if the late-arriving rows are merged as they are, X is **drawn again** (it breaks PRD §3-6 「다시 나타나지 않는다」). So the answer carries the snapshot seq S (§5-6 `as_of_seq`) and the window:
    - writes down separately the list events received from the moment it sent the query — all events after S were issued **after** the query read the registry so they are contained in this window.
    - applies the answer **after** the view has delivered through S — if it comes first, hold it and wait (no deadline — seq continuity guarantees the arrival of S · if the connection is cut, re-attach redoes the reconciliation). So at the moment of application the accumulator has seen all events through S.
    - an id in the snapshot = the result of starting from that row's state and re-reducing, in seq order, the written-down events with **seq > S** (those of that id, and the `AckUnavailable` that applies to all items) · ~~an id not in the snapshot = the accumulator's present state as it is (it stood on the list after S or became terminal through S — the accumulator has already seen both)~~ · events with seq ≤ S were already contained in the snapshot so they are not re-reduced.
    - ★**Edition 9 — the rule for an id not in the snapshot changed (implementation reflected · P5 review — common to three reviewers · orchestrator decision)**★: if the accumulator has an open row that is **in no row of the snapshot** (regardless of state — checked against all raw ids) and the written-down events with **seq > S** contain no `Queued` of that id either, we **close that row as a revival-possible tombstone** (no bubble — `QueuedInputRegistry.adoptSnapshot`). Reason: the premise of the struck line (「없으면 누산기가 이미 봤다」) becomes false after the ring head jumps — a row for which the accumulator saw only `Queued` outside the head and did not see the terminal remains forever as 「유령 행」 while absent from the snapshot. Since it is a tombstone, a late `Delivered` still revives it. seq tracking is done not by the reducer but by the accumulator (`deliveredSeq` — all frames) (golden unchanged).
    - The re-reduction fixes only list state — it does not draw a new bubble (the bubble of an item the accumulator knew was already drawn when that event came, and an item it did not know was not waiting so the vendor echo draws it or the copy of `AckUnavailable` already drew it when that event came — §5-2 「`Queued` 가 링에서 밀려난 뒤의 받음」 · the overlap is blocked by 「이미 그린 uuid」).
    - If the answer's incarnation marker differs from the view's incarnation, discard it.
    - ★**The reconciliation is tied to one replay cycle — the reconciliation generation (decision)**★. When the window sends a query it raises the reconciliation generation by one and attaches it to that query (a value inside the window — the wire is as it is). When the view leaves `'live'` and enters a new replay cycle — whether it is the `reset()` of a re-attach·new incarnation or the `startBuffering` of a hold overflow (the same incarnation — the ceiling above) — it raises the generation again and discards the written-down event record and the answer it was waiting for. It applies an answer only when its own generation equals the present generation, and the new cycle asks fresh at the next `'live'`. What it blocks: if an old query's answer were applied after the record was emptied, there would be no material to re-reduce the events after the snapshot (such as the `CancelRequested` another window's ✕ produced), so an item that was ✕-ed would be drawn again — the race above comes back. The incarnation marker check (just above) filters only a new incarnation and cannot filter an overflow re-request with the same marker.
    - For items the accumulator already knows from the ring, the result equals the accumulator state (the same reduction rule — golden). What differs is only items for which `Queued` was pushed off the ring so the accumulator **does not know** them — the accumulator let the events that came to such an item flow as 「모름 → 버린다」, so re-reducing the written-down events over the row lets ✕ win and keeps the body copy.

### 5-8. Relation to the first-submit latch (ADR-0226)

- ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — by user decision the place where codex JSON counts has changed (an ADR-0226 amendment target · §10-1 24)**★: for the codex app-server (JSON chat mode · `TransportOwned`), **the session does not count submissions** (`AgentSession::count_turn_submission` only applies the user-termination rejection and returns). The path reader calls the latch's first-turn port (`FirstTurnSink` = `SessionIdLatch::first_turn_sink` → the existing `note_submission` — the latch state machine as it is) once per incarnation at the peer's **first live user-message echo-back** (the `userMessage` of `item/started`·`item/completed` — whether or not `clientId` is there · mail turns and below-floor turns too · only a line that states it is another thread is excluded · history restore is not counted) (`Reader::witness_first_turn` · without a lock · after the screen emission). So **this mode has no 「첫 턴 전에 영속」 and persists after the first turn** — if it dies after sending and before the echo-back, nothing is persisted and the next activation starts as a new conversation. User: 「일단 실제 아이디가 생성된 기준이어야지 당연히. 그게 사용자 글을 에코할때잖아.」 · 「미세하게 중간에 종료되서 저장안되는건 그냥 초기화 하라고하고」. Two alternatives rejected: counting when the session receives (this section through edition 8 — if the first message typed while connecting is withdrawn by ✕, a zero-turn id is persisted · the entrance that first opened when P2c turned codex on with `TransportOwned`) · persisting at the `thread/start` response (the zero-turn id of an agent that does not send or withdraws). **Scope = codex JSON only** — claude and codex terminal (ADR-0226 decision 11) are as in the line below. ★The edition 8 codex argument below (count at submit · 「첫 턴 전에 영속」 · commit at the handshake place for the first submit during connecting) is wrong under this decision — it was folded into one strikethrough line, and 「codex 연결 중 ✕ 입구」 at the edge was marked with 「9판」★. Residues (→ §5-9): if the vendor changes to no longer echo `userMessage` back, persistence silently stops (only the latch's 「Held」 info log) · the commit (profile disk write) runs on the codex reader thread (bounded by rewriting agents.json — ADR-0071) · if the port panics on the reader thread, the pump `Failed` · the window between kill and a late commit (the same as the window of `record_session_id`).
- **For claude and codex terminal the place of counting is as it was — the submission (the moment the session hands it to the path), not the time it is handed to the vendor** (codex JSON is the implementation reflection above). The session write verb counts **before** it calls the path verb (`send_input`) (`AgentSession::count_turn_submission` → `SessionIdLatch::note_submission` · ① of the claude Queued branch — §5-4). Mail also passes the same count (`count_turn_submission`).
- ~~**In codex, submission and hand-over often occur apart** — the path holds items during connecting · waiting for the turn id · tool segment · answer segment · end-of-turn settlement · halt after error · a turn where steer was rejected. Even so we do not move the place: 「첫 턴 전에 영속」 stands as it is · the latch reacts only to the first submit of an incarnation, and the only place the first submit reaches among the segments where they occur apart is connecting, and that submit is committed at the place where the handshake gives the id (ADR-0226 TRD §3-2-4).~~ ★Edition 9 — this is edition 8's codex argument (edition 8 original = `6290c5b` §5-8). Since codex JSON counts not at submission or hand-over but at the peer's first echo-back (above), it does not matter if submission and hand-over occur apart★
- **Alternative rejected — counting at the time of hand-over (right before the writer's `turn/start`):** one more trigger would stand inside the path — the shape ADR-0226 rejected (「codex `writer_loop` 의 `turn/start` 직전」 — that ADR 「거부한 대안」 · TRD §3-2-5). ★Edition 9 — the user decision is not the time of hand-over but the peer's echo-back (the reader). One port stands on the path reader (`FirstTurnSink`), and ADR-0226 decisions 2 · 11 and 「영향/불변식」 are amended with a new ADR (a stamp on the old ADR)★
- **claude does not change** — whether or not it goes on the list, it is written to stdin at once, so submission is hand-over. The incarnation's first input is Direct because no turn is running (if mail opened the turn first, that mail is the first submission).
- **Edge — an id with no conversation:** if the first submission is withdrawn or discarded before it reaches the vendor (✕ of a codex connecting item · interrupt · agent end · rejection), an id with no conversation is persisted — the same kind as ADR-0226 TRD §6-3 「A 가 새로 만드는 쓰레기」 (a write failure after persistence · a kill before the first answer, etc.) and D4 (Phase B) absorbs it. The entrance this feature adds to that kind is one, ✕ on a codex connecting item (a connecting item is held so ✕ withdraws the message — both policies · from skeleton §9 P2). ★Edition 9 — that entrance is closed: codex JSON persists only at the first echo-back so the first message withdrawn by ✕ while connecting leaves no id. Instead, if it dies after sending and before the echo-back, nothing is persisted (the next activation = a new conversation · accepted by the user)★ On the claude side it does not increase — the alternative of always standing the first message of a resuming claude incarnation in the list (N7 (b)) was rejected (§10-5). It is not a new kind.

### 5-9. Failure modes

| Case | Outcome |
|---|---|
| Input sent at the moment a turn ends (claude) | Classified as Queued → the CLI opens a new turn with `started` right after `result` (M7 — 1.5 ms) → the list shows for a moment and then a bubble. Only a display wobble (PRD §4) |
| Input sent before the drain turn's first output (claude) | drain `started` → `Delivered` is written as progress so the core turn observation is already mid-turn and it is Queued. If it is in the middle of the emit of that `Delivered` that empties the list (the registry is empty and progress not yet), the waiting list table is still 「찼다」 so Queued (classification reads the list table first — §5-4). The mail busy decision also sees the same table so that gap narrows to `result`→drain `started` (≈1.5 ms — M7) (mail is delayed behind the user's drain turn — a delay the user accepted, PRD §3-9) |
| A steer handed over at the moment a turn ends (codex) | −32600 → returned to `Held` (no list event) → `turn/start` once at Idle. That turn is a segment where hand-over is impossible by the 「steer 거절」 mark so both policies hold (also items that newly came in that turn) · if a ✕ was pressed in between (`cancel_asked`) it is withdrawn and not sent again (§5-5) |
| A steer response comes after `turn/completed` (codex) | settlement waits for the response and error = `Held` · success = if the echo came, a receipt, if not, acceptance unknown — hand-over is once (generation discrimination) · the same even if the error response (−32600) comes before `turn/completed` (M10 — the order is not assumed) · ★implementation reflected — even if the echo comes before the response, settlement waits for that response (`steers_owed`) · an error response after the echo does not reverse the receipt (warning + `refused_after_echo`)★ |
| The steer response does not come within the deadline (30 s) (codex) | `awaiting_reply=false` (acceptance unknown) the same as a success response → a receipt if the echo comes · acceptance unknown if none until the turn end (it stays in the list) — the queue does not stop |
| End-of-turn settlement takes long (codex — the steer response does not come) | one settlement deadline measured from the turn end (30 s) — items it could not tell apart = acceptance unknown: they stay in the list with no event (N10 · an item that was ✕-ed is discarded at once · a Direct leaves only pending) → turn opening runs again (messages typed later are handed over as usual — N13 (b) — only the mail turn waits until that item leaves) |
| `turn/start` ends on our side without `turn/completed` (codex — five exits) | write failure·error response = `Dropped{Rejected}` · response deadline·decode failure·long turn id = acceptance unknown: it stays in the list (no resend · late echo = receipt · no debt — N10) — no item waiting for a response is left in Idle · for a Direct message, write failure·error response = the bubble is erased without a notification · acceptance unknown = no event (the bubble stands) · at any exit the halt after error is set (regardless of turn kind — below) |
| `turn/completed` comes before the `turn/start` response (codex — early exit) | the path holds it and releases it when the response fixes the turn id — the end-of-turn disposition (interrupt discard · settlement · failure) runs **at that release** · the turn id is taken only from the response (`turn/started` not read — §5-5) |
| Mail and user `Held` · follow-up turn debt are together at Idle (codex) | user first — the remaining user items go in one turn each separately, and if there is no user item and there is a debt the empty turn comes first, and mail opens a turn when neither exists (PRD §3-9 · user decision N6 — mail may be delayed) · during a halt after error only the turn of a user message that arrived after the halt opens. A mail turn does not release the debt (the model answers the mail — §5-5) |
| Turn end 「기록만」 (codex) | the echo comes before `turn/completed` (M10 green) → a receipt as usual · if that turn gave no answer, only for a `completed` turn one follow-up turn debt (★implementation reflected — only messages carried by `turn/steer` · a message carried by `turn/start` has no debt — §5-5 「답 없는 항목」★) → after settlement Idle releases it with a user turn or an empty input `turn/start` once (M9 green — the model answers that message) |
| Several receipts with no answer in one turn (codex) | the follow-up turn debt is one per turn → one follow-up turn |
| A mail turn almost opens before settlement (codex) | no turn opens during settlement · Idle order after settlement = user `Held` → debt (empty turn) → mail — a mail turn opens only after the debt is released |
| A late echo for an acceptance-unknown item (codex) | an ordinary receipt — it leaves the list and the vendor echo is the bubble (the turn table stays idle) · no debt is set (warning log + counting — that message is seen by the next user turn from the history · candidate to add later) · the moment it leaves, the writer wakes and held mail flows (a doorbell if the list empties · unless a halt after error) · ★accepted cost★ — mail may get its answer before that message (that message is answered by the next user turn) |
| An acceptance-unknown item stays long (codex — the late echo never comes) | it stays gray in the list (✕ present · the LLM list is `unconfirmed` — §5-6) · a new message typed afterward is handed over in order as usual (user decision N13 (b) — if received late, that bubble may stand after the later messages · exception to PRD AC2) · mail cannot go in until it leaves (N8 — no ceiling) · agent end = discard (§5-5 「수락 모름」) |
| ✕ on an acceptance-unknown item · a ✕-ed item becomes acceptance unknown (codex) | discard at once (`Dropped{Unknown}` — outcome (c)) · it does not pass through 「취소 중」 (it also leaves the LLM list at once) · the path removes that id from pending and wakes the writer (held mail flows — unless a halt after error) · late echo = revival → an end-of-conversation bubble in every window (b) · no turn to answer is opened (the next user turn sees it from the history · warning log + counting) (user decision N12 (a) — §5-5) |
| ✕ and a fold at the same time (claude) | the vendor decides — if before the fold filter (`Oi`), cancel; if after, `started` wins and a bubble (outcome (b)). No race on our side |
| ✕ and hand-over at the same time · ✕ of an already handed-over item (codex) | the order stands under the same state lock — if still held, cancelled at once (`Dropped{Withdrawn}` · no vendor call) · if already handed over (an instant ago or long ago), it leaves the list at once and is cancel-pending (not drawn in windows) → echo = an end-of-conversation bubble in every window · the vendor turned out not to have accepted (steer error response) = it is not sent again and is withdrawn · unknown = discard at once (revival if the echo is late — N12 (a)) · interrupt·termination = discard (§5-5) |
| The window's and the LLM's ✕ overlap (codex — cancel does not take the input lock) | both pass the registry check and reach `withdraw` — if the item was handed over, only the first emits `CancelRequested`·`CancelAnswered{false}` and the second answers `TooLate` (`requested`) with no event · if it is a held item·acceptance-unknown item, the second is `NotHeld` (`NOT_FOUND` — the first's outcome event soon stands in the registry) · the reducer's `Cancelling`+`CancelRequested` no-op is the second defense (§5-2 · §5-5) |
| Another window's·the LLM's cancel tries to reach the CLI before the message (claude) | **the input lock blocks it** (§5-0) — `Queued` is visible before the write so another window can press first, but the cancel line goes out only after the message write ends |
| A disposition meets a pre-announce item (codex) | death mark → the announcing side chains `Dropped` after `Queued` · the reducer tombstone is the second defense |
| A `Queued` that comes after the `finish` synthesis | the seal rewrites it as `Dropped{AgentEnded}` → it does not go on the list |
| Path-synthesized bubble (Direct echo) | `emit_without_turn_observation` — it does not turn the turn table on again |
| Vendor echo after a turn end (codex — acceptance-unknown item · revival after ✕ · during a halt after error · after the five exits · an old turn id after a new turn id) | the path reader's attribution gate emits it as `emit_without_turn_observation` (today's code) — the bubble stands and the turn table stays idle · closes it as `Delivered` and sets no debt (warning log — §5-5 「벤더가 보낸 에코」 · 「턴 끝 뒤에 받음이 오면」) |
| The turn that used the debt (empty turn · user turn) ends oddly (codex — `failed` · five exits) | the debt was released when issued — no empty turn is issued again (warning log · counting — ★implementation reflected: the field `debt_paid_by` that recognizes that turn stands for warning only · counting = `Anomalies.unpaid` · if it ends with an unrecognizable status it is not counted★) · the halt after error is set → that message is in the history so the user's next turn sees it together · held mail waits until that turn ends in success (user decision N11 (b) — §5-5 「내는 순간 푼다」) |
| Early in claude's first turn (receipt-possibility unknown) | assume `Available` and Queued (`Queued` after the write) — if the lifecycle comes, a normal outcome · if determined `Unavailable`, `AckUnavailable` → the remaining items become bubbles in place, and after that today's path (N1 (a) — §5-4) |
| The echo-back overtakes `Queued` in the same window (claude — `Unknown` window) | the echo-back draws the bubble and the following `Delivered` (vendor `started` · N1 (a)) adds no bubble because the uuid is already drawn · the late `Queued` is not drawn in the list — one bubble. That message does not pass through the list (the same kind as today's behavior · it stays in the registry until the outcome — §5-4 · §5-7) |
| The cannot-receive decision overlaps classification (claude — the decision stands on the ring first) | the core rewrites the `Queued` after the decision, inside the same lock, as `Queued`+`AckUnavailable` (a copy of that item — the reduction is `Delivered`) (N1 (a)) — it does not stay in the list permanently (§5-3) |
| The `Queued` of an item closed by cannot-receive determination is pushed off the ring (claude — re-attach) | the bubble stands again at that place by the copy the closing `AckUnavailable` carries — whether the receipt is before or after the snapshot, regardless of reconciliation (§5-2 · §5-7). A receipt to which a vendor echo attaches is drawn by that line |
| The cannot-receive decision overlaps a write failure (claude — `Unknown` window) | the `Unknown` branch emits `Queued` only after the write succeeds so N1 (a)'s synthetic `Delivered` does not reach a message that could not be written — the failed message does not go on the list and `Err` (§5-4) |
| Cannot-receive determination during cancel-pending (claude) | our cancel response that has come by then is the outcome — if it was removed, it was already cancelled at the moment of the response so what remains is only could-not-remove·error·no response → a bubble in place (N1 (a) — an old CLI can silently drop a cancel line so we do not wait · a late response is discarded) (§5-2 · §5-4) |
| The cancel request itself fails (claude — cancel line write failure · error response) | `CancelFailed` — the command returns an error but the item stays cancel-pending as it is (could not remove · not drawn again in any window) → the outcome is the vendor lifecycle: `started` = an end-of-conversation bubble · `cancelled`·`discarded`·`refused` = discard · cannot-receive determination = a bubble in place (N1 (a)) · agent end = discard (§5-2 · §5-4) |
| A failed turn closes a cancel-pending item with `cancelled` (claude) | our cancel response is `false`·error → 「버려짐」 and not 「취소됨」 (`Discarded{Unknown}` — confirmation = M12). We follow the vendor handling as it is (PRD §3-10) |
| Cancel response `cancelled:true` came but the lifecycle `cancelled` is not there yet (claude) | cancelled at once — we do not wait (`true` = the vendor removed it from its own queue · it cannot be drained again) · a late `cancelled` is discarded by the tombstone. If we waited, on the day that line does not come an item visible in no window would block mail without a ceiling (§5-2) |
| Lifecycle not advertised (old claude) · codex below the floor | today's behavior as it is (user decision N1 (a) — PRD R6). In claude, an item that went on the list before determination becomes a bubble in place (`AckUnavailable` — §5-2) · in codex we defer announce before the floor decision so there is no such item · `halted` does not stand for codex below the floor (held input after a failure goes out at once as today — §5-5) · ★the mail halt after error stands on all versions of both backends★ — it reads only our side's turn-end fact and what it blocks is our mail write (only the list and the codex path behave as today · §5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」) |
| The receipt never comes (vendor defect — PRD R7 · not a mid-turn slash command: M13 green, `/compact`·`/cost` gave a receipt) | it stays in the list until the agent ends → `finish` synthesis → discard. Meanwhile the list is not empty so new messages all pass through the list (PRD R7 — loss of AC1). Mail also cannot go in meanwhile (no fail-open in `inputs_pending` — §5-0 · PRD R7 · user decision N8) · if it is a cancel-pending item it has the same outcome, visible in no window (§5-4) |
| A late `item/completed` of an interrupted turn's tool (codex — old turn id, M6) | segment tracking and the 「답 없는 항목」 decision both tell by the current turn id — a late completion neither ends the tool segment nor emits a boundary signal (§5-5) |
| We hand over late on the tool-end signal (codex — reaction longer than the window) | the vendor's check has passed so it enters the next boundary (one boundary late · nothing is lost) — if that turn ends there, the 「기록만」 branch → end-of-turn settlement. If systematically late, M15 is red → stop and ask before bringing in the holding policy (§9 P7) ★edition 9 — M15 green → P8 landed · this stop condition did not arise (§9 P7)★ |
| We hand over late on the end-of-answer signal (codex — when holding the answer segment with M16 green) | we miss the follow-up sampling of the same turn → when the turn ends, the 「기록만」 branch → end-of-turn settlement (follow-up turn debt). If systematically late, M16 is red and the answer segment is handed over at once |
| A tool item does not announce its end (codex) | the end-of-sampling signal (`tokenUsage/updated`) empties the tool set and hands over what is held — it does not misjudge the next segment as a tool segment (§5-5 boundary signal ③) |
| Input while an item not confirmed as a tool is running (codex — echo · `hookPrompt` · `contextCompaction` · unknown variants and the like) | segment 「그 밖」 — both policies hand over at once. A message handed over early waits for the next boundary in the vendor's waiting box — all that is lost is the window to withdraw the message by ✕ (✕ removes it from the list — §5-5 「일찍 넘기는 것은 무해」) |
| The turn ends while we are holding (codex — not one boundary signal received) | `Held` was never handed over so it is not a settlement target — it goes into the next turn together with the remaining items (one turn late · nothing is lost). On the normal path the end-of-sampling signal comes before the turn end so this does not arise — if it does, it is a segment tracking defect (the §7 tests catch it) |
| Interrupt (codex — over the wire only today) | everything held and handed over `Dropped{Interrupted}` → discard (M6 — the vendor also discards) · an acceptance-unknown item from a previous turn and the halt after error are not touched · ★implementation reflected — 「전부」 is a list item: held mail remains and goes out at the next Idle (discarding would lose the mail) and the Direct · mail handed over in that turn are removed with no event (§5-5 `interrupted`)★ |
| Halt after error (codex — `turn/completed(failed)` · the five exits of `turn/start` · regardless of turn kind) | `halted` stands — `failed` runs settlement the same but with no debt · Idle does not open a turn on its own (empty turn · mail · the remaining `Held` — even if the limit lifts) · held items so ✕ withdraws the message — both policies (from skeleton P2) · that turn opens only when a user message that arrived after the marker (Queued or Direct) is in pending — the remaining items first, in one turn each separately (AC24 · decided by the present state — if that message leaves by ✕ before the turn opens, it does not open) · it is released when that turn ends with `completed` · if it ends oddly again it is set again at the arrival number of that moment · a message with no answer that already became a bubble is left as it is · the kernel holds mail (the classifier sets `last_end_failed` with `TurnEnd{Failed}` of the same trigger — §5-0) (PRD §3-10 · §5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」) |
| A turn that ended in error (claude — `result` `is_error` · `error*` subtype) | list items translate the vendor lifecycle as it is — if the vendor discards it, it leaves the list, and if it puts it into the conversation, it becomes a bubble. We do not force a match on our side (PRD §3-10 · confirmation = M12) · ★mail halts★ — the classifier folds that turn's error signal at the turn end (`Ended`) as `last_end_failed` and the kernel's busy decision (`BusyPolicy`) reads it → the next cleanly successful end (a non-error `result`) releases it · after the table update `turn_ended` rings so no mail leaks in between · a message typed during the halt is classified as usual (if idle, Direct — that turn releases it) (§5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」) |
| All held items during a halt after error are removed by ✕ · the list empties (codex) | the halt is not released — even if the doorbell rings, the kernel holds mail with `last_end_failed` → waits until the user's next turn ends in success (user decision N11 · §5-5 「✕」) |
| Only mail comes during a halt after error (both backends) | it waits — the kernel holds it (when the agent ends it goes to the next incarnation) · no ceiling · the screen has no reason (accepted cost · mail notification is backlog — §5-0 · §5-5 「오류 뒤 멈춤」) · the LLM knows through `stopped_after_error` of the list query (§5-6) |
| Mail the kernel released to the path right before the failure (codex) | the path holds it (`halted` — zero mail turns the path opens) → it goes out when the halt is released · the ledger is already `delivered` and if the agent ends in between it disappears (today's grade of the path input queue) · only what was in flight at that moment — during the halt the kernel holds new mail (rare · accepted — §5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」 · §5-5) · codex below the floor has no `halted` so it goes out at once as today · ★implementation reflected (P2 review concurrency F2 — residue accepted) — if the list empties and the doorbell rings before the `TurnEnd{Failed}` observation, one piece of mail may enter the halted path (the same kind — the path holds it and it goes out when the halt is released) · during a `completed` settlement that waits only for the response of an echoed steer, the kernel sees an empty list and releases mail so the path holds it for at most the settlement deadline (30 s) — if killed in that window, that mail disappears (mail's waiting just moves from the kernel to the path and is bounded)★ |
| Interrupt during a halt after error (`interrupted` — codex is over the wire only today) | only that turn's items are discarded · ★it neither sets nor releases the halt — both backends★ (the turn table folds an interrupted end as 「그 밖」 and codex `halted` is also unchanged · claude has no JSON interrupt today (`transport/stdio.rs:371`) and the JSON interrupt feature will carry an interrupted `result` the same way — ADR-0192 · §5-0 fold rule) |
| A turn the vendor opened by itself (claude — if there is a turn that runs without user input) | that turn's non-error `result` releases the halt — we do not know whether such a turn exists (uncertain) · accepted |
| A mid-turn decoder `Error` (claude — if the vendor adds in-turn errors in the future) | that turn is folded as an error — the halt stands too much (the conservative side — less automatic injection) · accepted · a line-buffer overflow is the same (§5-0) |
| A steer handed over in a `failed` turn ends with no echo (codex) | acceptance unknown — it stays gray · ✕ and is not sent again · per the vendor source a `failed` turn also records the waiting items and the echo comes before the turn end — likely (M17 — before measurement · §3-3) · if it did discard, the user has to ✕ it or retype it (accepted) |
| Two inputs in the same idle moment (codex) | only the first message is Direct — it goes into pending as an out-of-list item and the second stands behind it as Queued → the second is steered to the turn id opened by the first message's `turn/start`. `Queued` is never emitted with a Direct id — a Direct is not steered, and if there is no echo at turn end it leaves pending with no event and the bubble stays (a late echo is deduped — §5-5 「분류와 방출」) |
| A message sent while idle is rejected | `Dropped{Rejected}` → the drawn bubble is erased without a notification (PRD §3-5) — codex = Direct `turn/start` write failure·error response · claude = the lifecycle `refused` (§5-2) |
| Rejection after receipt (claude — `refused` after the `started` of a list item) | that bubble is erased without a notification and the outcome is rewritten and closed as `Discarded{Rejected}` (§5-2 · it did not come in M13 — the failed-turn side is M12) |
| Agent end | the core marks the remaining ids `Dropped{AgentEnded}` → discard (no return to the input box — PRD §3-7) |
| Daemon restart | the registry is volatile (with the incarnation) — waiting items disappear. The vendor side (claude CLI queue) also disappears with the process. The same grade as today's input queue (PRD §7) |
| A window's connection loss | not an interrupt — the registry stays and a re-attached window aligns through the §5-7 reconciliation (PRD §3-7 · AC16) |
| Ring eviction during re-attach | the §5-7 reconciliation fills it |
| A live event comes before the re-attach reconciliation's answer (the query read `Queued` and then another window's ✕) | the events after the snapshot seq the answer carries are re-reduced over the row — an item that was ✕-ed does not reappear · if the answer is ahead of the view's delivery, it is applied after delivery through S (§5-7) |
| A new replay cycle in the middle of reconciliation (`reset()` · the `startBuffering` of a hold overflow) | the reconciliation generation rises and the old query's answer and the written-down events are discarded → ask again at the next `'live'` — an old answer is not applied with no record, so an item that was ✕-ed is not drawn again (§5-7) |
| A live subscriber receives seq reversed (a producer outside the pump and the pump emit at the same time) | the client holds frames after the hole and flushes them in seq order (no deadline) — by reversal neither list nor conversation lines are lost (§5-7) |
| Reversed live frames split before and after `ReplayComplete` (connection queue `[…L, L+2, 마커, L+1]`) | flush flushes only as far as connected from `max(마지막+1, replay_from)` (the replay head the marker carries) (…L) and puts L+2 in the hold → when L+1 comes, L+1·L+2 in order (§5-7) |
| The sink cannot carry an event (wrongly arrived `TerminalBytes` · serialization failure — bug path) | a tag1 `Error` placeholder at that seq — later frames are not blocked and the chat view gets one error row · **the event itself is lost** (if it is a list event, that one transition) (§5-7) |
| The shell relay rejects frame decoding (unknown tag · short frame — a bug·version mismatch) | unknown tag = the header fields (agent · incarnation marker · seq) can be read so we make the same placeholder `Error` as the daemon sink at that seq and flush it — one error row in that agent's chat view · other agents·connections as they were · short frame = we do not know seq so we cut the connection → backoff reconnect → full replay (if deterministic the cut repeats — accepted, §5-7) — either way it is not silently discarded so the view does not stop up to the hold ceiling · if the window Channel registry lock is poisoned, all three places that hold that lock (frame pass-through · window registration · popout cleanup) take it back (§5-7) |
| The hold exceeds the ~~view buffer ceiling~~ hold ceiling (★implementation reflected — 2048 frames · 1 MiB★) (not on the normal path) | return to the buffer phase and request a full replay again (`startBuffering`) — if the hole place was pushed off the ring, skip at the replay head (truncation) (§5-7) |
| ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — residues the review left (accepted)**★ | The lines below were found and accepted by the implementation reviews (P1 · P3 · P5 · P2 deep) — they are also in the final report |
| A user message stands as `Queued` right after the kernel releases mail (claude — the kernel's check-then-write gap) | that mail can reach stdin before the user item — a kind that existed before this feature (P3 review) |
| WS cancel waits for `input_order` (claude) | the WS cancel branch runs on an asynchronous worker without `spawn_blocking` — one tokio worker waits briefly on that lock (the work inside the lock is only classification · emit · queue insertion — bounded · P3 review accepted) |
| claude keeps carrying the capability and changes only the key of the lifecycle line (vendor drift) | the 「알아볼 수 있는 줄」 door (§5-4 implementation reflected) filters only drift that comes with an init without the capability — if it carries the capability and changes only the key, the row never closes and mail is blocked without a ceiling (N8 — visible in the list · removed by ✕) · a CLI that emits neither init nor lifecycle has no alternative path in `Unknown` (M4 — what edition 8 accepted) |
| codex sends an unrecognizable `turn.status` (`inProgress` · a new value · none) | `TurnClose::Unknown` — the disposition is like `completed` · the halt as it is · no debt (§5-5 implementation reflected) |
| Behavior of the vendor for an automatic empty `turn/start` (follow-up turn debt) (codex) | measured only on 0.156.1 (M9) — the floor 0.140 and the versions between are not measured (P2 review A1) · the frontend's drawing when an empty turn lets an answer through with no user bubble · 「Wait」 is the share of P2 GUI measurement |
| The `TurnEnd` of the previous turn reaches after the next Direct bubble (codex — concurrency) | 「Wait」 is hidden until the first output (appearance only · P2 review concurrency F1) · the same kind: if a Direct goes out after a late receipt read Idle and before emit, the display-only boundary stands right after that Direct bubble |
| Settlement is open and a turn is about to open again (codex — none per the source) | `debug_assert!(settling.is_none())` — a reader thread panic only in debug builds (the concurrency lens confirmed it is unreachable) |
| codex changes to no longer echo `userMessage` back (vendor drift — codex JSON session id) | the first-turn port is not called and the id is **silently** not persisted (only the latch's 「Held」 info log — §5-8 implementation reflected) · if the commit beyond the port panics on the reader thread, the pump `Failed` |
| The first input of a resuming claude is Queued · a resumed agent of codex below the floor | the loading sheet remains until `Delivered` (claude) · the vendor echo (codex) (§5-7 gate implementation reflected · P2 review A2) |
