## 6. PTY isolation — what does not change and its proof

- **Files that do not change (diff must be 0):** `crates/engram-dashboard-agent/src/transport/pty.rs` · `transport/stdio.rs` · `transport/input_queue.rs` · `src/components/slot/TerminalSlot.tsx` · `src/lab/terminal/`. As a review check item, `git diff --stat master... -- <위 경로>` = empty output.
- **Branch structure:** the new branch of the session write path splits **at the very front by the mid-turn input policy the backend reported** (§5-0 — not by the encoder tag). Terminal is `MidTurnPolicy::None` so it is today's code path as it is (byte-identical), and it looks at neither turn observation, nor receipt-possibility, nor the input lock. The source (`InputOrigin`) is carried only as an argument and the `None` path does not read it — both human input and mail are the same bytes as today.
- **Path verbs:** the default implementation of `send_turn` is today's `send_input` and the default implementation of `withdraw` is `NotHeld` (§5-5) — the PTY·stdio paths implement neither (the files above have diff 0). The session calls the two verbs only when `TransportOwned` so in terminal they are not even called. A cancel command that came to a terminal agent finds the registry empty and is `NOT_FOUND` before reaching the path (§5-6).
- **Ring events:** the new `OutputEvent` variants are not made by the PTY pump (`pty.rs` makes only `TerminalBytes`). Since the registry is always empty, the core's `finish` synthesis · seal · rewriting after the cannot-receive decision also emit nothing (§5-3).
- **Mail — today's as it is (PRD §3-9 · AC22 terminal part):** since the registry never fills, 「찼다」 of that incarnation is not written to the waiting list table (always false — we read an absent entry in the table as false) and the doorbell at the moment the registry empties does not ring either (§5-0). So the kernel's busy decision `in_turn ∨ inputs_pending` is the same value as today's `in_turn` for a terminal incarnation, and mail goes in at the same time and in the same shape as today. The codex path's Idle order (user first) is a rule inside the JSON path so it does not reach the terminal.
- **Frontend:** `QueuedInputList` stands only on `RichSlot` — `TerminalSlot` is diff 0 (above). ★The seq continuity rule of `protocolClient.ts` (§5-7) also passes the terminal view★ — a terminal session has the pump as its only output producer so no hole arises, and when there is no hole it flushes at once so bytes·timing are the same (§7-1 frontend test). The daemon sink's placeholder arises only on the bug path and is tag1 so the terminal view does not draw it (only seq advances — `TerminalSlot.tsx:261`).
- **Regression (already existing — must be green):** `session::tests::write_input_raw_is_byte_identical` · `write_input_terminal_mode_does_not_emit_user_echo` · `submit_input_terminal_writes_the_body_then_the_submit_byte_separately` · `backend::tests::raw_encoder_is_byte_identical` · `input_echo_event_raw_is_none` · `raw_submission_is_cr_under_default_xterm_encoding` · claude `terminal_mode_spec_unchanged_regression` · codex `terminal_bytes_carry_no_turn_signal` · agent integration `tests/session_smoke.rs`·`transport_smoke.rs` (real PTY).
- **One existing test that changes:** codex `neither_mode_makes_a_synthetic_input_echo` (`backend/codex/mod.rs:1972`) — the very test ADR-0198 wrote down as 「구현하면 지워지는 것이 정상」. **Keep the terminal half** and rename it to `the_terminal_mode_makes_no_synthetic_input_echo` and delete only the JSON half.
- **New regressions:**
  - `a_policy_none_session_never_emits_queued_input_even_while_the_core_says_in_turn` — even if the core answers that a turn is running, a session of policy `None` (terminal) emits not one `QueuedInput` and the bytes are the same (both human input · mail).
  - `a_policy_none_agent_never_reports_inputs_pending` — a terminal incarnation's turn fact goes out only as `inputs_pending = false` and the busy decision stands on turn observation alone (mail timing is the same as today — PRD AC22).
- **GUI:** typing mid-turn in terminal claude·codex slots → only that TUI's own waiting indicator appears and there is no list (§7-2 run 12 · PRD AC21).

## 7. Test plan (ADR-0012)

### 7-1. By seam

| Seam / harness | What it proves |
|---|---|
| **Reducer (pure — `queued_input.rs`)** | All of the §5-2 state-based table: `Queued`+`Dropped` → Discarded (cause preserved) · `Queued`+`Withdrawn` → Cancelled · a second `Queued` of the same id → no-op (no re-emission) · `Cancelling`+`CancelRequested` (same id) → no-op · `Cancelling`+`Dropped{AgentEnded\|Rejected}` → Discarded (outcome (c)) · `Cancelling`+`Delivered` → Delivered (outcome (b) — including after the response `false`) · ★**cause attribution**: `Dropped{Unknown}`·`CancelAnswered{true}` in both orders → Cancelled · even if `CancelAnswered{true}` comes with no lifecycle line → Cancelled at once (the following `Dropped{Unknown}`·`Delivered` are discarded by the tombstone — no revival) · `CancelAnswered{false}`·`CancelFailed` after `Dropped{Unknown}` → Discarded{Unknown} · `Dropped{Unknown}` after `CancelAnswered{false}` → Discarded{Unknown}★ · ★**a failed cancel request is not reverted**: `CancelFailed` with no response → cancel-pending as it is (could not remove — it ### 7-2. What needs a real agent — GUI measurement (`/qa full`, isolated instance)

1. claude JSON: input during a long tool (`sleep 20` shell) → list (one gray line) → **at the end of the tool** it leaves the list and becomes an end-of-conversation bubble → the model reacts to that message. There is no bubble at the sent place.
2. claude JSON: ✕ in the same situation → it disappears at once in every window and does not reappear · it does not go to the model (the next response does not know that message) · the input box is empty · meanwhile in the LLM list (the road of run 14) it was `cancelling` until the outcome and then leaves.
3. claude JSON: ✕ loses the race (right after the tool end — after the fold filter) → the message that had disappeared at once appears as an end-of-conversation bubble and the answer comes (no notification). ★If reproduction is unstable, substitute the fixture test (§7-1 claude decoder·session) and the GUI only observes★.
4. claude JSON: input during a long answer with no tools → automatically sent as the next turn at turn end → the answer comes.
5. codex JSON: input during a long tool → list (✕ present) → at the tool end it leaves the list and becomes a bubble → the model reacts at that tool end (not pushed to the next boundary — AC4) · ✕ while a tool runs → disappears at once and does not go to the model (`AtEarliestBoundary` — P8) · ★✕ on an already handed-over item (input mid-tool of the `Immediate` landing (P2) · the segment that hands over at once) → it disappears from the list at once, and if that message is received it appears as an end-of-conversation bubble (outcome (b) — no notification)★ · input during a long answer with no tools → the answer comes in the same turn's follow-up or the next turn (✕ withdraws the message only when M16 is green).
6. codex JSON resume: input during hydration → list (no header line · ✕ — it is a held item so it withdraws the message, from P2) → the bubble is **below** the restored history · ✕ during hydration → disappears at once in every window and does not go to the model.
7. codex: interrupt while there are waiting items — ★the chat view has no operation, so send the wire `Interrupt` with CDP `invoke`★ → the item disappears from the list, **does not return** to the input box, and is not sent again.
8. At agent end (kill), the waiting items (including those awaiting the outcome of ✕ — already gone from the window and leaving the LLM list) → they disappear from the list · no return to the input box · no notification — both backends.
9. Sending while idle → no list flicker — both backends. Sending while the list remains (no turn) → end of the list, bubbles in the order typed — the means of reproduction = the gap after codex −32600 (a claude mid-turn slash command is not a means — M13 green: the receipt comes and it leaves the list the moment the running turn ends · [Phase 0b report](../../research/mid-turn-phase0b-measurements-2026-09-26.md) §1). If it cannot be reproduced, substitute the §7-1 session·path tests.
10. Several in a row → older on top · from the 4th, 「외 N개」 (while collapsed the three oldest · press to expand · full text in the tooltip for truncated items) · each a separate bubble · in codex the several left at turn end go together into one next turn (AC27).
11. There is no header line above the list — whether a tool is running · during an answer with no tools · while codex is connecting · no turn + list present, only gray items are visible at any time (AC8).
12. **Typing mid-turn in terminal claude·codex slots → no change** (§6).
13. A popout window + the main window with the same agent → the lists of both windows are the same, and on ✕ in one it disappears together from both windows at once (cancel-pending is in no window) · closing and reopening a window · shell restart (daemon·agents kept) → the list as it was · an item that was being cancelled appears in no window and the outcome is reflected · cutting and reattaching the window-daemon connection → no item disappears, and what was received in the meantime is a bubble.
14. LLM: `engram call` (`/control/call` road — §5-6. The new verbs are not in the `engram agent` verb list) `agent.listQueuedInputs` → with the received `id` `agent.cancelQueuedInput` → the screen changes the same.
15. In three-theme (dark · light · e-ink) screenshots the gray text is readable · it does not cover the name tag.
16. Arrival of mail between agents — **both backends · including when the receiver is mid-turn** → it does not go on the list and the shape is the same as today · if the receiver's user list has items, it goes in after the turn those items went into ends (AC22) · terminal slots at today's timing.

**Measurements (P7 — not GUI measurement · the result goes in the `docs/research/` report · definition = §3-3):**

17. **M15 — our daemon's reaction delay.** Measure on a real codex in an isolated instance with the trace timestamps of the P2 landing. In `Immediate` there is no steer released by the tool-end signal, so measure in two legs (definition = §3-3): ① the moment the path reader receives the current turn's tool `item/completed` (the running tool set becoming empty) → the moment the writer wakes — N times, with a command that pours output until it ends (a build·test that prints thousands of lines) · including parallel tools ② the moment the writer wakes → the moment the steer write ends — in the hop 「`turn/start` 응답(턴 id) → 쥔 항목 steer」 (make it with several left at the turn end · a message typed before the turn id — also record that whole hop) and in the hop where `Immediate` steers at once a message typed while a running command pours output (lock contention under a delta flood). For parallel tools also record the window from the last completion to the vendor check (the echo of an item handed over mid-tool marks the check). ① is also measured N times at the end-of-answer signal (`agentMessage`·`plan` `item/completed` when no tool is running), and for both signals the **reader lag** (the time the reader processed earlier lines without rest until just before that completion line) is written down together. Tool-end verdict = green if **tool-end reaction delay (tool-end ① maximum + the reader-lag maximum at that time + ② maximum) < 6 ms** (the minimum of the M7 window) · ★if red, stop before writing P8 and ask the user (PRD R8)★ ★edition 9 — M15 green → P8 landed · this stop condition did not arise (§9 P7)★. The **end-of-answer reaction delay** (end-of-answer ① maximum + the reader-lag maximum at that time + ② maximum) is not a verdict but a value for M16 to compare against (run 18).
18. **M16 — the end boundary of an answer with no tools.** Harness (Phase 0 M7 method · no code) — does a steer handed over at the moment of receiving `item/completed` of `agentMessage` (and `plan` too if reproduced) when no tool is running get into the same turn's follow-up sampling · N times, number of hits and window length (both a turn with no tools and the last answer after tools) · compare the window with M15's **end-of-answer reaction delay** (run 17 — it is not the tool-end reaction delay). Green = hold the answer segment too · red = hand over the answer segment at once — either way we do not stop.

## 8. Impact

- **ADRs to be amended (the amendment stamp goes on both sides — `/adr`):**
  - **ADR-0044** — the input-time synthetic echo is **only while idle** (PRD §3-5). Mid-turn (mechanism A) is waiting list → a bubble at the delivery place.
  - **ADR-0045** — the render model 「순서 보존 item 스트림」 gains the waiting list (state outside the conversation) · uuid dedup narrows to 「대기 중이 아닌 uuid 의 첫 항목」.
  - **ADR-0190** — we narrow 「json 모드 입력은 큐에 담고 **보낼 수 있을 때** 흘린다」 **for user input only**: mid-turn user input is held not until the turn end but only until **the earliest boundary the hand-over policy answers** (`HandOverPolicy` inside the codex backend — `Immediate` skeleton → measurement → `AtEarliestBoundary` · hand-over = `turn/steer` if a turn is in progress — §5-5). At Idle a user item opens the turn before mail · after an oddly ended turn (`failed` · the five exits of `turn/start`) the halt after error (`halted`) holds the remaining user items·empty turn·mail (what is already in the path — new mail is the kernel's `last_end_failed`, the ADR-0110 item) until the user's next turn succeeds. The queue remains for connecting·FIFO·end-of-turn settlement·halt after error·mail. It also applies to that ADR's re-discussion trigger ② 「큐 조작을 실제로 붙일 때」 (✕ = path verb `withdraw`).
  - **ADR-0198** — amended while implementing: the synthetic echo is only while idle (mid-turn is `Queued`) · the synthetic echo is emitted with no turn observation (§5-5) · if that bubble is rejected (`Dropped{Rejected}`) it is erased without a notification (PRD §3-5 — §5-2) · the merge key `clientUserMessageId`↔`clientId` is as it is and is also carried in `turn/steer` · closes the open question of hydration order in 「영향」 (waiting → delivery after history) · the 「보낸 자리에 정식 말풍선」 of the original proposal goes to rejected alternatives (§10-5).
  - **ADR-0193** — the principle (「보낼 수 있나」 is by the path's own state) is kept · the codex path steers during Active **as the hand-over policy answers** — the path decides the segment (cannot hand over · boundary open · tool · answer) by its own state (§5-5 segment table · the request that opens a turn is still one — `take_turn_locked`) · it states explicitly that claude turn observation is used **only for display classification** (not release material) · the codex turn end is **one settlement per turn** (collect only responses — there is no end-of-turn probe) and no turn is opened during settlement · settlement closes within one deadline measured from the turn end (the place that releases an early exit is also a turn end) · the order of opening a turn at Idle = **user first** — user `Held` → the follow-up turn of a user message that got no answer (empty input `turn/start`) → mail, and a mail turn does not release the follow-up turn debt (PRD §3-9 · §3-1) · ✕ is taken by the path verb `withdraw` — a held item is erased (no vendor call) and an already handed-over item is only removed from the list (both policies · the 「넘길 수 없음」 segments of the hand-over policy (connecting · waiting for the turn id · settlement · halt after error · a turn where steer was rejected) are held by both policies) · after an oddly ended turn (`failed` · the five exits of `turn/start` — regardless of turn kind) the halt after error (`halted` — a path field, outside `TurnState` · it blocks only turns the path opens by itself — mail's door is the kernel fact in the ADR-0110 item below) stands — it sets no debt and Idle opens no turn on its own (remaining items · empty turn · mail already in the path), and only one turn opens, the one opened by a user message that arrived after the halt and is still held (decided by state · the remaining first in one turn — PRD §3-10 · AC24) · it is released when that turn ends with `completed` (PRD AC29) · the follow-up turn debt is released the moment a user·empty `turn/start` is issued (even if that turn ends oddly it is not issued again — the halt takes over) · the turn id is taken only from the `turn/start` response (the path's existing rule as it is).
  - **ADR-0006** — one line `input_order → replay → queued_inputs` in the lock order · ★the lock of the waiting list table (mail fact 「사용자 목록이 비지 않았다」) is also a leaf taken **inside** the replay lock (`replay → 대기 목록 표` — it calls nothing outside)★ · the writing order of that table is written together: 「찼다」 inside the replay lock that reduced it · 「비었다」 and the doorbell after that emit's turn observation update · the table drops a write with a smaller seq · the adapter and the claude session classification read the list table → turn observation table in that order (§5-0 「적는 순서」 · §5-4). ★One exception★: since the session emits while holding `input_order`, the subscriber fan-out runs inside that lock — an exception to 「lock 미보유 send」, and the ground for no deadlock is the contract that **`OutputSink::send` does not block** (operating sink = `try_send` — daemon `agent_conn.rs` `FrameSink::try_send`). We write that contract **as an explicit contract** in the `OutputSink` trait doc (`types.rs` `trait OutputSink`) (「가득 차면 기다리지 말고 `SinkError`」 — landed). The output pump does not take `input_order` (§5-0 — ground = so that output does not stand behind the disk I/O of the latch commit that lock contains). The ADR-0226 latch commit (disk write) of the first submit is also inside that lock — it is once per incarnation so harmless. ★The main rule of 「lock 미보유 send」 is as it is★ — the live delivery reversal that is its cost is matched by the client through seq continuity (§5-7 · user decision N5 (a)). The alternative that changes this rule was rejected by the user (§10-5). ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — what to write in ADR-0006 grows**: ① the lock order is four — `input_order → replay → 명부 → 대기 목록 표(잎)` (§5-0 · §5-3) ② the codex path's emission line `Announcer.order → {상태 락, replay → 명부 → 대기 목록 표}` — announce does `core.emit` holding `order` so the fan-out runs inside that line (the **second exception** leaning on the same `OutputSink::send` non-blocking contract · P2 review doc-aware) ③ registry lock poison = `into_inner` (differently from the replay lock's `expect`)★.
  - **ADR-0226** — D5 loading end condition 「사용자 입력」 → 「전달된 입력의 말풍선」 · adds the id persistence of a first input that was cancelled·discarded after waiting to the §6-3 kind. ★**Implementation reflected (2026-09-26) — the amendment width grew (user decision §10-1 24)**: for codex JSON the id persistence time itself changes to **the peer's first user-message echo-back** (decisions 2 · 11 · 「영향/불변식」 — since it is a reversal, write it as a new ADR number and stamp ADR-0226 · `/adr`) · 「대기 후 취소·버려진 첫 입력의 id 영속」 is closed for codex JSON (there is no persistence before the first echo-back — §5-8)★.
  - **ADR-0127/0113 related (not an amendment)** — among the new events the turn signal is **only claude `Delivered`** (a translation of the vendor `started` — progress) and the rest and all of codex are not (§5-2 · §5-4) · the Queued branch (the input path) does not write to the turn table · the path-synthesized user bubble is emitted without observation (§5-5) · events the core rewrote (the seal · `Queued` after the determination) do not pass through the classifier (§5-3) — the classifier·core tests keep it. ★One change in mail timing★: in claude the mail busy decision sees the drain turn not from the first output but from `started` as 「바쁨」 — the gap in which mail was folded into the user's drain turn **narrows** to `result`→`started` (≈1.5 ms — M7), and even that ≈1.5 ms is busy because the user list is not empty (mail's idle — the messaging item below · PRD §3-9 · AC22).
  - **ADR-0127 — turn observation table (amendment — one signal · end kind · one fact):** the two classifiers produce an error end — claude reads the `Error` of a failed `result` as 「턴 오류」 `TurnSignal` (a new variant in `turn.rs:49` — `in_turn` unchanged), and codex reads a `TurnEnd{Failed}` attributed to our turn (the translated `turn/completed(failed)` · the `end_turn_if` of the five exits (`transport.rs:1228-1262`) — the five callers `:1278`·`:1701`·`:2111`·`:2180`·`:2191`) as an error end (`Ended` carries the kind of end). The turn observation table folds it into `last_end_failed` at each turn end — error end = true · cleanly successful end = false · interrupted end = as it is (§5-0 「오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편」). The writing side is still one classifier per agent and the cleanup points are still two, `finish` · the finalize recheck of `emit` (both `forget`) — 「세 번째 호출자」 does not arise. Release is the next clean turn end so there is no new doorbell (the turn end rings `turn_ended` after the table update).
  - **ADR-0110 — the messaging kernel's busy decision (amendment — the fact the port gives grows):** to the fact the turn observation port returns (`TurnFact`) we add **`inputs_pending`** (that incarnation's user list is not empty) and **`last_end_failed`** (after a turn that ended in error there has not yet been a cleanly successful end — both backends, the ADR-0127 item above · the door of mail on both backends is this one fact), and the decision `busy = in_turn ∨ inputs_pending ∨ last_end_failed` is made by the kernel's `BusyPolicy` (the adapter is thin — ADR-0110 · 「사실은 공용 · 정책은 소비자」 — the shape of ADR-0113 as it is). The source of `inputs_pending` is the waiting list table, **separate from** the turn observation table — we do not touch ADR-0127's turn observation table and its write points (「세 번째 호출자」) · `last_end_failed` is a field of that table but its writing side is the classifier as before (the ADR-0127 item above). When the registry empties, the flush doorbell rings by the same road as a turn end (`StatusSink` verb-addition candidate — §5-0). A terminal incarnation has an always-empty list so it is the same as today's decision (§6).
  - **ADR-0104 — an intended exception (user decision N8):** `inputs_pending` has no fail-open ceiling — while a user item whose receipt never comes remains, mail also cannot go in (when the agent ends the list empties — PRD §3-9 · R7). It is the opposite direction from ADR-0113 「안 가는 것 > 늦게 가는 것」 and the 30-minute turn observation ceiling (`BUSY_MAX_TURN` — ADR-0127 decision 4), and the user chose it knowing that: 「사용자 글이 막히면 우편도 막혀야 하는 거 아냐? 애초에 글이 막히는 것 자체가 이상한 건데」 — a blocked item is an anomaly (a missed signal), and an intended long wait (the halt after error) also blocks mail. ★The exception widens (user decision N11 (b))★ — the halt after error (`last_end_failed` — both backends block mail with the same fact) also has no ceiling: mail waits until the user types something and the screen has no reason for it (an accepted cost · a mail notification is backlog outside this feature · the alternative of continuing on its own at a limit-release signal was rejected — §10-5). The turn observation 30-minute ceiling is as it was. ★`delivered` = the actual injection time — the halt after error does not break it (the path's brief hold is today's grade · remaining gap §5-9)★ — during the halt mail stays in the kernel (the design before round 6 of the edition 8 review that made the codex path hold it broke that without declaring it — §10-5) · remaining gap = codex mail already in the path right before the failure (§5-9).
  - **ADR-0004 related (not an amendment)** — the backend reports the mid-turn input policy·receipt-possibility and the session splits only by those values (§5-0) · the hand-over policy `HandOverPolicy` is kept inside the codex backend and the session·manager see only `TransportOwned` (§5-5) — it follows that ADR's shape.

- **CLAUDE.md core invariants:** in the ownership split 「session += 대기 입력 명부 · 입력 자물쇠(코어는 emit 에서 먹이고, 종료 합성과 봉인을 한 덩이로 하고, 판정 뒤 `Queued` 를 바꿔 적고, 판명에 본문 사본을 채운다)」 · in 「seq dedup」 of the frontend integration micro-rules 「seq 연속 — live 와 버퍼→live flush 모두 `마지막+1` 만 배달하고 구멍 뒤는 붙든다(시한 없음 · 넘치면 전량 replay)」 (§5-7) · in the 「replay→live」 invariant 「sink 와 셸 중계는 seq 를 건너뛰지 않는다 — sink 가 못 실은 사건은 그 seq 의 자리채움 `Error` · 셸이 모르는 tag 의 프레임은 그 seq 의 같은 자리채움 · seq 를 못 읽는 짧은 프레임은 연결을 끊는다(전량 replay)」 · in the lock order 「`input_order → replay → queued_inputs`(잎) · `replay → 대기 목록 표`(잎 — 「비었다」 is after turn observation) — the output pump does not take `input_order` · the fan-out inside that lock leans on the `OutputSink::send` non-blocking contract」 · a new line 「대기 입력 상태 = 링 사건 한 줄기, 명부와 누산기가 같은 환원 규칙 — 턴 신호는 claude `Delivered`(벤더 `started`) 하나뿐 · 통로가 합성한 사용자 말풍선은 턴 관측 없이」 · in the 「턴 관측 정리」 line 「턴 관측 표의 `last_end_failed`(오류 뒤 멈춤 — 우편 · 두 백엔드) — 쓰는 쪽은 그 에이전트의 턴 분류기 하나(턴 끝에 접는다) · 정리는 같은 두 지점」. ★**Implementation reflected — we rewrite the list above in the landed shape (P6 · outside this document)**: ownership split = **core** holds the registry · the waiting list table `Arc` and **session** holds `input_order` · `mid_turn` · `delivery_ack`, and the registry is reached through the core (§5-3) · lock order four (`… → 명부 → 대기 목록 표`) + codex `order → 상태` · the halt field has its own cursor (§5-0) · `finish` does not ring the doorbell · in the integration micro-rules the hold ceiling (2048 · 1 MiB) · re-attach reconciliation (`'live'` → list query → apply after S → reconciliation generation · an open row not in the snapshot = tombstone) · replay after the output Channel registration · the overstated ground wording of `delete channel.onmessage` (a no-op on a real Channel — §5-7) · in the 「세션 id 영속」 invariant the codex JSON exception (the path calls the latch's first-turn port at the first echo-back — persistence after the first turn · no persistence if it dies before the echo-back · §5-8)★.
- **wire — ★`PROTOCOL_VERSION` 5 → 6★:**
  - What is added: `StructuredEvent::QueuedInput` (there is neither a waiting-reason field nor a ✕-flag field · `AckUnavailable` carries the body copy of the items it closes by receipt — §5-2) · `AgentCommand::{CancelQueuedInput, ListQueuedInputs}` + response (the list response carries the snapshot seq `as_of_seq` and the incarnation marker, and the row `state` is the three `queued`·`unconfirmed`·`cancelling` — §5-6 · §5-7) · regenerating the ts-rs bindings.
  - What only changes value: SubscribeAck's `replay_from`, which was one too large for an empty ring, is corrected to the next issue seq (§5-7) — the field·shape are as they are and there is no operating consumer that reads that value today, so it is not a reason to bump.
  - ★**The halt after error · withdrawing the end-of-turn probe do not increase the wire vocabulary**★ — the halt is a fact inside the daemon (turn observation table · kernel decision · codex path field) and the screen does not draw its reason (the accepted cost). What reaches the wire is only one boolean of the LLM list response (`stopped_after_error` — §5-6) and it rides on the same bump as P4's new commands · the probe was a request made to the vendor. The reason for the bump is still the one command axis below.
  - **The command axis forces the bump.** Adding variants to `AgentCommand` is the same **non-tolerant** axis as v3 (`protocol/src/lib.rs:56-60` — `SetEnvelopeFormat` precedent): an old daemon cannot decode an unknown variant and goes to the parse-failure branch of `agent_conn.rs:270-277`, where it answers with **`Error{request_id: None}`**. There is no correlation key so the new shell's waiting request cannot find its pair and **waits until the deadline** — exactly the silent malfunction v3 wrote as 「신클라가 Ack 를 기다리며 무한 대기」 (looking only at the event axis would miss it — §10-5).
  - Event axis (reference — this alone would not have raised it): new daemon + old shell = an unknown event marker stays on screen (loud) · old daemon + new shell = the waiting events just do not come and it is today's behavior (quiet but harmless — the same as the `TurnEnd` precedent).
  - ★**Sink placeholder (§5-7) — not a new variant**★: it carries the existing tag1 `StructuredEvent::Error{message}` at that seq. One meaning is added — that line may be 「이 seq 의 사건을 싣지 못했다」 (told apart only by the wording). An old shell also draws it as an error row and advances seq as today, so it is not a reason to bump. It arises only on the bug path.
  - What the bump makes different: discovery's `check_acceptable` **does not reuse a live daemon** whose pair does not match (the place that measures = `version_mismatch_live_daemon_errors_without_spawn`). ## 9. Implementation order — the build stands wherever we stop · no step looks worse than today

We **first lay down the new types·verbs with nobody calling them** and wire them up afterward. ★**Precondition for starting = `/review trd` of this document**★ — the user entrusted the TRD finalization and entry into implementation to the orchestrator on 2026-09-26, and the user sees the final report (user: 「나 자러갈테니깐 쭉쭉 진행해. 충분히 얘기한것같으니 구현 너가 알아서하고 후에 최종 보고하는걸로」 · §10-1 23 — this delegation fills the user confirmation of CLAUDE.md 「개발 스텝」). All the questions of PRD §9 have received answers — the pieces where those answers land are collected below the table.

★**Landing order = P0 → P1 → P4 → P5 → P3 → P2 → P7 → P8 → P6**★ — the common layer and the claude path come first, and codex goes in the order **skeleton that hands over at once (including ✕ · `withdraw`) → measurement → holding policy**. The P numbers in the table are names and not order (other sections refer to them by number). ★Edition 9 — the actual landing (only P7's M16 was moved earlier): master merge `6787c83` → M16 = before implementation start `6290c5b` (green) → P0·P1 `fcba626` → P4 `96ead26` → P5 `af9ebe3` → P3 `b40e187` → QA fix `160ba1e` → P2 `3014247` (coder pieces P2a–P2g · including the codex session id persistence amendment — §5-8) → M15 = after P2 (report `fbea43f` · green — §3-3) → P8 `37ecc5e` (review deep PASS · CI green) → P6 (including this edition 9). Each step = WIP commit per piece → `/review code deep` → `/qa` → squash into one step★. Reasons:
- **The screen (P5) comes before the producers (P3·P2)** — if a producer stands first, mid-turn input emits only `Queued` with no synthetic echo and there is no list to draw it, so **nothing shows on screen until it is received** — worse than today (a bubble at once at the sent place). If the screen stands first, it draws nothing while there is no producer so it is the same as today, and the moment a producer stands the list comes up with it.
- **For codex, the skeleton (P2) of the `Immediate` policy comes first** — mid-turn input stands in the list and is handed over at once by steer, so it does not look worse than today (held until the turn end · no ✕), and it makes a place to measure the premise of the holding policy (M15) on the **real path route** (segment tracking and writer waking are already in the skeleton — they just are not used in the hand-over decision · measured in two legs, §3-3). ★The three branches of ✕ and `withdraw` are in the skeleton★ — they use neither a vendor call nor a boundary time so they are independent of measurement, and without them a list item has no ✕ so an item waiting during a halt after error stands gray and even blocks mail (worse than today — PRD AC10 · AC18 · AC24 · AC28). The ✕ of an item handed over at once in the skeleton only removes it from the list (outcome (b)). Only the holding in tool·answer segments (P8) lands after measurement (P7). ★Edition 9 — landed: the operating default = the holding policy (§9 P8 row)★
- **The halt after error lands at the step of its path** — the kernel side (the 「턴 오류」 variant of the turn signal and the end kind of `Ended` · folding of the turn observation table · `TurnFact.last_end_failed` · the third term of `BusyPolicy`) is laid by P1 together with `inputs_pending`, changing the port and the decision at once (nobody emits an error end so it is always false — behavior unchanged), and the classifier branch that emits the error end is placed per path — the claude classifier in **P3**, the codex classifier (the attributed `TurnEnd{Failed}`) in **P2** together with the path field `halted`. Why P3: mail halting after an error is a felt change so it has to be in the same step as the producer (the classifier) (the step invariant below), that classifier (`backend/claude/mod.rs`) is a P3 file, and that change is one body with claude's error-turn handling (PRD §3-10 · AC25 · AC29) — if turned on in P1, only claude's mail would change before the claude path stands. It reads only the `Error` that today's decoder already emits so it does not wait for other pieces. Why P2 is the same — codex's mail halt and automatic-turn halt stand in one step and that classifier (`backend/codex/mod.rs`) is a P2 file (it reads only the `TurnEnd{Failed}` that already occurs today · the mail of codex below the floor also halts from this step).

★**Stop conditions**★
- ★**Both of Phase 0b's stop conditions were lifted**★ — M9·M10 (before P2) and M13 (before P3) are all green (2026-09-26 · [Phase 0b report](../../research/mid-turn-phase0b-measurements-2026-09-26.md) · §3-3). The remaining stop condition is the one below, M15 (M11 · M12 · M14 · M17 do not block).
- **P7 — ★result (edition 9): M15 green · M16 green → P8 landed** (numbers = the §3-3 M15 · M16 rows) — the operating default policy = `HandOverPolicy::AtEarliestBoundary` · it holds the answer segment too (`ANSWER_SEGMENT_HOLDS = true`) · `Immediate` is left as a seam (`with_hand_over`) and a place to go back to (constant `HAND_OVER_POLICY`)★. What follows is the record of the stop condition that did not arise: ~~if M15 cannot hit the tool-segment window (§3-3 — tool-end reaction delay = the sum of the tool-end ① · reader lag · ② maxima ≥ 6 ms), stop before writing P8 and ask the user (holding versus another path — PRD R8). The pieces landed so far stand as they are. All that is lost is the holding of tool·answer segments — codex hands over at once in those segments so the ✕ of a message typed there cannot withdraw it and only removes it from the list (outcome (b)). ✕ itself, and the ✕ that withdraws items held while hand-over is impossible (resuming · waiting for the turn id · halt after error · settlement · a turn where steer was rejected), already stood in P2 so AC10 · AC18 · AC24 · AC28 are as they were. If M16 is red we do not stop — we put the 「답 구간」 field of the segment table as 「곧바로」 and proceed with P8 (a branch PRD §3-1 already decided).~~ ★Edition 9 — M15 · M16 were both green so neither branch arose★. ★**Orchestrator plan (plan §6 §4.9)**★: if red, no P8 and leave it as `Immediate` and give the final report (the ground cited = design stance §10-1 22 · what is lost is only holding) — ★that plan diverged from PRD R8 · PRD §10 · ADR-0231 decision 2 「코드 전에 사용자에게」 (outside the proviso of §10-1 23)★ · it did not arise because M15 was green.

★**Step invariant: a change visible on screen lands in the same step as the producer that makes it.**★ Pieces that stand before the producer (P1·P4·P5) contain only things that **change nothing if there is no producer** — a place to draw new events · an empty list · new commands · an always-false mail fact are like that. A change that would be visible even with no producer moves to that producer's step. Example: the one loading-gate line (removing `!hasSent` from `historyPending`), if it stood first without a producer, after sending a message to a resumed codex agent with no history the loading sheet would not lift at once and would remain until the vendor echo (and in the meantime there is no list either) — so **it goes in P2** (§5-7 gate fix — what needs that gate is only codex connecting items · §10-5). Review check item: does the 「끝났을 때」 field of each P write 「오늘과 같게 보인다」 or only changes the producer of that P made.

N10 (a codex acceptance-unknown item = left in the list until a reason comes) → P2 (acceptance-unknown stage · five exits · settlement deadline · success response with no echo). The principle widened from N11 (b) (halt after error — 2026-09-26) → P1 (kernel `last_end_failed` decision — always false) · P2 (codex `halted` · codex classifier error end) · P3 (claude classifier's 「턴 오류」 signal). N12 (a) (✕-ed acceptance unknown = discard at once) → the `Unconfirmed` branch of P2 `withdraw` · the unknown branch of `cancel_asked` items. N13 (b) (later messages in order as usual) → P2 hand-over rule (the no-overtaking ban is among `Held`).
- **Where to split (by file overlap):** since P1 first brings in the input path skeleton of `session.rs` (source · policy branch · lock · cancel verb signature), the only places P3·P2 overlap in the session are **the bodies of each policy branch** — even so they are the same file, so by default one coder goes P3 → P2 sequentially. P8 is the same codex path file as P2 so it continues in the same stream after P7 (an isolated worktree only prevents conflicts). P4 is independent after P1 (files do not overlap — `manager.rs`·`commands.rs`·daemon; `manager.rs` overlaps P1's source field so it is after P1). P5 relies only on P1's contract so it is developed in parallel with mock data, and lands after P4 and before P3 (the one place of P5, `output_core.rs` — the empty ring `replay_from` — is the same file as P1 but the landing is P1 → P5 so it is sequential). ★P2 overlaps P5 by one `RichSlot.tsx` line★ — the landing order is already P5 → P2 so it is sequential, and that one line goes on top of the file after P5's layout change is finished. P1's `turn.rs` (turn signal variants · folding) and P3's claude classifier are different files — P3 only emits the variant P1 laid. P7's harness measurement (M16) does not overlap code so it can run alongside P3·P2.
- **Interfaces the main session must nail down first:** the `QueuedInputEvent`/`DropCause` shapes (`Queued{id, text}` — no ✕-flag field · `AckUnavailable` (body copy field `DeliveredCopy`) · `CancelAnswered` included · no waiting-reason field) and the wire JSON (`{"type":"QueuedInput","op":{…}}` style) · the `InputOrigin`·`MidTurnPolicy`·`DeliveryAck` shapes · the trait verb signatures (`send_turn` · `withdraw` → `Withdraw::{Withdrawn, TooLate, NotHeld}`) · `HandOverPolicy` and the names of segments·boundary signals · the bus·WS command names·arguments·outcome vocabulary (`requested`·`cancelled`)·verb-own error code (only `NOT_FOUND`) · the `listQueuedInputs` response shape (row `cancel` fields · the three `state` words — including `unconfirmed` · `as_of_seq` · `epoch` — P4 carries them and P5's re-attach reconciliation reads them · `stopped_after_error` — for the LLM, reads the turn table field P1 laid) · the path query `unconfirmed_inputs` signature (P4 lays it and P2 fills in the codex body) · the 「입력 영향」 marker shape · the `caller` argument of `LocalCommands::run`·the common entrance and the lease port signature · the kernel `TurnFact.{inputs_pending, last_end_failed}` and waiting list table signatures · the 「턴 오류」 variant name of `TurnSignal` and the end-kind shape of `Ended` (P1 lays it and the P3 claude · P2 codex classifiers emit it) · the codex settlement deadline constant (`REQUEST_DEADLINE` reuse) · the sink placeholder wording (a fixed string — visible in the chat view) · the bus lease rejection error code · the golden file location and format (including the tombstone ceiling at the head) · the request id rule `cancel:<uuid>` · the names·places of the M15 trace timestamps (two legs · two signals · reader lag) · the session cancel verb signature (P1 lays it, P4 calls it and P3 fills the claude body) · the registry's snapshot seq field (P1 writes it and P4 carries it) · the item classification of the hand-over policy (the decoder's tool vocabulary · output · other — the interface where the path reads the decoder vocabulary) · the `pending` field of a Direct item (`listed`).
